Backed up as `Backups/HenchmanInfoGump.cs.2026-11-25-183514.bak` (original) and `Backups/HenchmanInfoGump.cs.2026-11-25-185313.bak` (tweak). **Follow-up (#228):** the name/title header block is 600px wide (was 400) so a 32-character name plus a 32-character title stays on one line, and the Customize button asks for the henchman's paperdoll (`HenchmanCustomizeGumpBase.ShowPaperdoll`) before the customization gump is sent. Backed up as `Backups/HenchmanInfoGump.cs.2026-11-25-210349.bak`. |
| `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Civilized/Comrades/HenchmanCustomizeGump.cs` | Renamed the name option/header to **Name/Title** and laid out the hue grids. **#226:** the entry hue had been `0x7FFF`, which is an AddHtml colour, not a `hues.mul` text-entry index, so the name/title fields drew **black**; they now use `WhiteTextHue` (`2049`). The 70px/65px columns were still narrower than one swatch (a 22px `0xFA5` button plus the five asterisks), so the grids share an `AddColorSwatch` helper on 92px columns with 24px rows and the panels grew to hair `580×440`, beard `680×440`, skin `600×380`; the two `&amp;` beard styles are a literal `&` again (the client does not decode entities). See #224/#226. Backed up as `Backups/HenchmanCustomizeGump.cs.2026-11-25-185313.bak` (original) and `Backups/HenchmanCustomizeGump.cs.2026-11-25-200834.bak` (this pass). **Follow-up (#227):** the two gender branches now call the new `HenchmanCustomizeGumpBase.ResendBodyToClients()` after the switch (remove packet + fresh `MobileIncoming`), because TazUO/ClassicUO only recompute their cached `Mobile.IsFemale` when they create a mobile — a body-only update turned the world sprite male but left the paperdoll female. Backed up as `Backups/HenchmanCustomizeGump.cs.2026-11-25-204620.bak`. **Follow-up (#228):** `NameTitleMaxLength = 32` replaces the 16-character limit in `Sanitize` and in both text entries (the longest shipped vendor title is 28 characters), the NAME/TITLE panel grew to `480×240` with 360px entries and BACK moved to x=300, the main gump's header block is `340×40`, and the new `ShowPaperdoll` opens/re-opens the paperdoll when customization starts and after a name, gender or skin change. Backed up as `Backups/HenchmanCustomizeGump.cs.2026-11-25-210349.bak`. |
| `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Base/BaseVendor.cs` | Removed the `HenchmanFighterItem`/`HenchmanArcherItem`/`HenchmanWizardItem` × `TavernKeeper`/`Barkeeper` deed-swap branch in `OnDragDrop`; barkeeps no longer replace a henchman deed with a randomized one. See #224. Backed up as `Backups/BaseVendor.cs.2026-11-25-175658.bak`. |
| `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Base/Behavior.cs` | `DoOrderRelease` now closes the appearance-customization gumps (`HenchmanCustomizeGump.CloseFor( m_Mobile.ControlMaster )`) before `SetControlMaster( null )`, so releasing a human henchman dismisses an open customization gump; `OnDelete` remains the fallback for other deletion paths. See #224. Backed up as `Backups/Behavior.cs.2026-11-25-175658.bak`. |
| `Source/Scripts/System/Skills/ArmsLore.cs` | `AvoidDurabilityHit` returns `true` immediately for the four henchman types (`HenchmanFunctions.IgnoresDurability`), so armour, shields and clothing worn by a henchman never wear out — this is the one hook `BaseArmor.OnHit`, `BaseShield.OnHit` and `BaseClothing.OnHit` all consult, which also covers the `CrystalGoliath` / `AbsorbDamageAOS` corrosion path. Player Arms Lore and its “especially durable” message are untouched. See #229. Backed up as `Backups/ArmsLore.cs.2026-11-25-220713.bak`. |


No other files were modified.

---

## Change History (newest last)

1. **Artefact Book picker** — created `ArtefactBook.cs`: a book that opens a paginated gump listing every standard artefact and lets the player claim one. Default `Charges = 1` (negative = unlimited).
2. **Memento Collector + currency** — created `MementoCoin.cs` and `MementoCollector.cs`. The NPC pays coins by artefact tier and the coin is a stackable item.
3. **75 coins → Artefact Book + Talk dialogue** — `MementoCollector.OnDragDrop` now handles `MementoCoin` drops (75+ coins → `ArtefactBook`, extras refunded); added `TalkGumpTitle`/`TalkGumpSubject` and the `Talk.cs` dialogue branch.
4. **Random name** — the NPC now takes a standard `NameList.RandomName(...)` name; title stays `"the artefact collector"`.
5. **Gender / outfit pass** — random male or female (matching body + name); added a lit lantern; replaced robe/shoes with leather armour; added cloak, sash and boots; female bustiers used instead of the leather chest; no arm armour at that stage.
6. **Hue + idle actions + random gear** — shared hue changed to `2141` for the book, coins, cloak, sash and lantern; leather cap removed; leather armour dyed dark gray (`0x83B`); added adventurer-style idle emotes (yawn, sigh, etc.); equipment randomly swapped for standard artefacts on spawn.
7. **Cap/sleeves, wandering, no bustier rule, wieldable artefact** — re-added the leather cap and leather sleeves (`LeatherArms`); removed `CantWalk` so the NPC wanders; removed the female bustier special-case (everyone wears `LeatherChest`); the NPC now wields a random one-handed standard artefact weapon alongside the lantern.
8. **Artefact detail page** — clicking an artefact in the `ArtefactBook` list no longer grants it immediately. It now opens a detail page showing the artefact's icon and full stats/powers (via `ItemProps.ItemProperties`), with Back and Claim buttons; Claim performs the actual grant and charge consumption.
9. **Detail layout + unique powers** — the detail page now places the artefact picture on the left page and all information on the right page, and prepends a "UNIQUE POWER" description for artefacts with special behaviours (e.g., Merlin's Mystical Staff calls a dragon on double-click, Hammer of Thor casts lightning, Rod of Resurrection revives the fallen, etc.).
10. **Detail title placement** — the artefact name is now centred at the top of the left page, directly over the artefact picture (instead of spanning the whole book).
11. **Cloth hat** — the collector now wears a floppy cloth hat dyed the Memento hue (2141) to match the cloak and sash, replacing the dark-gray leather cap.
12. **Random hat type** — the collector now picks any of several cloth hat styles (floppy, wide-brim, cap, skull cap, bandana, straw, tall straw, wizard, feathered, tricorne, pirate, hood, witch, bonnet), all dyed hue 2141.
13. **Vendor team** — the collector is explicitly placed on the neutral team `0` used by town vendors, and re-asserts it in `OnAfterSpawn` so a spawner cannot move it to a different team.
14. **Custom merchant spawns** — added one `MementoCollector` spawner per merchant/wagon stall in `Info/Spawns/Merchant.map` (34 total), so a collector stands beside every custom merchant. Applies on the next `[buildworld`.
15. **Coin singular/plural** — a single `MementoCoin` now displays as "Memento Coin"; stacks of two or more display as "Memento Coins". The internal `Name` stays plural so stacks still merge.
16. **Merchant name colour** — the collector's name now uses the innocent/vendor blue (`Notoriety.GetHue( Notoriety.Innocent )`) instead of the guard white (`1154`), matching the other merchants.
17. **Vendor vulnerability** — removed the transient `Blessed` flag (which `OnThink` was clearing anyway) and turned off `AlwaysAttackable`; `NameHue` is back to the default so notoriety drives the colour. The collector is now vulnerable and Innocent exactly like a regular vendor.
18. **Invulnerable / untargetable** — reverted the `BaseCreature` edit; `MementoCollector` now derives from `BaseNPC` (already in the always-invulnerable set), so its `Blessed` flag persists and it cannot be targeted or harmed. `Deserialize` re-asserts `Blessed`, and `NameHue` keeps the merchant blue.
19. **Tome rename + page number** — the book is now named "Tome of Wondrous Artifacts"; the "STANDARD ARTEFACTS" heading inside the book was removed, and the page indicator is now just the page number at the bottom next to the arrows. The NPC dialogue/messages were updated to say "Tome of Wondrous Artifacts".
20. **Page indicator position** — the page counter is now right-aligned as "Page X of Y" just left of the next-page arrow (bottom right).
21. **Detail Claim button** — on the artefact detail page the Claim action now uses a checkmark-box button (`0x837`/`0x838`) in the arrow's place, with the "Claim" label right-aligned next to it.
22. **Magery-powered magic carpets** — `BaseBoat.ApplySpeedBoost` now selects `SkillName.Magery` instead of `SkillName.Seafaring` when the boat is a magic carpet (`isCarpet( this )`); sailing vessels still use Seafaring. The 50/75/100/125 thresholds still grant 1/2/3/4 boosts and the maximum remains capped at 4 by `MAX_SPEED_BOOSTS`, so carpets can never gain more than 4 bonus speed steps. The Avatar "Fast Seaman" boat-speed upgrade still applies to both vessel types, but the combined result is clamped to 4.
23. **Speed caps 6 / carpet 4 + explicit skill isolation** — `MAX_SPEED_BOOSTS` raised from 4 to 6, so the Avatar "Fast Seaman" upgrade (via `Constants.BOAT_SPEED_MAX_LEVEL`) can now reach 6 on regular boats; added `MAX_CARPET_SPEED_BOOSTS = 4` and a carpet-only clamp in `ApplySpeedBoost`, so magic carpets never exceed 4 bonus speed steps regardless of Magery or Avatar values. Boats are driven only by Seafaring (Magery has no effect) and carpets only by Magery (Seafaring has no effect).
24. **Compile shortcuts (Linux + Windows)** — added `Compile-Linux.sh` and `Compile-Windows.bat` in the `World` folder. Each computes the repository base folder (the parent of `World`), runs `git pull` there, then runs the matching compiler from `Source/Tools` (`compile-world-linux.sh` / `compile-world-win.bat`). If `git pull` fails the shortcut stops before compiling so stale/conflicting code is never built. The Linux shortcut is executable; `git pull` is best run with a clean working tree.
25. **Linux double-click fix (desktop launcher + GUI PATH)** — double-clicking the `.sh` in KDE Dolphin opened it in a text editor, and when run from the GUI `mcs`/`mono`/`csc` were missing from `PATH` (they live in Linuxbrew, added only by `~/.bashrc`). Added `World/Compile-Linux.desktop`: a `Terminal=true` KDE launcher that runs the script in Konsole and keeps the window open for the result (executable + trusted, also registered in the app menu as "Compile World (Linux)"). Hardened `Compile-Linux.sh` to prepend Homebrew/Linuxbrew `bin` and the standard bin dirs to `PATH`, and to pause on exit when launched from the desktop (`COMPILE_SHORTCUT_PAUSE=1`). Verified the full `git pull` → `mcs` compile flow under a stripped GUI-like environment (`env -i`, minimal `PATH`) with exit 0. `Compile-Linux.sh` backed up as `Backups/Compile-Linux.sh.2026-11-07-192511.bak`.
26. **Reverted the `.desktop` launcher (scripts only)** — per request, removed `World/Compile-Linux.desktop` and its `~/.local/share/applications` registration, and restored `Compile-Linux.sh` to the plain git-pull → compile script (dropping the `.desktop`-only `PATH`/pause additions). The shortcuts are again plain scripts: `Compile-Linux.sh` (Linux) and `Compile-Windows.bat` (Windows).
27. **Double-click now runs the `.sh` (KIO execute + Konsole relaunch)** — on KDE Plasma 6 a `.sh` opened in the editor on double-click because KIO's executable-script memo was set to open-in-application. Set `~/.config/kiorc` `[Executable scripts] behaviourOnLaunch=execute` (confirmed from KIO's `WidgetsOpenOrExecuteFileHandler`) so Dolphin executes executable scripts. Because KIO executes without a terminal (and a session `PATH` lacking Linuxbrew), `Compile-Linux.sh` now adds Homebrew/Linuxbrew/standard bin dirs to `PATH` and, when run without a TTY, re-execs itself via `konsole --hold -e bash …` so the output is visible and the window stays open. Verified: the fake-konsole relaunch wiring and a full build under a GUI-like minimal `PATH` (`env -i`, `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin`) compiled cleanly (exit 0). `Compile-Linux.sh` backed up as `Backups/Compile-Linux.sh.2026-11-07-193557.bak`.
28. **Konsole window auto-closes when the build finishes** — dropped `--hold` from the double-click relaunch (`konsole -e bash …`), so the window closes itself at the end instead of staying open. Console runs are unaffected. `Compile-Linux.sh` backed up as `Backups/Compile-Linux.sh.2026-11-07-193951.bak`.
29. **Dungeon Master's Guide fixes** — (a) `Worlds.GetDungeonListing(int)` cases 61/62 were `"Dungeon of the Lich King"`/`"Dungeon of the Mad Archmage"` (no leading "the") while `GetAreaEntrance` matches `"the Dungeon of …"`, so those two book rows resolved to location (0,0) and opened the map at the world origin — added the `the ` prefix in `World.cs`. (b) Added `Worlds.DungeonCount = 85` and switched the gump loop from the hardcoded `85` to it. (c) `OnResponse` now guards the map with `Sextants.HasSextant( from )` and messages the player instead of opening an empty `Sextants.MapGump` when they carry no sextant. Verified: every listing name now has a matching `GetAreaEntrance` zone; both edited files pass `mcs` syntax parsing. Backups: `Backups/World.cs.2026-11-07-222342.bak`, `Backups/DungeonMastersGuide.cs.2026-11-07-222342.bak`.
30. **Knives Chat3 "not functional" — chat button + load robustness** — the client chat button sends packet `0xB5` → core `ChatRequest` → `EventSink.InvokeChatRequest`, but Knives Chat3 had **no `EventSink.ChatRequest` subscriber** (`General.Initialize()` only wired Speech/Login/CharacterCreated), so the chat button did nothing (the in-game book even says "the CHAT button does not work… type `[c` instead"). Added the subscription + `OnChatRequest` → `General.List(m, 0)`. The command entries (`[c`/`[chat` via the Public channel, `[Va`/`[ViewAll`, etc.) were already wired and still work. Also hardened `Channel.Load()` to fall back to `PredefinedChannels()` on any load error, so a stale/corrupt `Channels.bin` can never leave chat with zero channels (which would also make `[c` unregistered). Verified all chat→core symbols exist (no compile issue) and both files pass `mcs` syntax parsing. Backups: `Backups/General.cs.2026-11-07-230824.bak`, `Backups/Channel.cs.2026-11-07-231000.bak`.
31. **Chat button matches `[c`** — the ChatRequest handler opened `General.List(m, 0)` = `ListPage.All` (a "View All" player list), which showed up as an empty Channels gump with no channels and no create option. Changed it to route through the Public channel exactly like `[c` (`Channel.GetByType(typeof(Public)).OnChat(m, "")`), so the button sets the current channel and opens the real channel chat view (falls back to `List(m,0)` only if Public is missing). `General.cs` backed up as `Backups/General.cs.2026-11-07-232515.bak`. Verified via `mcs` syntax parsing.
32. **Hair stylist "hire"/service gump shows no options** — the `CustomHairstylist` barber menu (`HairstylistBuyGump`, opened from the context option) filtered its rows by affordability, so with less than 50 gold it opened with zero buttons. Changed it to always list the applicable services (facial-hair entries still hidden for females) and size the background by the shown count; affordability is enforced on click via the existing "You cannot afford my services for that style." message. Verified via `mcs` syntax parsing. Backed up as `Backups/CustomHairstylist.cs.2026-11-07-235505.bak`.
33. **Yard/lawn tools blocked inside the Ranger Outpost** — house-yard decoration failed for houses inside named regions such as "the Ranger Outpost" because `LawnTarget.ValidatePlacement` re-used the region's `AllowHousing` (which for the outpost requires Camping/Tracking base ≥ 90). Now, if the target is on the player's own house land (`m_House.IsCoOwner(from)`), placement is allowed without the region's housing gate; the ownership check was also switched from `IsOwner` to `IsCoOwner` to match `LawnTools.InHouse`/`InteriorDecorator`. Verified via `mcs` syntax parsing. Backed up as `Backups/LawnTarget.cs.2026-11-08-092723.bak`.
34. **Lawn/yard placement eased (house-relative bounds)** — the frequent "You will have to get closer and place within your land!" came from limiting placement to a 15-tile box around the player's standing spot. Replaced it with `IsWithinHouseLand`: the target must land within the house's footprint (union of `house.Area`) expanded by the same `Remodeling.Front/Back/Left/Right` (15) yard margin. Player position no longer matters beyond being inside/owning the house, while the space restriction around the house is preserved. Verified via `mcs` syntax parsing. Backed up as `Backups/LawnTarget.cs.2026-11-08-094152.bak`.
35. **Yard margin doubled (15 → 30)** — set `Remodeling.Left/Right/Front/Back` from 15 to 30 (the "dimensions of the lawn"), so lawn items can be placed within the house footprint plus a 30-tile margin per side. Note this shared constant also governs `InteriorDecorator`'s placement reach, which is now ±30 as well. Verified via `mcs` syntax parsing. Backed up as `Backups/Remodeling.cs.2026-11-08-094510.bak`.
37. **Yard tools — region ignored entirely (house ownership only)** — after region-based checks kept blocking placement inside the Ranger Outpost, `LawnTarget.ValidatePlacement` was simplified: it no longer looks at the target region at all and no longer enforces a distance/footprint bound. The only requirement is that the player is standing inside a house they own or co-own (`BaseHouse.FindHouseAt(from)` + `IsCoOwner`). The unused `IsWithinHouseLand` helper was removed. (This drops the house-relative distance cap; placement distance is now limited only by the client's target range.) Verified via `mcs` syntax parsing. Backed up as `Backups/LawnTarget.cs.2026-11-08-100718.bak`.
38. **Yard margin reverted to 15** — set `Remodeling.Left/Right/Front/Back` back to 15 (was temporarily 30). Lawn tools (`LawnTarget`) no longer use these constants (region/distance checks were removed), so this only affects the house decorator's reach and the `OutDoorRegion` house-owner `AllowHousing` margin. Verified via `mcs` syntax parsing. Backed up as `Backups/Remodeling.cs.2026-11-08-102029.bak`.
39. **Yard range restriction restored (house-relative, region still ignored)** — after the distance check was removed, yard placement had no limit. Re-added `LawnTarget.IsWithinHouseLand`, now using the house's `Components` bounding box (more reliable than `Area`) plus the `Remodeling` margin (15 per side) = footprint + 15. Region remains ignored; the only requirements are being inside a house you own/co-own and the target landing within that footprint+15 area. Verified via `mcs` syntax parsing. Backed up as `Backups/LawnTarget.cs.2026-11-08-102748.bak`.
40. **Umbra: guards ignore murderers; NPCs still serve them** — in `IntelligentAction.GetMyEnemies` (`Behavior.cs`), a `PlayerMobile` with `Kills > 0` or `Criminal` while in `UmbraRegion` is now treated as a non-enemy (extending the existing Umbra exemption for Death Knights/evil races). This makes `TownGuards.IsEnemy` return false (Umbra guards don't attack murderers) and clears the final `GetMyEnemies` gate in NPC access checks. Also added `UmbraRegion` to the `publicRegion` check in `BaseCreature.CheckChattingAccess` (talk/teach/interact) and `BaseVendor.CheckVendorAccess` (buy/sell), so murderers get normal NPC service in Umbra. Region matched by type (`UmbraRegion`, "the Undercity of Umbra"). Only initiating hostility is suppressed — a guard still defends itself if attacked; non-murderers and hostile creatures there are unaffected; outside Umbra nothing changes. Verified via `mcs` syntax parsing. Backups: `Backups/Behavior.cs.2026-11-08-112024.bak`, `Backups/BaseCreature.cs.2026-11-08-112024.bak`, `Backups/BaseVendor.cs.2026-11-08-112024.bak`.
41. **Umbra: guards respond if a murderer commits a fresh crime** — narrowed the Umbra exemption in `IntelligentAction.GetMyEnemies` from `(m.Criminal || m.Kills > 0)` to `m.Kills > 0 && !m.Criminal`. A murderer is now left alone in Umbra only while they are **not** currently criminal. If they attack an NPC (or an innocent player), the normal `BaseWeapon.OnHit → DoHarmful → IsHarmfulCriminal (blue NPC == Innocent) → CriminalAction` path sets their `Criminal` flag, `GetMyEnemies` stops exempting them, and Umbra guards attack them; NPC vendors also temporarily refuse service while criminal. Once the criminal flag lapses, they're tolerated again. Verified via `mcs` syntax parsing. Backed up as `Backups/Behavior.cs.2026-11-08-114344.bak`.
42. **Moongate: Umbra Cave vortex for negative-karma players** — added a fallback in `GateMoon`'s dynamic slot (gate 30) so a player who is **not** Glade-eligible and has **no other Vortex gate** can reach "Vortex - Umbra Cave" **only if `Karma < 0`**. `GetGateName(30)` now returns the Umbra Cave for non-evil negative-karma players (instead of the locked Glade slot) and for evil non-creature players with negative karma; `OnResponse(gate30)` maps that to the Umbra Cave entrance (`5254,1063,5` on Sosaria, same as the evil-dead race vortex). The gump's red CANCEL only applies to the real Druid's Glade destination, so the Umbra Cave fallback is a normal selectable button. Conditions: `Karma < 0`, not already Glade-accessible, and not already eligible for a race-based Vortex. Backed up as `Backups/GateMoon.cs.2026-11-08-120059.bak`.
43. **Umbra door shares the Umbra Cave vortex access** — `RaceTeleporter`'s `"umbra"` door (`UseTeleporter`) previously only let evil-dead creatures (`IsEvilDeadCreature && RaceHomeLand == 1`) through. It now uses a new shared predicate `GateMoon.CanUseUmbraCave( m )` that returns true when `GetGateName( DYNAMIC_GATE_ID, m ) == "Vortex - Umbra Cave"`, so exactly the players who can take the **"Vortex - Umbra Cave"** moongate (evil-dead land 1, or any negative-karma player with no other Vortex) can now pass the door. Everyone else still gets "The doors seem to be rusted shut!". Backed up as `Backups/RaceTeleporter.cs.2026-11-08-120728.bak` and `Backups/GateMoon.cs.2026-11-08-120728.bak`.
44. **Lawn/Shanty decoration removal: no line-of-sight requirement** — `LawnRemoveTarget` (and the identical `ShantyRemoveTarget`) defaulted to `Target.CheckLOS = true`, so refunding/removing a decoration failed with "Target can not be seen." when it was out of line of sight (e.g. behind a wall). Both now set `CheckLOS = false` in their constructors, mirroring the placement targets (`LawnTarget`/`ShantyTarget`). The ownership check (`item.House != m_House`) and the `CanSee` visibility gate are unchanged; only the LOS requirement is removed. Backed up as `Backups/LawnRemoveTarget.cs.2026-11-08-122014.bak` and `Backups/ShantyRemoveTarget.cs.2026-11-08-122014.bak`.
45. **Memento Collector wears all standard artefacts** — changed only the collector's gear. `DressArmour` now dresses every armour slot (gorget/arms/gloves/legs/chest) plus boots and a helm with a **standard artefact** each (falling back to dark leather only if the catalogue has nothing on that layer); the hued `Cloak`/`BodySash` are kept, and the lit `Lantern` is now added after the wielded artefact so it is always carried. `SwapWithArtefact` iterates the layer's artefacts in random order so a gender-locked entry can't leave plain leather. No behaviour/coin logic was touched. Backed up as `Backups/MementoCollector.cs.2026-11-08-124115.bak`.
46. **Exodus boss rework (barrier/retaliation only)** — (a) Removed the pet-kill mechanic: pets that melee the barrier are no longer hit with a 10,000-damage EBolt (`SendEBoltOnPet` deleted). (b) Barrier HP threshold lowered from **90% to 50%** (`CanUseField`; `OnThink` now re-raises the field whenever HP is at/above 50%, instead of only at full HP). (c) Removed the 25% spell reflection (`CheckReflect` override deleted). (d) Replaced the retaliatory EBolt on spell damage with casting **Fire Field** and **Meteor Swarm** on the caster — reproduced directly (`CastFireFieldOn`/`CastMeteorOn`) because the spell cast-sequence can't resolve a target for an NPC; still gated on the original 40% roll. Ranged-attacker EBolt unchanged. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-132834.bak`.
47. **Exodus dungeon relocation disabled (TEMPORARY — testing)** — commented out `Worlds.MoveToRandomDungeon( this )` in `OnAfterSpawn` so Exodus stays wherever it is spawned. The burn-away spawn visual is kept. Re-enable by uncommenting that line. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-134032.bak`.
48. **Exodus stats tripled (TEMPORARY — testing)** — tripled the core stat block: STR 500-700→1500-2100, DEX 177-255→531-765, INT 151-250→453-750, Hits 400-500→1200-1500, Damage 18-23→54-69. Resistances, skills (already 110), Fame/Karma and VirtualArmor were left unchanged. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-134523.bak`.
49. **Exodus health doubled again (TEMPORARY — testing)** — `SetHits` 1200-1500 → **2400-3000** (~6× the original 400-500). Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-134711.bak`.
50. **Exodus skills set to 125 (TEMPORARY — testing)** — all five combat skills (Anatomy, Psychology, MagicResist, Tactics, FistFighting) raised from 110 → **125** (`SetSkill` auto-raises the per-skill cap to match). Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-134750.bak`.
51. **Exodus near-instant natural regen (TEMPORARY — testing)** — in `RegenRates.Mobile_HitsRegenRate`, added `if ( from is Exodus ) points += 200;` (following the existing `Leviathan` special-case). This drives the standard `HitsTimer` to its ~50 ms floor, regenerating ~1 HP every ~50 ms (≈20 HP/s) with **no new timer**. Backed up as `Backups/RegenRates.cs.2026-11-08-135201.bak`.
52. **Exodus damage halved (TEMPORARY — testing)** — `SetDamage` 54-69 → **27-34**. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-135529.bak`.
53. **Exodus barrier: removed Fire Field, kept Meteor** — deleted the `CastFireFieldOn` helper and its call in `OnDamagedBySpell`; the 40% spell retaliation now casts only **Meteor Swarm** on the caster. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-135634.bak`.
54. **Exodus bleeds burning blood when struck** — added a `MonsterSplatter` burning-blood splash in `OnGotMeleeAttack`, following the MagmaElemental/Daemon pattern: name "burning blood", hue `0x489`, glowing, 1-in-4 chance, and only one splatter within 10 tiles at a time. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-140142.bak`.
55. **Exodus periodic greater-explosion strikes** — added a repeating `ExplosionTimer` (3s interval). While a player is within 20 tiles, it picks a random point within 5 tiles of Exodus, draws a temporary fiery landing indicator (`Effects` `0x3709`, fire hue), then 1.5s later detonates a greater-explosion blast there (`0x36B0`/`0x307`) dealing **70–80 fire** (split among >2 targets) to **PlayerMobiles only** — Exodus and other NPCs are never hit. Timer starts in the ctor and `Deserialize`, and is stopped in `OnAfterDelete`. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-141141.bak`.
56. **Exodus buffs: potion range/rate + meteor damage** — (a) periodic greater-explosion blast radius doubled (`GetMobilesInRange` 2→4). (b) potion rate upped to ~10 per 3 s (`ExplosionTimer` interval 3.0s→0.3s, priority OneSecond→FiftyMS). (c) barrier Meteor Swarm damage 25–40→**125–200** (5×). Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-141936.bak`.
57. **Exodus potion burst corrected (landing spread, simultaneous 10)** — reverted the blast radius (back to 2) and the 0.3s cadence. Instead doubled the **landing spread** (offsets −10..10, clamp 100 = within 10 tiles) and made the timer fire a **burst of 10 potions at once** every 3 s (`ExplosionTimer` back to 3.0s/OneSecond; `DropExplosion` now loops 10× `LaunchExplosion`). Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-142142.bak`.
58. **Exodus potions now come in 3×3 blocks (5 per burst)** — `DropExplosion` releases **5 blocks** per 3 s; each `LaunchBlock` picks a center within 10 tiles, then spawns a filled **3×3** (9 tiles) via `LaunchExplosion( map, x, y )` = **45 simultaneous blasts** per burst, all telegraphed 1.5 s ahead, still radius 2, players-only. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-142352.bak`.
59. **Exodus explosion damage halved** — periodic greater-explosion blast damage 70–80 → **35–40** (`ExplodeArea`). Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-142800.bak`.
60. **Exodus explosions gated to combat** — `DropExplosion` now only fires while `Combatant != null` (i.e. he's fighting someone), replacing the previous "player within 20 tiles" gate. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-143434.bak`.
61. **Meteor leaves a 5×5 conflagration field** — `CastMeteorOn` now spawns a `ConflagrationPotion.Explode` (5×5 burning fire field, ~10 s, spares the thrower/Exodus) centered on the meteor's landing spot. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-143633.bak`.
62. **Meteor: ground-target with warning (distinct hue)** — `CastMeteorOn` now aims at the **ground beneath** the attacker (`GetSurfaceTop`), shows a warning at that tile (`0x3709`, hue **0x480** — deliberately different from the explosion indicator's `0x489`), then after 1.5 s `MeteorImpact` lands there: **125–200 fire** to the original attacker if still within ~3 tiles (dodgeable), plus the 5×5 conflagration. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-144022.bak`.
63. **Removed the burning blood** — deleted the `MonsterSplatter` "burning blood" splash from `OnGotMeleeAttack` (it fell through to the splatter's damaging default, so it hurt anyone stepping on it). Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-151527.bak`.
64. **Removed the meteor (and conflagration) retaliation** — deleted the `CastMeteorOn`/`MeteorImpact` methods and the `OnDamagedBySpell` trigger that cast them, so spell hits no longer drop a meteor + 5×5 conflagration. `OnDamagedBySpell` now only handles the barrier drop. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-151848.bak`.
65. **Exodus explosion retuned** — warning delay 1.5s→**2s**; cadence 3s→**10s** (`ExplosionTimer`); now casts a **single 3×3 block** centered on a **random player within 20 tiles** (removed the five random-spot blocks and `LaunchBlock`). Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-152339.bak`.
66. **Barrier spell reflect (40%) + damage −⅓** — re-added `CheckReflect` so the barrier reflects **40%** of incoming spells back at the caster (only while `m_FieldActive`); base damage lowered by a third: `SetDamage` 27–34 → **18–23**. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-152512.bak`.
67. **Reverted ALL barrier changes** — restored the original barrier: `CanUseField` **90%** (was 50%), `OnThink` re-raise only at full HP (`!IsHurt`), `CheckReflect` **25% unconditional** (was 40% barrier-gated), and the **EBolt-on-spell** + **pet-kill** (`SendEBoltOnPet`) retaliations and their methods. Non-barrier changes (stats, explosions, spawn, regen, base damage) untouched. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-152750.bak`.
68. **Reverted the near-instant regen** — removed the `if ( from is Exodus ) points += 200;` special-case from `RegenRates.Mobile_HitsRegenRate`, restoring normal creature regeneration. Backed up as `Backups/RegenRates.cs.2026-11-08-152927.bak`.
69. **Exodus base damage −⅓ (again)** — `SetDamage` 18–23 → **12–15**. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-153230.bak`.
70. **Conflagration ring on every 25% HP lost** — added `m_RingBands` tracking in `OnThink`: each time Exodus crosses **75% / 50% / 25%** HP, `StartConflagrationRing` runs a `RingTimer` that expands one Chebyshev ring per second from his location out to **15 tiles**, **skipping every other tile** (checkerboard). Each tile shows the explosion-style warning (`0x3709`, hue `0x489`), then 1 s later a real `BaseConflagrationPotion.InternalItem` fire ignites there and is deleted after **3 s**. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-155755.bak`.
71. **Conflagration ring — 10 s cooldown** — added `m_NextRingAllowed`; the ring now triggers at most once every **10 seconds** (`reached > m_RingBands && DateTime.Now >= m_NextRingAllowed`, cooldown set on fire). Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-160007.bak`.
72. **Reverted the last base-damage nerf** — `SetDamage` 12–15 → **18–23** (back to his original value). Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-160151.bak`.
73. **Tripled base damage** — `SetDamage` 18–23 → **54–69** (3×). Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-160250.bak`.
74. **Conflagration ring spacing + damage** — fire tiles now leave **exactly one empty tile between them** (even-even grid: skip if `(dx & 1)` or `(dy & 1)`; was checkerboard `(dx+dy)&1`); fire damage raised to **Greater Conflagration** values (**20–30**, was 10–20). Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-161116.bak`.
75. **Exodus no longer flees** — added `!(m_Mobile is Exodus)` to the low-health flee condition in `MeleeAI.DoActionCombat` (`Behavior.cs`), so Exodus never sets `ActionType.Flee` and keeps fighting instead of running when below ~20% HP. Backed up as `Backups/Behavior.cs.2026-11-08-161519.bak`.
76. **Exodus gains Magery 125** — added `SetSkill( SkillName.Magery, 125.0 )`. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-161757.bak`.
77. **Exodus is now a mage** — `AIType.AI_Melee` → **`AIType.AI_Mage`** (`FightMode.Closest, 10, 1, 0.2, 0.4` unchanged), so he now actively casts spells with his 125 Magery. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-162041.bak`.
78. **Removed the conflagration ring** — deleted the 25%-HP conflagration ring entirely: `m_RingBands`/`m_NextRingAllowed` fields, the `OnThink` trigger, `StartConflagrationRing`, `RingTimer`, and `IgniteRingFire`. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-162619.bak`.
79. **Melee damage −20%** — `SetDamage` 54–69 → **43–55**. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-162816.bak`.
80. **Explosion cooldown 10 s → 8 s** — `ExplosionTimer` interval 10.0 s → **8.0 s** (periodic 3×3 greater-explosion burst). Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-162927.bak`.
81. **Exodus drops champion-style gold on death** — `OnBeforeDeath` now calls `SpawnChampGold()`, mirroring `BaseChampion`: it starts a `GoldTimer` for every tile within a 12-tile radius, each spawning a `Gold(300–800 × GetGoldCutRate)` pile after a random 0–10 s delay with a burst effect. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-163202.bak`.
82. **Two horde minion items on death** — `OnDeath` now drops **two `HoardMinionFamiliarItem`** into his corpse (like a champion's minion drop). Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-163421.bak`.
83. **Melee damage −25%** — `SetDamage` 43–55 → **32–41**. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-164345.bak`.
85. **Str & base damage −20%** — `SetStr` 1500–2100 → **1200–1680**; `SetDamage` 32–41 → **26–33**. Effective melee (pre-resist) ≈ **160–251**; vs a 70-all-resist target ≈ **48–75**/hit (50% phys / 50% energy, both reduced 70%). Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-172834.bak`.
86. **Exodus flee — fear immunity (Dispel Evil / Cause Fear)** — overrode `BeginFlee` (no-op) and `CheckFlee` (returns false) on Exodus, so fear effects can no longer make him run (he's evil — `Karma < 0` — so Dispel Evil could `BeginFlee(30s)` him). Combined with the earlier `MeleeAI`/`MageAI` guard patches, he no longer flees from low HP **or** fear. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-173312.bak`.
87. **Str & base damage −25%** — `SetStr` 1200–1680 → **900–1260**; `SetDamage` 26–33 → **20–25**. Effective melee (pre-resist) ≈ **105–159**; vs 70-all-resist ≈ **32–48**/hit. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-175153.bak`.
88. **Exodus flee — the REAL fix (OmniAI)** — root cause: `BaseCreature.ChangeAIType` routes `AI_Mage` (and `AI_Melee`/`AI_Archer`/`AI_Berserk`/`AI_Predator`) to **`OmniAI`**, *not* `MageAI`/`MeleeAI`, so the earlier guard patches (#75/#84) never applied. Added `!(m_Mobile is Exodus)` to OmniAI's low-health flee guard (`OmniAI Core.cs` line 229). Combined with the `BeginFlee`/`CheckFlee` fear-immunity overrides (#86), Exodus now never flees. Backed up as `Backups/OmniAI Core.cs.2026-11-08-175428.bak`.
84. **Exodus flee fix (MageAI)** — he now uses `AI_Mage`, and the earlier no-flee patch only covered `MeleeAI`. Added `!(m_Mobile is Exodus)` to `MageAI.DoActionCombat`'s low-health flee condition (line 9411) too, so he no longer flees under the mage AI. Backed up as `Backups/Behavior.cs.2026-11-08-164556.bak`.
89. **Exodus spawn relocation restored** — re-enabled `Worlds.MoveToRandomDungeon( this )` in `OnAfterSpawn` (reverts TEMPORARY #47). Exodus relocates to a random dungeon on spawn again; the burn-away spawn visual is kept. Backed up as `Backups/Exodus.cs.2026-11-08-192433.bak`.
90. **Glistening ore veins — only visible to miners** — `RichVeinMineable` ("A glistening ore vein") now overrides `SendInfoTo(NetState, bool)` and skips sending entirely when the viewer's **trained (base) Mining is below 65** (staff, `AccessLevel > Player`, always see them). The 65 threshold matches `RichVeinMining.CheckHarvest`, which requires `Mining.Base >= 65` to mine a vein. `Visible` stays `true`, so the core still offers the vein to eligible clients through the normal range-update path, but ineligible clients are never sent the world packet or OPL. The proximity sparkle effect is also sent **only** to eligible miners (it previously broadcast to everyone in range, which would have revealed a vein that is invisible to them). Backed up as `Backups/RichVeinMineable.cs.2026-11-09-162448.bak`.
91. **New item: `ZorkPad` (green data pad running Zork)** — added `Source/Scripts/Items/Technology/ZorkPad.cs` and `ZorkGame.cs`. `ZorkPad : Item` uses the DataPad appearance (`0x27FB`/`0x27FC`) but is pinned to the shard's green hue **`0x85D`** (`ZorkPad.GreenHue`), `Technology = true`, `Light = Circle150`, weight 1. Double-clicking (within 4 tiles) opens `ZorkPadGump` — a large (740×560) black CRT with green phosphor text, a scrolling transcript and a typeable prompt + ENTER button. Input is parsed by `ZorkGame`, a compact text-adventure engine covering the White House area of Zork I (West/North/South/Behind House, Forest, Clearing, Kitchen, Living Room, Attic, Cellar) with a real parser (n/s/e/w/up/down, look, examine, take, drop, open/close, read, move, turn on/off lamp, inventory, enter window, wait, score, restart), item handling (mailbox/leaflet, sword, brass lantern with darkness, rug revealing the trap door, trophy case, rope) and a grue death if you wander the dark cellar without the lamp. Per-player progress is held in memory keyed by mobile serial (resets on server restart).
92. **ZorkPad — Enter submits the command** — pressing Enter in the prompt now runs the command. Core: added `Gump.OnTextEntry` and a `0xAC` (TextEntry) packet handler that finds the gump by serial and invokes it (defensive parse for clients that include an extra 2-byte button field). `ZorkPadGump` overrides `OnTextEntry` to **simulate a click on the ENTER button** (it feeds the text through `OnResponse` with button id 2, so Enter and the button are identical) — and also treats a button-0 reply that carries text as a submit. This pass also doubled the gump to 740×560 with a solid black background (gump tile `2624` — the shard's own "Black box") and green `#00FF06` text, up to 30 transcript lines, and a `build 6` marker in the header (plus a dark-gray border: tile `9200` frame around the black `2624` interior, and a labelled **SEND** button — button art `4005`/`4007` with a green "SEND" label) so you can confirm the new scripts are running; the ENTER button still works. Backed up as `Backups/PacketHandlers.cs.2026-11-09-180202.bak` and `Backups/Gump.cs.2026-11-09-180202.bak`.
93. **Reverted the 0xAC packet handler (Enter-via-packet removed)** — `Source/System/Network/PacketHandlers.cs` restored to its original state (no `0xAC` registration, no `TextEntry` handler, no startup `[Memento]` marker), so pressing Enter no longer reaches the server. Also removed the gump's button-0 "close-with-text" fallback and its debug message, so **only the SEND button (id 2) submits** — closing the pad with text left in the box no longer re-submits. `Gump.OnTextEntry` and `ZorkPadGump.OnTextEntry` remain in place but are now unreachable (dead code) and can be removed for a full undo.
94. **Reverted `Gump.cs` (completed the undo)** — restored `Source/System/Gumps/Gump.cs` to its original state (removed the `OnTextEntry` virtual) and deleted the now-orphaned `ZorkPadGump.OnTextEntry` override in `ZorkPad.cs`. The Enter‑via‑0xAC feature is fully removed; only the **SEND** button submits commands. Both core files (`PacketHandlers.cs`, `Gump.cs`) are back to original, so `git status` shows only the new Zork item/engine files and this tracker.
95. **ZorkPad — one screen at a time (no scrollback)** — `ZorkGame.Process` now calls `st.Log.Clear()` at the start of every command, so the display is replaced each turn instead of appending and scrolling. The transcript `AddHtml` has its scrollbar turned off (`scrollbar=false`) and the text area grew to 300 px tall with the prompt row moved down (y≈468) to fit a full screen without clipping. Header marker bumped to **`build 7`**.
96. **ZorkPad — simulated QWERTY keyboard** — added a clickable keyboard beneath the screen in proper QWERTY order (digits row, QWERTYUIOP, ASDFGHJKL, ZXCVBNM, then SPACE / BKSP / CLR / ENTER). Keys are plain `AddButton` art (`0x918`/`0x919` — no item overlay, so the "unused" placeholder graphic is gone), uniform 56 px wide, capital letters, and black centred labels (single characters `<BIG>`, word keys normal size so `ENTER`/`BKSP` stay on one line). The gump is a solid dark‑gray board (`9750`, a flat gray fill) with the black screen panel (`2624`) only behind the text area, so the gray board frames the gump and fills behind the keyboard. Gump 740×780 (keyboard nudged slightly left of the gump axis; the `>` prompt aligned with the entry line); header **`build 17`**. Clicking a key appends its (lowercase) letter to the input line; BKSP removes a char, CLR clears, SPACE adds a space, ENTER submits. Because a client's text entry can't be written to, the in-progress line is kept server-side (`m_Drafts`, per mobile) and the gump is re-opened with it as the text entry's `initialText`. Gump is 740×780 with the screen, prompt and keyboard all centred on the gump's axis and the keyboard sitting at the bottom; header now **`build 17`**. The separate ENTER button beside the input was removed (the keyboard ENTER submits) and the prompt marker uses a literal `>` because the client doesn't decode the `&gt;` entity.
97. **ZorkPad — logic pass: clear only on movement + room-graph fixes** — the screen clears **when you move to a new room or type `look` / an empty line** (`Go`, `Enter`, the grue respawn, and the `look` case all call `st.Log.Clear()`), while ordinary actions (`look`, `examine`, `take`, …) append beneath what's already shown (supersedes #95's per-command clearing). Room graph corrected toward Zork I: the **attic is reached from the Kitchen** staircase (`up`), not the Living Room — removed the Living Room's `up` exit and made the Attic `down` return to the Kitchen; the Kitchen description now mentions the staircase, and the Cellar description no longer promises north/south passages it doesn't have. Header `build 18`.
98. **ZorkPad — fixed the literal `&gt;` in the transcript** — `ZorkGame.Escape()` no longer HTML-encodes `>`/`&`; this client doesn't decode entities, so the `> command` echo was printing as `&gt;`. It now only strips `<` (to avoid starting a tag) and leaves `>`/`&` as plain text. Header `build 19`.
99. **ZorkPad — scoring / treasure hunt** — added a score system. Three treasures: **jeweled egg** (Attic, 5 pts), **platinum bar** (Cellar, 10 pts), **huge emerald** (Clearing, 5 pts). `put <treasure> in case` while in the Living Room banks it in the trophy case for its points (`put` now parses `in`/`into`; a bare `put` still drops). When all three are banked the game prints `YOU HAVE WON!`; `score` reports points/moves/treasures, and the running **`SCORE n/20`** is shown top‑right, aligned with the `ZORK I   build 20` line. This is a scoring layer over the White House slice — **not** the full ~110‑room Zork I.
100. **ZorkPad — room/exit audit & fixes** — went through the room graph line by line and fixed the "not lining up" problems: `South of House` `south` used to land in the single `Forest` room whose only exit led to **West of House** (wrong side); it now leads to a dedicated `forestsouth` (north → `South of House`). Added `Behind House` `west → Kitchen` (the window, guarded by `windowOpen`) so the Kitchen↔Behind‑House link is reciprocal in both directions. `Obvious exits` is now printed in a fixed compass order (north, south, east, west, up, down, out) instead of random dictionary order. Verified with a script: all exits resolve to existing rooms and every exit has a back‑path; all 12 things are defined and all item placements point at real rooms/containers. Header `build 22`.
101. **ZorkPad — world build‑out, stage 1** — expanded the map. **Forest ring:** `Forest` (west of house) now links `north → forest1 → east → Forest Path` and `south → forest2 → east → forestsouth` (→ South of House), so the whole west/north/south of the house is a closed loop instead of dead ends. **Underground:** the Cellar gains a real `north` passage to a new dark **Stone Room**. New treasure **sack of coins** (5 pts) in the Stone Room → now **4 treasures / 25 points** (`SCORE n/25`). Graph re‑verified: **15 rooms, all exits reciprocal**, 13 things, placements valid. Header `build 23`.
102. **ZorkPad — world build‑out, stage 2 (underground + combat)** — added **Troll Room → East‑West Passage → {Round Room, Small Cave}** below the Stone Room (all dark, need the lantern). New **`attack`/`kill`** verb: three landed hits with the carried **elvish sword** kill the troll (no weapon = you can't hurt it); the troll vanishes when dead. **Brass bauble** (5 pts) in the Troll Room is guarded until the troll dies; **silver chalice** (5 pts) in the Small Cave. Now **6 treasures / 35 points** (`SCORE n/35`). Graph re‑verified: **19 rooms, all exits reciprocal**, 16 things, placements valid. Header `build 24`.
103. **ZorkPad — world build‑out, stage 3 (dam & reservoir puzzle)** — pushed past the troll: **Round Room → Loud Room → Dam → Reservoir → Drained Passage**. New water puzzle: `open sluice` (or `open gate`) in the Dam opens the sluice (`gateOpen` flag); descending (`down`) from the Reservoir is blocked while it's full ("you would drown") and only works once drained. The **Drained Passage** holds a **jade figurine** (5 pts). `close sluice` refills it. Now **7 treasures / 40 points** (`SCORE n/40`). Graph re‑verified: **23 rooms, all exits reciprocal**, 18 things, placements valid. Header `build 25`.
104. **ZorkPad — world build‑out, stage 4 (coal mine & machine room)** — `East‑West Passage` now leads `north` to the **Coal Mine Entrance**; a **boulder** blocks the shaft (`move boulder` rolls it aside → `down` opens). Below: **Coal Mine** (star sapphire, 5 pts) → `east` → **Machine Room** (bag of diamonds, 10 pts). Now **9 treasures / 55 points** (`SCORE n/55`). Graph re‑verified: **26 rooms, all exits reciprocal**, 21 things, placements valid. Header `build 26`.
105. **ZorkPad — world build‑out, stage 5 (the Thief + combat/health)** — added a **player health** system (`MaxHealth = 10`, shown in the status line as `HP:n/10`). The **Thief** waits in the Round Room guarding a **golden crown** (10 pts); `attack thief` fights 4 rounds with the sword — **each exchange costs you 1 HP**, and if you have no weapon he just stabs you. Reaching 0 HP respawns you west of the house with health restored. Killing the thief drops the crown (guarded by `thiefDead`). Now **10 treasures / 65 points** (`SCORE n/65`). Graph re‑verified: **26 rooms, all exits reciprocal**, 23 things, placements valid. Header `build 27`.
106. **ZorkPad — world build‑out, stage 6 (winnable game, ranks & endgame)** — added three treasures — **jeweled scarab** (10, Forest), **silver bell** (10, Clearing), **pot of gold** (15, Machine Room) — for **13 treasures / 100 points** (`SCORE n/100`). `score` now reports a **rank** (Beginner → Amateur → Junior → Seasoned → Expert → **Master Adventurer**). Banking the final treasure prints the **endgame text** (rank + final score + "RESTART to play again"). `HELP` now mentions `attack` and `put <treasure> in case`. Graph re‑verified: **26 rooms, all exits reciprocal**, 26 things, 13 treasures, 100 max points. Header `build 28`.
107. **ZorkPad — ported the complete Zork I map (110 rooms)** — replaced the hand‑built 26‑room slice with a **generated port of the original Zork I room graph and object table**, extracted mechanically from the ZIL source (`github.com/historicalsource/zork1`, MIT licence © 2025 Microsoft — attribution header in the new file). New file `World/Source/Scripts/Items/Technology/ZorkWorld.cs` (849 generated lines) holds `BuildRooms()` / `BuildThings()` / `BuildGates()` / `InitWorld()`; `ZorkGame` is now `partial`.
  - **110 rooms, 315 exits, 37 "blocked‑exit" messages, 38 conditional exit gates, 122 objects with their original names/aliases/values and 93 starting placements.**
  - Static audit: every exit resolves to a real room, every gate points at a real room, every placement resolves, and **all 110 rooms are reachable** from West of House.
  - Scoring rebuilt for the real game: **19 treasures + 65 room‑visit points = 229 max score**; `SCORE n/229`, rank ladder, trophy‑case win unchanged.
  - Engine gained: **container model** (nested, open/close, surfaces), **per‑exit gates** (`GateOK`), **wall messages**, **diagonal/land directions**, **room‑visit scoring**, maze randomiser, `RandomMaze`.
  - New verbs: `climb`, `enter window`, `say` (`xyzzy`, `odysseus` → cyclops flees, `echo` → Loud Room), `wave sceptre` (solid rainbow), `ring bell` (exorcism), `tie rope` (Dome Room), `inflate`/`deflate` boat, `launch`/`board` (river chain), `dig` (scarab), plus `turn bolt` (dam → low tide) and `turn switch` (machine → coal makes diamond).
  - Header `build 29`. Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, **zero errors**.
108. **ZorkPad — first functional test pass, 6 real bugs fixed** — added `World/Source/Tools/ZorkTest/ztest.cs`, a harness that loads the built assemblies, creates a state via reflection and drives `ZorkGame.Process()` one command at a time. **59 checks / 59 pass.** It found and these fixes address:
  - **Global objects were unfindable** — `IsPresent` checked the (null) location before the `Global` flag, so `open window`, `climb tree`, `look at forest` etc. all failed with "You don't see that here." The window gate made the kitchen — and therefore the whole game — unreachable.
  - **`in`/`enter` ignored room exits** — the verb went straight to the house-window handler, so the Entrance to Hades, Stone Barrow and every other `IN` exit could never be used.
  - **The thief had 0 HP** (the `ThiefHP = 4` initialiser was lost when `NewState` was rewritten) — he died on the first hit.
  - **Room-visit points were awarded when leaving a room** instead of when entering it (Kitchen 10, Cellar 25, Treasure Room 25, E‑W Passage 5).
  - **Rank never reached Master on a win** — `Rank` now returns *Master Adventurer* whenever `Won` is set.
  - **`climb` mis-parsed** "tree" (the `trees` synonym of *forest* matched first) — `Climb` now matches the typed text and the room's `up` exit.
  - Verified end-to-end: a full 19-treasure playthrough banks **229/229** and prints the win text at *Master Adventurer*.
109. **ZorkPad — the remaining action routines ported (thief AI, bat, gas, dumbwaiter, mirror, egg)** — the six behaviours that were still just data now run, transcribed from `1actions.zil` (`I-THIEF`, `ROBBER-FUNCTION`, `ROB`, `DEPOSIT-BOOTY`, `BAT-F`, `FLY-ME`, `BOOM-ROOM`, `BASKET-F`, `MIRROR-MIRROR`):
  - **The thief now wanders and robs.** He moves between 30 haunt rooms on a turn-by-turn tick, announces himself (*"Someone carrying a large bag is casually leaning against one of the walls here…"*), and while he shares your room he has a 30 % chance per turn to lift a carried treasure (*"You suddenly notice that the X vanished."*) into his bag. `give <treasure> to thief` makes him stop and admire it (he is engrossed for six turns); `attack thief` works wherever he is, not just the Round Room; and when he dies his whole bag — stolen treasures, stiletto, loot — drops where he fell (*"As the thief dies, the power of his magic decreases…"*).
  - **The vampire bat.** Entering the Bat Room without garlic gets you flown to a random mine room (*"The bat grabs you by the scruff of your neck and lifts you away…"*). With garlic in hand **or** in the room he stays on the ceiling (`take`/`attack` → *"You can't reach him; he's on the ceiling."*), which is how you safely take the jade.
  - **Coal gas.** Entering the Gas Room while carrying a burning torch, the temple candles or a lit match detonates: *"…the smell coming from this room was coal gas"* / `** BOOOOOOOOOOOM **`, and you wake up west of the house.
  - **The dumbwaiter.** `raise basket` / `lower basket` in the Shaft Room or Lower Shaft moves the raised/lowered baskets between the two ends of the shaft.
  - **The mirror rooms.** `rub mirror` swaps the entire contents of the two mirror rooms and moves you through (*"There is a rumble from deep within the earth and the room shakes."*); `attack mirror` smashes it for good (*"The mirror breaks into a thousand pieces…"*), after which rubbing only reports the pieces.
  - **The egg is a trap.** `attack egg` smashes it and crushes the clockwork canary inside (leaving the broken egg); opening it is the correct move.
  - **Crash fix found by the tests:** `attack mirror` threw `KeyNotFoundException` (there is no object literally called "mirror") and would have taken the server down; `Matches` now fails closed on unknown ids.
  - Test harness extended to **76 checks, all passing** (stable over repeated runs). Header `build 30`. Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
110. **ZorkPad — authentic 350‑point scoring, real lighting, weight‑based crawlways** — the score is now computed exactly as `V-SCORE`/`SCORE-OBJ`/`OTVAL-FROB` do in `gverbs.zil`, so the game tops out at the original **350 points** instead of my invented 229:
  - **`SCORE = BASE-SCORE + sum(TVALUE of everything in the trophy case)`.** `VALUE` is awarded once when a treasure is first taken (or first put in the case); `TVALUE` is the running value of the case contents; rooms award their own `VALUE` on first entry (Kitchen 10, Cellar 25, Treasure Room 25, E‑W Passage 5 = 65); the **lit Lower Shaft is worth 13**; and **every death costs 10**. 143 + 129 + 65 + 13 = **350 exactly**.
  - **Rooms are lit by `ONBIT`, not by a hand‑written list.** 33 rooms are permanently lit (Kitchen, Living Room, the Dam, Gallery, both Temples, Entrance to Hades, Land of the Living Dead, Mirror Room 2, River 1/5, Shore…) and the other 77 need light — my previous curated list had the Dam and Hades dark and the mine entrance lit.
  - **Lights are real objects** (`LIGHTBIT`): the brass lantern, the ivory torch and the temple candles light whatever room they are in — including **inside the dumbwaiter basket**, which is how the Lower Shaft's 13 points are earned: put the burning candles in the basket, `lower basket`, drop everything heavy and crawl through.
  - **Crawlways use Zork's weight rule** — every carried item must weigh 4 or less (`SIZE`), not "empty-handed" as I had it, so the lamp (15), torch (20) and candles (10) are what actually stop you.
  - **The grue is a chance, not a certainty** (30 % per dark move), matching the original's "you are likely to be eaten" — that is what makes dashing through the dark Timber Room survivable.
  - **`TRYTAKEBIT` no longer means "untakeable"** — the rope and the chalice were both impossible to pick up. Items are now takeable per `TAKEBIT`, with guards: the chalice is held by the thief's magic until he dies, and the troll/thief keep their own weapons while alive.
  - The rank ladder is the original one: 350 *Master Adventurer*, >330 *Wizard*, >300 *Master*, >200 *Adventurer*, >100 *Junior*, >50 *Novice*, >25 *Amateur*, else *Beginner*; `score` reads "Your score is N (total of 350 points), in M moves…".
  - Dumbwaiter now starts raised (it was inverted, so `lower basket` reported "already at the bottom").
  - Tests: **98 checks, all passing**, including a full walk that earns the 350 (four point‑rooms + lit lower shaft + every treasure found, taken and banked). Header `build 31`. Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
111. **ZorkPad — item erosion: lantern battery, the sword's glow, matches, food & water** — the last of the resource systems, all transcribed from `1actions.zil` / `gverbs.zil`:
  - **The brass lantern burns down.** `LAMP-TABLE` is `100 / 70 / 15`, so the lamp lasts **185 lit turns** and warns three times — *"The lamp appears a bit dimmer."* → *"The lamp is definitely dimmer now."* → *"The lamp is nearly out."* — then dies with *"You'd better have more light than from the brass lantern."* A dead lamp refuses to relight (*"A burned-out lamp won't light."*) and `examine lamp` reports *"The lamp has burned out."* The ivory torch is now the long‑lasting alternative, exactly as in the original.
  - **The elvish sword glows** (`I-SWORD`): *"Your sword has begun to glow very brightly."* when a villain (troll, thief or cyclops) shares the room, *"…a faint blue glow."* when one is only in an adjacent room, and *"Your sword is no longer glowing."* when the threat passes — printed only when the state changes, and also on `look`.
  - **Matches are finite** (`MATCH-COUNT 6`): `count matches`, `light match` (*"One of the matches starts to burn."*, two turns of light), *"I'm afraid that you have run out of matches."* when the book is empty, and the authentic trap — *"This room is drafty, and the match goes out instantly."* in the Timber Room and Lower Shaft.
  - **Food and drink** (`V-EAT`/`V-DRINK`): `eat garlic` (or the lunch) → *"Thank you very much. It really hit the spot."* and the item is consumed; `drink water` from the opened bottle → *"…I was rather thirsty (from all this talking, probably)."* with the water consumed, and `drink` at the reservoir/river/shore works straight from the water.
  - Eating the garlic before the Bat Room (or drinking the water early) now costs you, exactly as in the original.
  - Tests: **115 checks, all passing** (stable across repeated runs) — new coverage for the battery warnings and burn‑out, the three sword‑glow states, the matchbook count/burn‑out/draft, eating and both drinking paths. Header `build 32`. Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
112. **ZorkPad — throwing, the candle table, and the Tiny Cave draft** — the last of the verbs and light timers:
  - **`throw`** (`V-THROW`): `throw <thing>` drops it (*"Thrown."*); `throw <thing> at <creature>` makes it duck (*"The troll ducks as the nasty knife flies by and crashes to the ground."*); and `throw <thing> at me` runs Zork's joke death — *"…you fall over backwards trying to duck, and break your neck."* (−10 points, you wake outside).
  - **The lantern smashes** if thrown: *"The lamp has smashed into the floor, and the light has gone out."* — it becomes the broken lamp, so the game can no longer be lit that way.
  - **Throwing the knife at the thief** (`ROBBER-FUNCTION` THROW branch): a **10 %** chance he bolts — *"You evidently frightened the robber, though you didn't hit him. He flees, but the contents of his bag fall on the floor."* — dropping everything he had stolen; otherwise *"You missed… He does seem angered by your attempt."*
  - **The temple candles burn down** (`CANDLE-TABLE: 20 / 10 / 5` = **35 turns**): *"The candles grow shorter."* → *"The candles are becoming quite short."* → *"The candles won't last long now."* → *"You'd better have more light than from the pair of candles."* They start their timer only once handled, as in the source.
  - **The Tiny Cave is drafty**: entering with lit candles has a **50 %** chance of *"A gust of wind blows out your candles!"*
  - `give <thing> to <troll/cyclops/bat>` now answers *"It refuses it politely."* instead of the generic refusal.
  - Tests: **127 checks, all passing** (stable over repeated runs) — new coverage for all four throw paths, the smashed lamp, both thief‑throw outcomes (looped so the 10 % branch is exercised deterministically), the candle warnings/burn‑out and the Tiny Cave gust. Header `build 33`. Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
113. **ZorkPad — the canary, container capacity, the thief's blackout, colour verbs**:
  - **The windable canary** (`CANARY-OBJECT`): `wind canary` in the forest (Forest 1–3, the Path, or Up a Tree) calls a songbird — *"The canary chirps, slightly off-key, an aria from a forgotten opera…"* — and **the brass bauble drops into the grass**, which is where that treasure actually comes from. Elsewhere it only *"chirps blithely, if somewhat tinnily"*, and winding anything else gives *"There is an unpleasant grinding noise…"*. **Correction:** the bauble had been force‑placed in the Troll Room as a stand‑in; that hack is gone and the treasure is now obtained properly.
  - **`pray`** (`V-PRAY`): at the South Temple it teleports you to the forest — *"If you pray enough, your prayers may be answered."*
  - **Container capacity and lids** (`V-PUT`): a container with a lid must be opened first (*"The glass bottle isn't open."*) and over‑filling is refused (*"There's no room."*, comparing contents' weight against `CAPACITY`). The trophy case is exempt — the source gives it `TRANSBIT` and no `OPENBIT`, so it is open‑topped.
  - **The thief can be knocked out**: a sword hit has a 25 % chance of dropping him — *"The thief collapses, unconscious."* — spilling **his stiletto onto the floor** (now takeable) and stopping his robbing; eight turns later *"The robber revives, briefly feigning continued unconsciousness, and, when he sees his moment, scrambles away from you."* and he recovers the blade.
  - **Colour verbs**: `yell` (*"Aaaarrrrgggghhhh!"*), `zork` (*"At your service!"*), `hello <creature>` (*"…bows his head to you in greeting."*) / `hello <object>` (*"It's a well known fact that only schizophrenics say "Hello" to a …"*) and `swim` (*"Swimming isn't usually allowed in the dungeon."*).
  - A fleeing thief no longer "flees" back into the room he was standing in.
  - Tests: **143 checks, all passing** (25 consecutive clean runs) — new coverage for the canary/bauble chain, `pray`, all four colour verbs, the lid/capacity refusals, the knock‑out (stiletto on the floor) and the revival. Header `build 34`. Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
114. **ZorkPad — leap, forest ambience, the angered thief, the trophy case listing**:
  - **`leap`/`jump`** (`V-LEAP` / `V-SKIP`): jumping out of the tree lands you safely on the path (*"In a feat of unaccustomed daring, you manage to land on your feet without killing yourself."*); jumping where there is no way down is fatal — *"This was not a very safe place to try jumping."* plus a random *"Geronimo…"* / *"You should have looked before you leaped."* / *"In the movies, your life would be passing before your eyes."* — −10 points; `jump over troll` gets *"The troll is too big to jump over."*; anything else gets the `WHEEEEE` lines (*"Very good. Now you can go to the second grade."*).
  - **Forest ambience** (`I-FOREST-ROOM`): a 15 % chance per turn in Forest 1–3, the Path or Up a Tree of *"You hear in the distance the chirping of a song bird."*

With a rueful nod of his head, he steps backward into the gloom and disappears."*
  - **The trophy case shows what you have banked**: standing in the Living Room now prints *"The trophy case contains a crystal skull, a sceptre and …"*.
  - One ordering bug fixed: jumping out of the tree lost its message because movement clears the scroll.

115. **ZorkPad — final polish: the Living Room rewrites itself, the sword goes dark mid‑fight** — the last two cosmetic gaps:

Remaining known deltas are cosmetic (`sing`/`dance` are not Zork I verbs; the sword's `FIGHTBIT` mid‑round flourish), and the gump layer still needs an in‑client look.
116. **ZorkPad — polish: authentic room text, description modes, diagnose, gump footer** — the finishing pass:
  - **Six rooms now read exactly as Infocom wrote them**, transcribed from their `M-LOOK` routines: the Kitchen, Cellar, Loud Room, Dome Room, Torch Room and *"You are standing on the top of the Flood Control Dam #3, which was quite a tourist attraction in times far distant…"*
  - **Those descriptions are live**, as the originals are: the Kitchen's window is *"slightly ajar"* until you open it; the Loud Room is *"deafeningly loud with an undetermined rushing sound"* until the echo, then *"eerie in its quietness"*; the Dome and Torch Rooms grow a rope once it is tied; the Dam reports whether the sluice gates are shut, open, or draining the reservoir.
  - **`verbose` / `brief` / `superbrief`** (`V-VERBOSE`/`V-BRIEF`/`V-SUPER-BRIEF`): *"Maximum verbosity."*, *"Brief descriptions."*, *"Superbrief descriptions."* — brief mode describes a room only the first time, superbrief only names it, which makes the small terminal screen far more usable; the game still starts verbose.
  - **`diagnose`** (`V-DIAGNOSE`): *"You are in perfect health."* / *"You have a light wound, which will be cured with rest."* / *"You can survive several wounds."*
