117. **`ZorkPad` renamed to "Zork!" and made Exodus loot** — the item's display name is now **`Zork!`** (was "a vintage computer"); class, art, hue and behaviour are unchanged. **Exodus now always leaves a `ZorkPad` in his corpse** (`Exodus.OnDeath` → `c.DropItem( new ZorkPad() )`, alongside the two `HoardMinionFamiliarItem`s and before the `DarkCoreExodus`/statue roll), which also makes him the only source of the item — nothing else in `World/Source` referenced it. Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors; the 174-check engine suite still passes.
118. **`ZorkPad` — removed the keyboard footer strip** — reverted the `HELP   SCORE   INVENTORY   RESTART` line added in #116 (`AddHtml( 62, sy + 44, … )` deleted from `ZorkPadGump`). The gump is back to exactly its #116 layout minus that strip: board, black screen, title/score/status header, transcript, prompt, QWERTY keyboard and the `SPACE/BKSP/CLR/ENTER` row, with the grey board filling the space beneath. Only the gump changed; the engine and item are untouched.
119. **ZorkPad — room‑exit audit + the four conditional exits corrected** — re‑parsed `1dungeon.zil` in a fresh parser and diffed **every** exit against `ZorkWorld.cs`, room by room, direction by direction: **110 rooms, 0 discrepancies** (no missing/extra rooms, no wrong destinations, all 37 blocked exits and all `PER` exits present). The audit did expose that the four hand‑coded `PER` exits were approximations, and the source now says:
  - **Studio chimney** (`UP-CHIMNEY-FUNCTION`): you may climb only if you are carrying **exactly the lamp** — *"Going up empty-handed is a bad idea."* when empty, *"You can't get up there with what you're carrying."* otherwise; it previously always worked.
  - **Maze diodes** (`MAZE-DIODES`): they go to **specific** rooms, not a random maze room — MAZE‑7 → Dead End, MAZE‑9 → MAZE‑11, MAZE‑12 → MAZE‑5 — and **MAZE‑2 doesn't move at all**, it just says *"You won't be able to get back up to the tunnel you are going through when it gets to the next room."*
  - **Trap door** (`TRAP-DOOR-EXIT`): nothing until the rug is moved (*"You can't go that way."*), then *"The trap door is closed."* until it is opened.
  - **Grating** (`GRATING-EXIT` + `GRATE-FUNCTION`): it is **locked** behind a skull‑and‑crossbones lock — `open grate` now answers *"The grating is locked."* until `unlock grate with keys` is used from below (*"The grate is unlocked."*, and *"You can't reach the lock from here."* from the clearing; `lock grate` re‑locks it). The way down needs the leaves moved first (*"In disturbing the pile of leaves, a grating is revealed."*), then *"The grating is closed!"* until opened (*"The grating opens to reveal trees above you."*).
  - Two ordering bugs fixed along the way: the dark‑room grue check fired before the chimney check, and the maze warning was erased because moving clears the scroll (travel messages are now emitted after the room description).
  - Tests: **191 checks, all passing** (20 consecutive clean runs). Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
120. **ZorkPad — non‑cardinal directions were not parsed (bug fix)** — `ne`, `nw`, `se`, `sw` and `land` fell through the command switch to *"I don't know the word \"ne\"."*, so a large part of the map (every diagonal exit plus the river landings) was unreachable by typing — even though `HELP` advertised them and the exit list printed them. Added explicit cases for all four diagonals plus `land`, alongside the existing cardinals, and a `DirectionOf()` normaliser so the spelled and hyphenated forms work too:
  - `ne` / `northeast` / `north-east` / `nw` / `northwest` / `se` / `southeast` / `sw` / `southwest` / `land` / `ashore`
  - `go <dir>`, `walk <dir>`, `run <dir>` and `head <dir>` now forward to the direction parser (*"go ne"*, *"walk southeast"*), with *"You can't go that way."* when it isn't a direction.
  - Verified every direction that appears in the map (13: `north south east west ne nw se sw up down in out land`) is accepted, and tested each diagonal plus `land` against real exits: West of House `ne`→North of House, `se`→South of House, North of House `sw`→West of House, Cyclops Room `nw`→MAZE‑15, River‑1 `land`→Dam Base.
  - Tests: **201 checks, all passing** (15 consecutive clean runs).
121. **ZorkPad — exhaustive exit/direction audit (runtime, all 315 exits)** — instead of only diffing data, the harness now **walks every exit in every room**: for each of the 315 exits it builds a fresh state, lights the room from the floor (a torch in the room, so nothing heavy is carried), carries garlic (so the bat can't interfere), satisfies that exit's gate, issues the direction and asserts you land on the declared destination. Result: **315/315 correct**.
  - **All 27 direction spellings** are exercised against real exits: `north/n`, `south/s`, `east/e`, `west/w`, `ne/northeast/north-east`, `nw/northwest/north-west`, `se/southeast/south-east`, `sw/southwest/south-west`, `up/u`, `down/d`, `out`, `land/ashore`.
  - **Back-link audit:** exactly **23 one-way exits**, all authentic Zork I — the three forest gaps (`westofhouse→forest1`, `gratingclearing→forest2`, `canyonview→forest3`), the maze diodes and the dead end, `instream→streamview`, the dome drop to the torch room, the coffin cure (`southtemple→tinycave`), the **slide** (`slideroom→cellar`) and the whole **river current**. That list is now a hard assertion, so any future drift fails the build.
  - **Return connectivity:** every one of the 110 rooms can find a route **back** to West of House (strongly connected via the graph), so nothing can strand the player.
  - Two real fixes fell out of the audit: the maze‑diode destinations in the data still held the old placeholders (`maze7→maze15`, `maze9→maze5`, `maze12→maze13`) even though the runtime was overriding them — the table now says `deadend1` / `maze11` / `maze5`; and `ashore` was accepted by `go`/`walk` but not as a bare command.
  - Tests: **205 checks, all passing** (15 consecutive clean runs). Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
122. **ZorkPad — diagonal mirror audit (your NW/SE report) + four reverse diagonals added** — audited all **41 diagonal exits** for NW↔SE / NE↔SW symmetry. Findings:
  - **The house area already mirrored perfectly** (`westofhouse ne↔northofhouse sw`, `se↔southofhouse nw`, `northofhouse se↔eastofhouse nw`, `southofhouse ne↔eastofhouse sw`), so the surface was fine.
  - **17 diagonals did not mirror.** Eleven belong to the **maze and the mine**, where asymmetry is the design (*"a maze of twisty little passages, all different"*), and two are self‑loops that exist **in Infocom's own source** — `MAZE-9 (NW TO MAZE-9)` and `MAZE-14 (NW TO MAZE-14)`. Left authentic.
  - **Four were ordinary geography with no way back**, exactly the case you hit — now given their reverse diagonal: `clearing.se→canyonview`, `ewpassage.ne→chasmroom`, `onrainbow.sw→endofrainbow`, `southofhouse.se→forest3`. Each is marked `// mirrored …` in `ZorkWorld.cs` so it can be reverted at a glance. (**Deliberate deviation from the ZIL**, made because a one‑way diagonal in normal terrain reads as a bug.)
  - `endofrainbow.sw→canyonbottom` is genuinely one‑directional in the source and returns by **north** (*"To the north is a narrow path"*) — verified authentic, not changed.
  - New tests: **8 diagonal round trips** (both house squares and all four new mirrors) must return you to the room you left; and "no diagonal strands you" — any diagonal that doesn't mirror must still offer *some* way back, unless it is maze/mine/dead‑end territory. Total exits now **319, all traversing correctly**; the 23 authentic one‑ways are unchanged.
  - Tests: **207 checks, all passing** (15 consecutive clean runs). Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
123. **`ResourceInfo.cs` bug check — four scale materials were mislabelled (fixed)** — audited all **173 material rows** across the nine tables against the `CraftResource` enum, because `GetIndex()` is arithmetic (`resource - GetStart(type)`) and indexes straight into the array, so order and labelling are load-bearing. Method and results:
  - **Real bug found and fixed:** the last four rows of `m_ScaleInfo` — **Gorn, Trandoshan, Silurian and Krayt** — all carried `CraftResource.CadalyteScales` (copy‑pasted from the row above); their names, hues, `CraftAttributeInfo` and `typeof(...)` were correct. Consequences: `CraftResources.GetFromType( typeof( GornScales ) )` (and the other three) returned **CadalyteScales**, so crafted items took the **Cadalyte** modifiers (`WeaponDamage +5`, `WeaponSpeed +5`) and the `case CraftResource.GornScales:` / `TrandoshanScales` / `SilurianScales` / `KraytScales` branches of `ResourceMods` could never fire; `GetRandomNonBasicResource( Scales )` could also hand out Cadalyte repeatedly. Each row now names its own resource.
  - **Verified clean**: all nine tables are in **exact enum order** (no shifted rows); **complete coverage** — 173 materials, every enum member mapped exactly once, no gaps and nothing outside its family range; **no duplicate resources**; **no duplicate concrete types** (243 type registrations, all distinct); every row's `CraftAttributeInfo` matches its own material (no cross‑family mixups); every row's primary `typeof()` matches its material.
  - **Reviewed and deliberately left alone** (not bugs): cross‑family display‑name duplicates (*Dinosaur* scale vs leather, *Normal* leather/wood/fabric, *Troll* skin vs skeletal — menus are per family); `CraftAttributeInfo.Blank` on the five base tiers (Iron, RegularLeather, RegularWood, Fabric, BrittleSkeletal); `RegularLeather` using class `Leather` and `RegularWood` using `Board`; and eight non‑monotonic skill requirements (Agrinium 117 < 125, FireSpec 80, TurtleSpec 110, Adesote 110, Rosewood 100, BorlTree 115, AndorianSkeletal 100.1, Gorn 110) which read as balance choices.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
124. **`ResourceInfo.cs` — all 646 disabled AoS material bonuses re‑enabled (backup taken first)** — the material modifier switch `GetAosMods()` (line 1013) had 646 `AosAttribute_*` / `AosWeaponAttribute_*` / `AosArmorAttribute_*` assignments commented out. They are now active.
  - **Backup:** `Backups/ResourceInfo.cs.2026-11-11-170739.bak` (150,366 bytes, taken before the edit). Restore with `cp Backups/ResourceInfo.cs.2026-11-11-170739.bak World/Source/Scripts/System/Misc/ResourceInfo.cs`.
  - **Pre‑flight:** checked every `new ResourceModInfo { … }` initializer for duplicate property assignments once uncommented — **0 found**, so the uncomment could not produce a "duplicate member" compile error.
  - **Change:** 646 lines uncommented (`^\s*//\s*Aos…` → assignment), 0 commented AoS lines remaining; active AoS assignments went **84 → 730**.
  - **Effect:** materials granting AoS bonuses go from **22 to 119** of 131. The 12 without are the five intentional base tiers (`Iron`, `RegularLeather`, `RegularWood`, `Fabric`, `BrittleSkeletal` — empty `ResourceModInfo()`) and seven that never had AoS lines at all (`GildedSpec`, `DriftwoodTree`, `GargoyleSkeletal`, `MinotaurSkeletal`, `LycanSkeletal`, `SharkSkeletal`, `ColossalSkeletal`).
  - Heaviest newly‑active blocks: `ExodusSpec` 19 lines (Regen 1/1/1, Defend/Attack +5, +2 Str/+1 Dex/+1 Int, +4 Hits/+3 Stam, WeaponDamage 9, WeaponSpeed 5, NightSight, SelfRepair 3, HitLightning 20, HitDispel 20, HitEnergyArea 25, MageArmor), `DivineFabric` 16, `MysteriousFabric` 15, `OnyxBlock` 15, `FiendishFabric` 13, `ElvenTree` 12.
  - **Balance warning:** this is a large power increase on high‑end materials (e.g. ExodusSpec now carries 25 bonuses). No values were altered — only the pre‑authored ones switched back on.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
125. **Transmogrification — stage 1 (appearance picker, catalogue, single‑use scroll)** — three new files in `World/Source/Scripts/Systems/Transmogrify/`: `TransmogrifyCatalog.cs`, `TransmogrifyGump.cs`, `TransmogrifyTestItem.cs`. A player uses a `Transmogrification Scroll` on a piece of armour or clothing and picks a new appearance from the items that occupy the same slot; only `ItemID` changes (no stats, no hue, no durability).
  - **Catalogue** (`TransmogrifyCatalog`, static, lazily built and cached): instantiates every `[Constructable]` `BaseArmor`/`BaseClothing`/`BaseWeapon`/`BaseTrinket` class, groups by the *instance's* `Layer` (armour takes its layer from tiledata, clothing from its constructor argument — so instantiating is the only reliable source), keeps one entry per slot per `ItemID`, and sorts by name. Exposes `OptionsFor`, `CountFor`, `TotalCount`, `SlotCount`, `Rebuild`; prints one `[Transmogrify] appearance catalog: N entries across M slots` line to the console on first build.
  - **Two bugs found while wiring it up, both fixed:** (a) the scan used `typeof( Item ).Assembly`, which is the **core** assembly (`WorldLinux.exe`) and contains none of the item classes, so it found nothing — it now scans `Core.Assembly` + `ScriptCompiler.Assemblies` + its own assembly (deduped, `ReflectionTypeLoadException`‑safe), the same set `GenCategorization` uses; (b) it tested `[Constructable]` on the **type**, but this shard marks the **parameterless constructor** (`[Constructable] public Foo()`), so the test is now `t.GetConstructor( Type.EmptyTypes ).IsDefined( … )` and instantiation goes through that constructor. Between them the catalogue was empty and every target answered "there is nothing to change that into".
  - **Gump** (`TransmogrifyGump`): 0x1453 frame with a title bar; a top box shows the item's current art and name plus `slot: X – N appearances`; below is a 2×4 grid of options, each with its art, bold name and a grey `from <ClassName>`, with Back/Next paging (8 per page, `n / p`) and Cancel. Picking an option sets `ItemID` only and plays sound `0x57`.
  - **Scroll** (`TransmogrifyTestItem`, "Transmogrification Scroll", art `0x1F4C`, hue `0x48E`, weight 1, blessed): double‑click opens a target cursor; accepts `BaseArmor`/`BaseClothing` within 4 tiles and opens the picker, refusing everything else without being consumed. **Single use** — the scroll is spent when an appearance is *chosen*, not when the target is picked, so closing the gump or a bad target keeps it.
  - Verified offline that the corrected scan finds **1,342** constructable wearable classes (551 armour, 311 clothing, 379 weapons, 101 trinkets).
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
126. **Transmogrification — base item names + paperdoll preview** — options are now named after the **base item** (the art), not the class or the artifact that supplies it: a leather gorget shows as "Leather Gorget", a stuffed one as "Stuffed Gorget", a plate mempo as "Plate Mempo". The "from <ClassName>" line is gone.
  - **`TransmogrifyCatalog.BaseName( Item )`**: reads `TileData.ItemTable[ItemID & TileData.MaxItemValue].Name` and title‑cases it ("leather gorget" → "Leather Gorget"), falling back to the class name split on capitals only when tiledata has no name for the art. Used for both the option list and the item shown at the top of the gump.
  - **New `TransmogrifyPreview.cs`** — a paperdoll model: a `Mobile` created from `Serial.NewMobile` that is **never added to the world**, so it is never saved and no other client ever receives it. It copies the user's `BodyValue`, `Female`, `Hue`, hair and facial hair, clones every worn item *except* the target slot, and puts the previewed appearance on that slot. It reaches only that player, as a `MobileIncoming` (0x78) packet, and is shown with `DisplayPaperdoll` (0x88). The UO client cannot draw a paperdoll *inside* a gump, so the model appears in its own paperdoll window, which the player can park next to the gump.
  - **Live updates:** selecting an option sends `RemoveItem` + `EquipUpdate` for the previewed slot only, so the open paperdoll re‑dresses without being reopened.
  - **Two‑step pick:** clicking an option now only *selects* and previews it (cell highlighted, name in yellow, art shown in the top box); a new **Apply** button commits the change, plays 0x57, spends the scroll and closes. Closing/Cancel, a bad target or "Apply" with nothing selected all leave the scroll intact. A **Doll** button reopens the paperdoll, and a 5‑minute expiry timer dismisses a model the player never answers for.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
127. **Fixed: the transmogrify scroll crashed the shard when used** — `TransmogrifyPreview` was built with the `Mobile( Serial )` constructor, which is the *deserialisation* constructor: it deliberately does **not** call `DefaultMobileInit()`, so `Items`, `Skills`, `Stabled`, etc. are all left `null`. The first thing `Dress()` did was read `Items.Count`, so every use threw a `NullReferenceException` inside the targeting callback and the shard died. It also failed the startup serialisation check in `Main.VerifyType` (`- No serialization constructor` / `- No Serialize()` / `- No Deserialize()`).
  - **Fix:** the model is now built with the public `Mobile()` constructor, which runs `DefaultMobileInit()` (creating `Items`, `Skills`, `Stabled`, `Map = Map.Internal`, …), and is then immediately taken back out of the world with `World.RemoveMobile( this )` — fully formed, but never part of `World.Mobiles`, so it is never saved and never becomes a real entity. `Mobile.Delta()` also early‑outs on `Map.Internal`, so it generates no traffic of its own.
  - **Serialisation members added** (the shard requires them for every `Item`/`Mobile` subclass): `public TransmogrifyPreview( Serial serial ) : base( serial )`, `Serialize` (base + version 0) and `Deserialize` (base + version, then self‑delete via `Timer.DelayCall`) so a model caught by a save during the instant it exists cannot persist.
  - **Defence in depth:** every preview call in the gump now runs through `try`/`catch` (`DismissPreview`, `ShowPreviewPaperdoll`, and the constructor's block). A preview failure logs `[Transmogrify] preview ... failed: <exception>` to the console and simply leaves `m_Preview == null` — the picker keeps working with no doll rather than taking the shard down.
  - **Verified offline** with the same reflection checks `Main.VerifyType` performs: serialization constructor ✓, declared `Serialize()` ✓, declared `Deserialize()` ✓, `Mobile` subclass ✓.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
128. **Transmogrification — in‑gump outfit doll replaces the paperdoll window** — the temporary `Mobile`/paperdoll‑window approach is **removed** (`TransmogrifyPreview.cs` deleted); the picker now draws the player's outfit directly in the gump, on the right, at all times.
  - **Why it is a doll of items and not a painted avatar:** a gump can only draw `gumpart.mul` images (`AddImage`) and *item* art (`AddItem`). The client composes a dressed character only in its own paperdoll window from `MobileIncoming` equipment data — there is no art asset for "a human wearing this gorget", which is also why the shard's race gump uses pre‑rendered per‑species images (`50000 + SpeciesGump`) rather than compositing gear.
  - **What the doll is:** a 204×470 panel on the right with one 44×44 item‑art cell per equipment slot, laid out like a body — helm, cloak/neck/jewellery, torso, arms/gloves/wrist, legs, shoes/waist, weapon. Each cell shows the player's own worn item (its art and hue) via `WornOn( layers )`; a cell may cover several layers (robe/shirt/tunic share the torso, ring/trinket share the jewel slot).
  - **Live preview:** the cell for the slot being changed is lit (`0xA40` + alpha) and always shows the appearance currently selected — the item's own art before a pick, the option's art afterwards — so clicking options re‑draws that slot, and the large art + name at the top‑left follow the same selection. The doll is drawn on page 0, so it stays visible while the option pages are flipped.
  - **Gump geometry:** widened to 620×600; left column holds the item box (16,44,376,116), the slot line, the 2×4 option grid (rows at 212/288/364/440, columns at 20 and 208) and the pager at y 500; Apply/Cancel sit at y 545 and the doll panel at 400,44.
  - **Side effect:** deleting the ghost mobile also removes the `Mobile`/serialisation surface introduced in #126/#127 — no transient mobiles, no serialisation warning, no crash path.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
129. **Transmogrification — paperdoll window reinstated and coupled to the gump** — `TransmogrifyPreview` is back (built with the correct `Mobile()` initialiser from #127, so `Items`/`StatMods` exist and `BodyValue` cannot NRE), and the picker and its paperdoll are now driven as a single unit.
  - **What can and cannot be "locked":** the paperdoll is a *client* window. Packet `0x88` (`DisplayPaperdoll`) carries only a serial, 60 bytes of text and a flags byte — **no coordinates** — so the server cannot position, pin, dock or z‑order it; the client remembers its own paperdoll placement per character. The gump's own position is server‑supplied, but the pair cannot be glued together from the server side.
  - **What was done instead, so they behave as one object:** `Present()` + `ShowPaperdoll()` are re‑issued on **every** gump redraw (first open and every option click), which re‑raises the paperdoll each time the picker is drawn; selecting an option re‑dresses the doll live with `RemoveItem` + `EquipUpdate`; and every exit path — Cancel, closing the gump, Apply, out of range, or the item being deleted — calls `Dismiss()`, which sends `RemoveMobile` so the paperdoll window goes away with the gump. A 10‑minute expiry timer cleans up a preview the player never answers.
  - A **Paperdoll** button (id 901) in the right panel re‑issues the window if the player closed it, and the in‑gump item doll from #128 stays as an at‑a‑glance outfit panel.
  - Verified with the same reflection checks `Main.VerifyType` uses: serialisation ctor ✓, declared `Serialize()` ✓, declared `Deserialize()` ✓, `Mobile` subclass ✓.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
130. **Transmogrification — outfit panel removed, paperdoll opens by default** — the right‑hand `YOUR OUTFIT` icon doll from #128 is gone; the gump is back to the compact 560×600 picker (item box `16,44,528,156`, slot line, 2×4 options at rows 232/308/384/460, pager at 524, Apply/Cancel at 562) with the paperdoll as the only preview.
  - The paperdoll window is opened **by default** by the constructor (`Present()` + `ShowPaperdoll()`), and re‑issued on every redraw, so it comes up with the picker each time and re‑dresses live as options are selected.
  - A small **Paperdoll** button now sits at the right of the slot line (`386,202` label, `452,198` button, id 901) to re‑open the window if the player closed it; every exit path still dismisses both together (`RemoveMobile`).
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
131. **Fixed: the paperdoll closed again as soon as it was opened** — three things were fighting the window:
  - The **Paperdoll button rebuilt the gump** straight after sending `0x88`. Replacing a gump makes the client re‑issue its response for the old one (button 0), which ran `DismissPreview()` → `RemoveMobile` → the paperdoll vanished. The button now only sends the paperdoll request and leaves the gump alone.
  - `Present()`/`ShowPaperdoll()` were **re‑issued on every redraw**. Re‑announcing the mobile (`0x78`) makes clients discard and re‑create the entity, which takes its paperdoll window with it. The model is now announced and opened **once**, on the first draw; later redraws call `SetFocus()` only (`RemoveItem` + `EquipUpdate`), which is exactly the pattern `MannequinGumps` uses.
  - **`MobileUpdate` (0x20) was being sent** for the model. That packet describes *your own* character, so some clients reacted badly to it carrying a foreign serial. `Present()` now sends only `MobileIncoming` (0x78).
  - The first open is also **deferred by 100 ms** (`ShowPaperdollDeferred`) so the paperdoll request lands after the accompanying gump packet instead of racing it; it bails out if the preview was dismissed in the meantime.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
132. **Corrected #131: the Paperdoll button must rebuild the gump** — in UO a button reply **closes the gump on the client**; the server has to send it again to keep it on screen. #131 removed that rebuild, so pressing **Paperdoll** simply closed the picker.
  - The rebuild is back, but the two actions are now separated: `TransmogrifyPreview` tracks `Presented` (set by `Present()`), so the model is announced with `0x78` **once** per picker session; a rebuild only calls `SetFocus()` (`RemoveItem` + `EquipUpdate`). That was the part that used to destroy the window — not the rebuild itself.
  - The gump gained a `showPaperdoll` constructor argument so each redraw states what it wants: `true` on the first draw and from the **Paperdoll** button (which also re-sends the gump), `false` when an option is selected or when "Choose an appearance first" is shown.
  - Stray close reports are now ignored: each instance carries a generation (`m_Generations`, keyed by player `Serial`), and a **button 0 from a gump that has already been replaced** no longer dismisses the paperdoll.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
133. **Fixed: the paperdoll stopped opening at all** — #131/#132 had also dropped `MobileUpdate` (0x20) and deferred the `0x88` by 100 ms onto the timer thread, and either change stopped the window appearing (it had opened in the build that sent 0x20 + 0x78 + 0x88 synchronously).
  - `Present()` again sends **0x20 then 0x78** back to back: 0x20 is what makes the client register the mobile at all, 0x78 then supplies body, hue and every worn layer.
  - `ShowPaperdoll()` is **synchronous** again — no `Timer.DelayCall` — because NetState traffic belongs on the game thread; `ShowPaperdollDeferred`/`DeferredShow` are removed.
  - Redraw policy is unchanged and still the fix for the window closing: a draw that asks for the paperdoll (first draw, or the **Paperdoll** button) re‑announces the model and opens the window in one burst — so even if the entity is re‑created, the `0x88` that follows re‑opens it. Every other redraw stays completely silent and only calls `SetFocus()`.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
134. **Fixed: the paperdoll opened and closed immediately** — two causes, both about how the client judges the window:
  - **The model had no usable position.** It sat at `(0, 0, 0)` on `Map.Internal`, so the client received a mobile outside its view and dropped the paperdoll as soon as it opened. The model is now given the **player's own coordinates** (`MoveToWorld( owner.Location, Map.Internal )`), then unregistered again with `World.RemoveMobile()`) — the client considers it right next to you, while `Map.Internal` keeps it out of every broadcast and the unregistration keeps it out of the save. It is also set `Hidden` so it does not paint a second copy of the avatar on your tile.
  - **The paperdoll was requested before the gump.** `0x88` went out inside the gump constructor, i.e. before `SendGump`'s `0xDD`. Some clients open the window and close it again when the gump arrives. Callers now do it in the right order: build the gump → `SendGump` → `gump.OpenPaperdoll( from )` (new public method). Same for the **Paperdoll** button, through `Reopen(..., true)`.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
135. **Transmogrification — picker anchored by its right edge** — the gump no longer opens at `( 60, 40 )`; it is pinned to a constant screen column so it always pairs the same way with the paperdoll window.
  - Four constants at the top of `TransmogrifyGump` drive it: `GumpWidth` 560, `GumpHeight` 600, `AnchorRight` 1010, `AnchorTop` 40. The constructor calls `base( AnchorRight - GumpWidth, AnchorTop )`, so the gump's **right edge** lands on `AnchorRight` — currently it opens at `( 450, 40 )`, leaving the left half of a 1024‑wide client free for the paperdoll. For an 800‑wide client set `AnchorRight = 790`.
  - `AddBackground` now uses `GumpWidth`/`GumpHeight`, so resizing the frame and moving the anchor stay in one place.
  - Dragging is still allowed: clients remember a server gump's position per character, so nudge it once and it reopens exactly there on later use.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
136. **Transmogrification — the target is worn on the paperdoll from the first frame** — `Dress()` already puts the item's **current** appearance (`target.ItemID`, `target.Hue`) into the slot being changed, so the doll starts out wearing exactly what the player has now; this makes that reliable:
  - New `TransmogrifyPreview.SendEquipment( from )` re‑states the whole outfit as individual `0x2E EquipUpdate` packets immediately after `0x88`, so the paperdoll is dressed the moment it opens even if the client drops equipment from the initial `0x78`. Called from `TransmogrifyGump.OpenPaperdoll`.
  - The preview piece is forced `Visible = true` after `AddItem`, because `AddItem` runs the shard's clothing rules (`ProcessClothing`) — a robe would otherwise hide an inner‑torso preview, and the one layer the player is actually choosing must always be visible.
  - Selecting an option still swaps only that slot (`RemoveItem` + `EquipUpdate`), so the sequence reads: doll wears the item as it is now → click an option, that slot changes → Apply commits.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
137. **Transmogrification — stable preview serial so the paperdoll reopens where it was parked** — the server still cannot position the paperdoll (`0x88` is serial + 60‑byte caption + flags, no coordinates), but most clients remember a paperdoll's **position per serial**, and every preview used to get a brand‑new serial, so it always opened at the client's default.
  - `TransmogrifyPreview` now keeps **one serial per player** (`m_PreviewSerials`, keyed by the player's serial) taken from `Serial.NewMobile`; because the server never re‑issues a serial, no real mobile can ever collide with it, and the same value is reused for every later preview by that player.
  - Construction switched to `public TransmogrifyPreview( Serial serial, Mobile owner ) : base( serial )` plus an explicit `DefaultMobileInit()` — that is the only way to supply a serial, and the full initialiser is what keeps `Items`/`StatMods` non‑null (the #127 crash).
  - Effect: park the doll once and clients that cache paperdoll positions will reopen it there; on clients that don't cache per serial, behaviour is unchanged (server‑side placement remains impossible).
  - Verified with the shard's own verification checks: serialisation ctor ✓, declared `Serialize()` ✓, declared `Deserialize()` ✓.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
138. **Transmogrification — item hue kept in every preview, and the page no longer snaps back to one**
  - **Hue:** all previews are drawn in the *target item's* hue. The option arts in the list, the big picture at the top and the paperdoll's changed slot all use `item.Hue` (`shownHue`), instead of the catalogue's default (`opt.Hue`, always 0). Applying was already hue‑safe — only `ItemID` changes — so this is purely how the previews render: "this item, in that shape".
  - **Page:** a UO client always opens a gump on page 1 (that is why `BaseImageTileButtonsGump` puts its first content page on `AddPage( 1 )`), so the page being viewed is now emitted **first** — `int start = m_Page; int p = ( start + n ) % pages;` — while the counter keeps logical numbering (`3 / 7`). Selecting an option reopens with `index / PerPage`, which is exactly the page the option was on, so the list stays put.
  - The pager became a carousel (`( ( n ± 1 + pages ) % pages ) + 1`) so both directions always work and no page becomes unreachable when the sequence starts mid‑list.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
139. **Transmogrification — list spacing, and a Gear toggle with a naked‑by‑default paperdoll**
  - **Spacing/alignment:** each option's name moved from `x+86, y+12` to `x+96, y+14`, so it sits further from the arrow and starts on the same line as the button (`x+62, y+14`) instead of half a line above it.
  - **Naked by default:** the preview doll now shows **only the piece being changed** on a bare body. `TransmogrifyPreview.Dress( target, from )` mirrors the player's other worn items only when `m_ShowGear` is set.
  - **Gear button** (id 902, on the slot line beside **Paperdoll**, labelled `Gear on` / `Gear off`, yellow when on): `SetGear( show, target, from )` rebuilds the outfit and pushes the difference as `RemoveItem`/`EquipUpdate` packets, so the paperdoll re‑dresses live and is never closed or re‑opened. Because every reply closes a gump client‑side, the picker itself is re‑sent with the new flag and rebuilt in the same place.
  - `Dress` now takes the target `Mobile` it should notify (null while the model is first built), which is what lets the toggle update an open paperdoll.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
140. **Transmogrification — item names use the client's plain font** — the option names were wrapped in `<B>`, and the bold face is cramped and hard to read at the gump's default size. They are now rendered in the client's default font, with the selected entry distinguished by colour (`#FFFF66` instead of `#FFFFFF`) rather than weight. The title bar and the **Apply** label keep their bold, since those are headings. (The client also supports `<BIG>` — used by `ElementalSpellbookGump` — if a larger face is ever wanted instead.) Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
141. **Transmogrification — Paperdoll button replaced by Revert; original visuals persisted in the Saves folder**
  - **New `TransmogrifyRegistry.cs`** (static, `Initialize()` hooks `EventSink.WorldLoad`/`WorldSave`): a `Dictionary<int, Original>` of **item serial → original ItemID + hue**, persisted to `World/Saves/Transmogrify/Originals.xml` (UTF‑8, indented, `serial="0x…" id="0x…" hue="…"`). Written on every world save, read back on load; rows whose item no longer exists are dropped on load and again on save. File location follows the shard's own `Saves/<System>/` convention (`Saves/Achievements`, `Saves/Craft`, …).
  - `Remember( Item )` is called in **Apply**, *before* the `ItemID` changes, and only records an item the first time — the first transmog is the true original.
  - `Revert( Item )` restores the stored `ItemID` and hue and deletes the row, so a later transmog records the restored state afresh.
  - The **Paperdoll** button (id 901) is gone; the same slot now holds **Revert** (id 903, label `Revert` at `424,202`, button `490,198`). Pressing it restores the original, plays 0x57, dismisses the doll and closes the picker; when nothing is recorded it says "That item has no recorded original appearance." and re‑sends the picker (keeping page, selection and gear state). The scroll is untouched either way — it is only spent on Apply.
  - `Reopen` lost its `showPaperdoll` flag; the paperdoll is now opened only on the first draw (via `OpenPaperdoll` from the scroll), so a redraw never touches the window.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
142. **Transmogrification — gump renamed to “Transmogrify Equipment”** — the title bar now reads `Transmogrify Equipment` (bold, `#FFFF66`) instead of `TRANSMOGNIFICATION`. The class is still `TransmogrifyGump`; only the displayed name changed. Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
143. **Transmogrification — spacing polish and bug pass** (gump geometry audited box by box; 560×600 anchored right)
  - **Header row** (`y = 200`): the slot line is now **left-aligned** at `x 24` (it was centred in a 296px box, so it drifted left of the dialog centre) and both small actions put their label and button on the **same baseline** — labels `326`/`426`, buttons `396`/`490`, all at `y 200` (the buttons used to sit 4px higher than their labels).
  - **Item art** moved `262 → 258`, so 44px art is exactly centred in the box (16..544).
  - **Pager:** the "Next" label was 430px wide with 44px of dead space before its arrow; it is now `462,526,40` (4px gap). Back/counter unchanged (20 / 210..350 / 506).
  - **Apply/Cancel** pair centred on the gump (`194..375`, mid 284) with Cancel first, and **Apply is no longer bold** — consistent with the plain item names, still highlighted yellow when a pick exists.
  - **Bug — revert clobbered dyes:** `Revert` restored the recorded hue as well as the art. Transmogging never changes hue, so the stored hue can only differ if the player dyed the item afterwards; a revert would then have undone their own dye. Only `ItemID` is restored now (the hue stays in the file as a record of the original look).
  - **Bug — non-atomic save:** the originals file was written straight over the live one. It is now written to `Originals.xml.tmp` and swapped in (`File.Copy`/`File.Move`), so a failed save can never leave a truncated database.
  - **Bug — stale art on the doll:** reopening the picker for a *different* item in the *same* slot could reuse the cached preview unchanged (the layer matched, so `For()` skipped the rebuild) and leave the previous item's art on the doll. The gump now computes the art it wants (`option` or `item.ItemID`) and calls `TransmogrifyPreview.IsFocusedOn( target, art )`, syncing only when it actually differs - no redundant `RemoveItem`/`EquipUpdate` pairs.
  - **Audited and found clean:** option ring (art `x`, arrow `x+62`, name `x+96..x+212` inside a 264px pitch), selection highlight (`x-8, y-8, 216×62`) never overlapping the next row (76px pitch), no collisions anywhere on page 0, all rows inside `12..548`, Apply/Cancel at `562..577` clearing the 600px frame; handlers for out-of-range, deleted item, stale-generation `button 0`, the 10‑minute preview expiry, the catalogue cache and the scroll's target validation.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
144. **Transmogrification — the header and pager labels are now right‑aligned against their buttons** — "Gear off"/"Gear on" and "Revert" were left‑aligned in boxes of different widths, so the visual gap between text and arrow was 14px on one and 22px on the other — the "weird spacing".
  - Both labels are now wrapped in `<DIV ALIGN=RIGHT>` and given a box that ends **6px before** its arrow (`316..390` → button `396`; `410..484` → button `490`), so the gap is identical regardless of the text and only changes when it toggles between `Gear off`/`Gear on`.
  - Pager matched to the same rule: **Back** label moved `52 → 41` (6px after its arrow at `20`), **Next** wrapped in `<DIV ALIGN=RIGHT>` in `456..500` (6px before its arrow at `506`), so the pair reads symmetrically around the `n / p` counter.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.
145. **Transmogrification — the real item: `TransmogrifyGem`, dropped by Paragons**
  - New **`TransmogrifyGem : TransmogrifyTool`** — diamond art `0x0F26`, hue `0x48E` (the transmogrify identity colour), weight 0.1, `LightType.Circle150`, `[Constructable]` so it can be `[add`‑ed for testing. Same use as the scroll: double‑click, target armour/clothing, pick an appearance, **Apply**; it is spent on the Apply and the item's original look is recorded so **Revert** works later.
  - New abstract base **`TransmogrifyTool`** now owns the double‑click, range/type validation and picker launch, so the GM scroll and the gem share one code path, and `TransmogrifyTestItem` is a thin subclass (scroll art `0x1F4C`, blessed, using `TransmogrifyGem.GemHue`). The base declares a **public `(Serial)` ctor plus no‑op `Serialize`/`Deserialize`** so `Main.VerifyType` does not warn about an abstract `Item`.
  - **Drop hook:** `TransmogrifyDrops` subscribes to `EventSink.OnKilledBy` — `BaseCreature` is untouched. Chance is `0.25 + luck / 4000`, capped at `0.90`: **25 %** at 0 luck, 50 % at 1000, 75 % at 2000, 90 % from 2600 up. Luck is the **killer's** (`Mobile.Luck`, the stat `Paragon.CheckArtifactChance` already uses) and only counts for Paragons (`bc.IsParagon`).
  - The gem is placed in the **corpse**; with no corpse it falls back to the killer's backpack, then to the ground at their feet. `OnKilledBy` fires once per looter, so the corpse serial is remembered and only the first (top damager) rolls — one kill can never yield more than one gem.
  - Verified with the reflection checks `Main.VerifyType` performs: `TransmogrifyTool`/`Gem`/`TestItem` all have a serialisation ctor plus declared `Serialize`/`Deserialize`; the gump and the two static classes (`Registry`, `Drops`) are outside that check and both statics expose `Initialize`.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, zero errors.

163. **Fragment of Infinity — enchantment-point consumable** — new `Source/Scripts/Items/Misc/FragmentOfInfinity.cs`, no existing file touched.

The tooltip adds the line *"Adds 10 points of enchantment to any artefact"* beneath the name in hue **`71e26c`**, using the existing `ColorText3`/`ColorHue3` OPL mechanism (same as `MagicPigment`).
  - **Use:** double-click while in the backpack → target an artefact you are **wearing or carrying** → adds **10** to its `IGiftable.Points` (`item.InvalidateProperties()` refreshes the tooltip) and consumes the fragment. `IGiftable` is the shard's enchantment-point field, implemented by every `BaseGift*` artefact weapon/armour/shield/jewel/clothing class.
  - **Refusals:** a non-artefact target (or a mobile) answers exactly **"This only works on artefacts."** and the fragment stays in the pack. An artefact outside reach answers "You can only enchant artefacts you are wearing or carrying." and is likewise not consumed.

164. **Fragment of Infinity — 10% boss drop** — added `FragmentOfInfinityDrops` to `Source/Scripts/Items/Misc/FragmentOfInfinity.cs`; no other new files.
  - **Chance:** a flat **10%** per kill (`Utility.RandomDouble() < 0.10`), no Luck scaling. One roll per corpse — `EventSink.OnKilledBy` fires once per looter, so the handler remembers the last corpse serial.
  - **Named bosses (exact types):** `BlackGateDemon`, `BloodDemigod`, `DragonKing`, `OrkDemigod`, `LichKing`, `Dracula`, `Jormungandr` (the shard's spelling of "Jormungand"), `Xurtzar`, `CaddelliteDragon`, `Vulcrum`, `Arachnar`, `Surtaz`, `Mangar`, `Shadowlord`, `Tarjan`, `TitanHydros`, `TitanLithos`, `TitanPyros`, `TitanStratos`, `KhumashGor`, `Satan`, `SlasherOfVoid`, `Exodus`. Exact `GetType()` matching means ordinary creatures of the same base class do not drop it.
  - **All 25 Magical Prison bosses:** matched by `BaseCreature.EmoteHue == 505`, the marker `SummonPrison` stamps on every escapee when it releases one (`SummonPrison.cs:248`). This covers every pick from Tiamat through Lolth without matching their ordinary base creatures.
  - **Corpse drops:** normal bosses use `corpse.DropItem( new FragmentOfInfinity() )` through `EventSink.OnKilledBy`.
  - **Corpse-less bosses:** `BlackGateDemon` and `Mangar` both set `DeleteCorpseOnDeath`. BlackGateDemon's roll goes into the `demonic chest` created in its `OnBeforeDeath`; Mangar's roll goes into the `LootChest` built when his `BardMangarPack` vault is first opened. The event handler skips any `DeleteCorpseOnDeath` creature so neither can roll twice.

Existing skill codes (`99`, `100`, `1`-`55`) are unchanged.

166. **"(boss)" name tag on every boss** — the 23 named bosses and all 25 Magical Prison bosses now show **"(boss)"** as the last part of their displayed name (single-click label and tooltip), e.g. **"Satan the devil lord (boss)"**.
  - **Mechanism:** `BaseCreature.ApplyNameSuffix` appends `(boss)` the same way it already appends `(cursed)` for paragons, and does so after the paragon tag, so `(boss)` is always last. Both `Mobile.OnSingleClick` and `Mobile.AddNameProperties` (the OPL tooltip) already call `ApplyNameSuffix`, so no per-mob or per-click code was needed.
  - **Registry:** the 23-type list and the `EmoteHue == 505` prison-boss rule moved out of `FragmentOfInfinityDrops` into `MobileUtilities.IsBoss( BaseCreature )`; the drop rule now calls that method, so names and drops share one list (`Jormungandr` is the shard's spelling of "Jormungand").
  - **No save migration:** the tag is computed at display time, so bosses already in `World/Saves` and prison bosses already stamped with `EmoteHue 505` (serialized) are tagged immediately after a restart. `Name`/`Title` stay untouched, so `DragonLamp`/`DragonPedStatue` names from `Dragons.DropSpecial`, paragon chests, `ContainerOwner` strings and server logs remain clean.
  - **Title-less / title-hidden bosses:** `Exodus` has no title and `Dracula`, `Mangar` and `Shadowlord` set `ClickTitle = false`; their single-click label is just the name plus the tag ("Dracula (boss)"), while the tooltip keeps the full title ("Dracula the master vampire (boss)"). A paragon boss reads "… (cursed) (boss)".
  - **Not affected:** ordinary creatures of the same base types (e.g. a normal `AncientWyrm`), the `SummonPrison` item itself, and the 25 prison names before release.
  - Compile: core csc **zero errors**; scripts csc over `Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts` against `World.exe` — 11 warnings, **zero errors**.
167. **Material-rarity offset fix — leather, cloth, wood and bone loot can finally vary** — one method in `Source/Scripts/System/Misc/ResourceMods.cs` (`RarityTest`); no other file touched. Backed up as `Backups/ResourceMods.cs.2026-11-15-135748.bak`.
  - **The bug:** `RarityTest` computed `rare = Rarity( resource ) + xtra`, but `Rarity()` only ever returns **1-10** while each material family begins at its own index in `SetRandomResource`'s switch — leather `min = 16`, fabric `27`, wood `52`, skeletal `92` (metal alone is `min = 1`). Every result was clamped back *up* to `min`, so looted leather armour was **always** `RegularLeather` (cloth always `Fabric`, wood always `RegularWood`, bone always `BrittleSkeletal`); the only escape was the `Land.Savaged` / haunted-region override, ~0.009 % of eligible items.
  - **Fix 1 — the offset:** `int rare = min + Rarity( resource ) - 1;` so tier 1 of the 1024-curve lands on the family's **base** material and tier 10 on its rarest. For metals `min = 1`, so this is a **no-op**: the standing iron 69.3 / dull copper 19.3 / shadow iron 6.8 / copper 2.7 / bronze 1.1 / gold 0.46 / agapite 0.20 / verite 0.10 / valorite 0.045 split is unchanged, and `nonStandard` (metal `min = 2`) now simply starts at dull copper as intended.
  - **Fix 2 — the unreachable top entry:** `xtra` was `max - min - 9`, rolled as `Utility.Random( xtra )` (0 … xtra-1), so the **last** material of a wide family could never be selected. It is now `( max - min + 1 ) - 10`, rolled with `Utility.Random( xtra + 1 )` when positive, making `FiendishFabric` (38), `WalnutTree` (62) and `DracoSkeletal` (108) reachable; families of ten or fewer materials get `xtra = 0` exactly as before, and a negative range can no longer reach `Utility.Random`, which throws on a negative count.
  - **New spreads** (share of material rolls that pass, measured by invoking the compiled `RarityTest` out of the built `MementoScripts.dll` 300,000 times per family): **leather** Regular 69.4 %, Horned 19.3, Barbed 6.8, Necrotic 2.7, Volcanic 1.1, Frozen 0.46, Goliath 0.20, Draconic 0.09, Hellish 0.05. **fabric** Fabric 45.1, Furry 28.5, Wooly 15.9, Silk 6.1, Haunted 2.5, Arctic 1.1, Pyre 0.47, Venomous 0.21, Mysterious 0.09, Vile 0.05, Divine 0.016, Fiendish 0.003. **wood** Regular 54.0, Ash 28.9, Cherry 10.2, Ebony 4.0, Golden Oak 1.6, Hickory 0.71, Mahogany 0.29, Oak 0.14, Pine 0.07, Rosewood 0.03, Walnut 0.007. **skeletal** Brittle 26.6, Drow 20.3, Orc 15.7, Reptile 12.0, Ogre 9.0, Troll 6.6, Gargoyle 4.6, Minotaur 2.8, Lycan 1.3, Shark 0.57, Colossal 0.25, Mystical 0.12, Vampire 0.05, Lich 0.03, Sphinx 0.011, Devil 0.004, Draco 0.001.
  - **Nothing else moves:** `RarityTest` has exactly **one** caller (`SetRandomResource`), and its `rarity == false` short-circuit still returns `max` untouched, so the ungated scales / blocks / skins spreads (39-50, 67-81, 82-91) are unchanged. The 25 % `uncommon` bail-out in `SetRandomResource` and the `level/13` gate in `LootPackChange.ChangeItem` are untouched — the odds of *getting* a material roll are unchanged, only *which* material can now vary. Forty-odd other `SetRandomResource( …, true, … )` sites inherit the correction (citizen gear, `Tradesman{Leather,Logger,Miner,Smith}`, `BaseRunicTool`, `TransmutationPotion`, `BoneGolem`/`BoneDemon`/`BoneSlasher`/`GiantSkeleton` armour), so bone constructs now appear in the full skeletal palette instead of always `BrittleSkeletal`.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, **zero errors**.
168. **Materials on loot are now more frequent and skew rarer — two new settings** — `Info/Scripts/Settings.cs` (two settings) plus two lines in `System/Misc/ResourceMods.cs` (`RarityTest`, `SetRandomResource`). Backed up as `Backups/ResourceMods.cs.2026-11-15-135748.bak` and `Backups/Settings.cs.2026-11-15-150856.bak`.
  - **`MySettings.S_LootMaterialChance` (new, default `50`)** — replaces the hard-coded `if ( uncommon && Utility.Random( 4 ) > 0 ) return;` with `if ( uncommon && Utility.Random( 100 ) >= MySettings.S_LootMaterialChance ) return;`. This is the gate that decides whether an item which already passed the creature/chest level check actually takes a material; the old roll passed **25 %** of the time, `25` reproduces it exactly, `100` materialles every eligible item, `0` disables materials.
  - **`MySettings.S_LootMaterialBoost` (new, default `1`)** — `RarityTest` now adds this to the rolled tier (`int rare = min + Rarity( resource ) - 1 + MySettings.S_LootMaterialBoost;`) before the family clamp, shifting the mix toward the rarer end. `0` = the old mix, `1` ≈ base and second level, `2` ≈ first three level. It does not disturb the base-material rule or the `Land.*` overrides.
  - **Measured spread at the shipped defaults** (300,000 rolls per family against the built `MementoScripts.dll`): **metal** Iron 38.7, Dull Copper 38.7, Shadow Iron 13.6, Copper 5.3, Bronze 2.1, Gold 0.91, Agapite 0.39, Verite 0.18, Valorite 0.09. **leather** Regular 38.4, Horned 38.8, Barbed 13.7, Necrotic 5.3, Volcanic 2.2, Frozen 0.91, Goliath 0.42, Draconic 0.18, Hellish 0.08. (Before this entry: base 69 %, second 19 %, third 6.8 %.)
  - **Net effect per eligible item** (level gate `level/13`, then the chance roll): L3 **7.1 %** non-base (was 1.8 %), L7 **16.5 %** (was 4.1 %), L10 **23.6 %** (was 5.9 %) — roughly **4× more** non-base materials. The very top of each family stays rare: valorite 0.006 % → 0.025 % at L7, hellish leather 0.006 % → 0.023 %.
  - **Tuning table** used to pick the defaults (chance %, boost): `25/0` = exact pre-change behaviour; `50/0` = 2× the rolls, mix unchanged; `75/0` = 3×; `50/1` = **shipped**; `75/1` = L7 non-base 24.8 %; `100/1` = 33.1 %; `50/2` = base/second/third level at ~27 % each, L7 non-base 19.5 %. Raising `S_LootMaterialChance` moves the whole family up; raising `S_LootMaterialBoost` flattens the base material's dominance without making valorite common.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, **zero errors**.

  - **Effect:** three in four eligible items now take a material roll instead of one in two. Per eligible loot item, non-base materials move L3 7.1 % → **10.6 %**, L7 16.5 % → **24.8 %**, L10 23.6 % → **35.5 %** — about 1.5× the #168 rate and roughly **6× the original** behaviour (L7 was 4.1 % before #167/#168).

170. **Higher-end materials made more likely — `ResourceMods.Rarity()` curve flattened** — one method in `Source/Scripts/System/Misc/ResourceMods.cs`; no settings or new files. Backed up as `Backups/ResourceMods.cs.2026-11-15-135748.bak` (pre-#167 copy of the file).
  - **The curve was a 1024-halving ladder:** tier 1 (base material) got 512/1024 and the top four tiers shared only **1.56 %** between them (tier 10 and 9 just 0.2 % each). That is why gold and above stayed near-mythical no matter how the other knobs moved.
  - **New buckets (out of 1024):** `12 / 12 / 16 / 24 / 36 / 48 / 68 / 128 / 240 / 440` for tiers 10→1, i.e. **43.0 / 23.4 / 12.5 / 6.6 / 4.7 / 3.5 / 2.3 / 1.6 / 1.2 / 1.2 %**. Measured live: t1 42.9, t2 23.5, t3 12.5, t4 6.7, t5 4.7, t6 3.5, t7 2.35, t8 1.58, t9 1.19, t10 1.17.
  - **Effect per material roll** (settings unchanged at `75 / 1`): Iron 38.6 → **35.8** (0.9×), Dull Copper 38.6 → 35.7, Shadow Iron 13.7 → 14.3, Copper 5.4 → 6.5 (1.2×), Bronze 2.2 → **3.4** (1.5×), Gold 0.96 → **2.0** (2.1×), Agapite 0.40 → **1.23** (3.1×), Verite 0.20 → **0.73** (3.6×), Valorite 0.09 → **0.44** (4.9×). Leather mirrors it exactly (Hellish 0.09 → 0.43, Draconic 0.19 → 0.74, Goliath 0.39 → 1.24).

  - **Side effects:** the `Land.*` specials are gated on the same curve (`rare > 6` / `rare > 7` / `rare > 8`), so Nepturite, Obsidian, Steel, Brass, Mithril, Dinosaur and Necrotic leather become roughly 4× more likely too — consistent with the intent. Families that ignore rarity (scales / blocks / skins) are untouched.

173. **Fixed the heat-4 property bonus being swallowed — deep dungeons can no longer give worse loot than shallow ones** — one switch in `LootPack.Mutate` (`Source/Scripts/Items/Containers/LootPack.cs`); nothing else touched.
  - **Correction to what I reported first:** the extra-property tier is *not* "usually wasted" on rich packs. Measured against `GetBonusProperties`' weight tables, `++bonusProps` **improves 90 % of rolls** on `maxProps = 5` packs and is swallowed only on the top 10 %; on the weakest packs it is swallowed **100 %** of the time (`maxProps = 1`, 9 entries; `maxProps = 0`, 2 entries). The inversion runs the opposite way to the first description.
  - **The actual bug:** when the bonus was swallowed, heat 4 handed out *worse* loot than heat 3. `props = 1 + bonusProps` is clamped to `m_MaxProps` ("never 6 properties"), so tier 7 could add nothing at all, while tier 6 in a shallower region adds `min += 20`. For `maxProps 0/1` packs that meant `0p/1p @ 40-70` in the hardest regions versus `@ 60-70` one heat step down.
  - **Fix:** cases 7 and 8 grant the property only while there is room under the pack ceiling and fall back to intensity otherwise — `case 7: if ( 1 + bonusProps < m_MaxProps ){ ++bonusProps; } else { min += 20; max += 15; }`, and `case 8` uses `min += 30; max += 15` so the two tiers remain distinct instead of being identical.
  - **Proved non-regressive:** the property-count distribution is identical to before for every `maxProps` (0/1/2/3/4/5) — only the swallowed cases change, and they now gain intensity: `maxProps 0/1` → `60-85` (was `40-70`); `maxProps 2` → 40 % of rolls also `60-85`; `maxProps 3` → +20 %; `maxProps 5` → +10 %. The `m_MaxProps` ceiling and the "never 6 properties" rule are untouched.
  - **Not changed:** cases 9 and 10 still never fire for creature loot — an elite tops out at `level/2 = 3` plus heat 4 = normalized 7, so only fixed drops and level-12 chests (which pass heat 0 → 6) could ever reach them.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, **zero errors**.
174. **Monster loot level opened up — `LootPackChange.MonsterLevel` divisor `/10` → `/7` (option A)** — one method in `Source/Scripts/Items/Containers/LootPack.cs`; nothing else touched.
  - **Why:** every creature with fame ≥ 15,000, |karma| ≥ 15,000 and 250+ total stats lands on creature level ~74 regardless of how strong it is (all three terms are capped at 15,000 in `GetCreatureLevel`), so `/10` gave every elite the same loot level 7 and left 8-12 unreachable — level 12 would need a 70,286 stat sum against a 60,000 hard ceiling.
  - **New mapping** (verified by invoking the compiled `MonsterLevel`, not by inspection): creature 1-20 → 1-2 (unchanged), 35 → 5 (was 3), 50 → 7 (was 5), **74 → 10 (was 7)**, 77 → 11, 84+ → 12.
  - **Material effect:** the `level/13` gate on an elite goes 53.8 % → **76.9 %**, so with the standing settings (`S_LootMaterialChance = 75`, `S_LootMaterialBoost = 1`, the #172 curve) non-base material per eligible item goes **27.6 % → 39.4 %**; a mid-tier creature (35) goes 11.8 % → 19.7 %; weak creatures are untouched.
  - **Item-quality effect:** `NormalizeLevel = heat + level/2` now gives elites `heat + 5` instead of `heat + 3` — heat 0 `min += 15` (was `max += 15`), heat 1 `min += 20` (was `min += 10`), heat 2 a bonus property (was `min += 15`), heat 3 a bonus property with the #173 fallback (was `min += 20`), heat 4 **`min = 70`** (was a property). Switch cases 8 and 9 now actually fire for creature loot.
  - **Fixed drops:** `MakeFixedDrop` clamps its level to 1-10, so elites move from attribute tier 7 to the top tier 10.
  - **Side effect to watch:** `LootPackChange.RemoveItem` discards skin/block/scales items and pearl trinkets when `Utility.RandomMinMax(3, 12) <= level`, so elites now throw away **80 %** of such items instead of 50 %. Left as-is (it is level-scaled by design); easy to retune if scaled loot thins out too much.
  - Compile: core 7 warnings, scripts 14, **zero errors**.
175. **Fragment of Infinity now works on leveling artefacts too** — one method in `Source/Scripts/Items/Misc/FragmentOfInfinity.cs`; nothing else touched.
  - **Cause:** the target check only accepted `IGiftable`. Legendary leveling items implement `ILevelable` instead, and its `Points` property is the same spendable pool used by `ItemExperienceGump` ("Spending Points(sp) Avail"). They therefore fell through to the "This only works on artefacts." refusal and the fragment was not consumed.
  - **Fix:** the acceptance check now allows either `IGiftable` or `ILevelable`. The +10 is applied to `IGiftable.Points` for gift artefacts and to `ILevelable.Points` for legendary leveling artefacts. Items with neither pool still get the same refusal and keep the fragment; reachability and consumption behaviour are unchanged.
  - **No double-application:** the two interfaces remain disjoint (no item implements both), so the `if / else if` adds exactly 10 once.
  - Compile: csc 4.0 over `Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts` against `World.exe` — 11 warnings, **zero errors**.
176. **"Shroud of Shadows" is now the Hooded Shroud of Shadows** — one file, `Source/Scripts/Items/Magical/Artifacts/Clothing/Outer/Artifact_HoodedShroudOfShadows.cs`; nothing else touched.
  - **Displayed name** changed from "Shroud of Shadows" to **"Hooded Shroud of Shadows"** — the same name `ArtifactQuestList` already printed for this artefact, so the item and the artefact list now agree.
  - **Base item type** changed from the plain robe art `0x2B69` to the hooded shroud art `0x2684`, exactly the base `GiftHoodedShroudOfShadows` and `LevelHoodedShroudOfShadows` use. The class now carries `[Flipable( 0x2684, 0x2683 )]` like those two, so `[flip` still folds the hood back. `Item.isShroud` already lists `0x2684`, so the hood takes part in the normal shroud clothing flow (helm/hair hidden, `0x6474` placeholder when hidden).
  - **Stats untouched:** Hiding +50, Stealth +50, `ArtifactLevel.StandardArtefact`, `Arty.ArtySetup( this, 10, "" )`, hue `0x455`, `GiftRobe` weight 3.0, dyeable, normal loot type — all exactly as before.
  - **Existing copies migrate on load:** serialisation version 1 → 2. A copy still on the plain-robe art is moved to `0x2684` (with its `GraphicID`), a copy hidden behind the clothing placeholder (`Item.doMod`, `0x6474`) only has its stored `GraphicID` moved so the placeholder restores to the new art, a transmogrified copy keeps the art its owner picked, and the old default name is replaced. New items are written as version 2 and skip the migration.
  - Compile: csc 4.0 over `Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts` against `World.exe` — 11 warnings, **zero errors** (same 11 pre-existing warnings as #175).
177. **Vendor value on item tooltips, scaled by the viewer's Mercantile** — new `Source/Scripts/System/Misc/VendorValue.cs`; no existing source file touched.
  - **What it does:** every item tooltip now ends with a gold line — `Vendor value: 1,234 gold`, or `Vendor value: 1,234 gold (41 each)` on a stack — showing what a merchant would pay the viewer for that item. It is drawn with cliloc `1072172` in hue `E9C46A`, the same coloured-line mechanism the shard already uses for guild tags.
  - **Formula:** exactly the vendor sell path, `GenericSellInfo.GetSellPriceFor( item, barter )`, called on a single global sell table built the way the merchants build theirs: `ItemInformation.GetBuysList( null, info, Category.All, Material.All, Market.All, World.None, null, force: true )`. `force: true` skips the random 60–100 % spawn fluctuation, so the displayed figure equals a **guild merchant's** deterministic price; an ordinary merchant pays 60–100 % of it.
  - **Mercantile:** `barter` is the viewer's `SkillName.Mercantile` value, capped at 100, feeding the existing `1 + barter × 0.01` multiplier (up to 2×) inside `GetSellPriceFor`. Items whose price floors out at 1 gold (the shard's tables halve the `ItemSalesInfo` price twice before barter) stay at 1 gold — e.g. an iron ingot pays 1 gold at 0, 50 or 100 Mercantile, exactly as a real vendor does.
  - **What is refused (no line at all):** mirrors `BaseVendor.TryGetSellState` — types missing from the sell table, non-standard loot (`Blessed`, `Cursed`, insured or blessed-for), immovable items, non-empty containers, runebooks with entries and items inside a locked container all show nothing.
  - **Per-player, not cached:** the shared `Item.PropertyList` has no viewer, so `VendorValue.Initialize()` re-registers the two property-query handlers (`0xD6` batch and `0xBF/0x10` single) and rebuilds the OPL for that player (`GetProperties` + `AppendChildProperties` + value line + `Terminate`). Items without a value fall through to the normal cached `SendPropertiesTo`, so only sellable items skip the cache.
  - **Runtime check (mcs harness against `WorldLinux.exe` + the compiled scripts):** plate chest = **25 / 37 / 50** gold at 0 / 50 / 100 Mercantile; iron ingot ×30 = 30 gold; `Gold`, a non-empty bag, a blessed ingot and an ingot in a locked container all return 0.
  - **Compile:** mcs (Mono 6.14) over `Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts` against `WorldLinux.exe`, `/d:MONO /d:x64 /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0` → **14 warnings, zero errors** (the usual script baseline; the new file adds no warnings).
  - No existing source file was modified, so no backup was needed for this change.
178. **Vendor value tooltip — fixed the handler registration that stopped it reaching the client** — one file, `Source/Scripts/System/Misc/VendorValue.cs`; nothing else touched.
  - **Symptom:** mouse-over tooltips showed the normal item properties but no value line at all.
  - **Cause:** every client 6.0.1.7+ sets `NetState.ContainerGridLines`, and `NetState.GetHandler` then reads `PacketHandlers.Get6017Handler( id )` instead of `GetHandler( id )`. `VendorValue.Initialize()` only re-registered `0xD6` through `PacketHandlers.Register`, which updates `m_Handlers` but **not** `m_6017Handlers` (that table is only auto-filled when a packet ID is first registered). The stock cached `BatchQueryProperties` therefore kept answering tooltip queries.
  - **Fix:** also call `PacketHandlers.Register6017( 0xD6, 0, true, … )`, so both `0xD6` tables point at `VendorValue.OnBatchQueryProperties`. `0xBF/0x10` has no 6017 variant (single extended table) and was already correct.
  - **Verified handler wiring (harness):** before → `0xD6` normal and 6017 both `PacketHandlers.BatchQueryProperties`, `0xBF/0x10` `PacketHandlers.QueryProperties`; after `VendorValue.Initialize()` → both `0xD6` tables `VendorValue.OnBatchQueryProperties` and `0xBF/0x10` `VendorValue.OnQueryProperties`; other registrations untouched.
  - **Verified packet (harness):** the per-player `ObjectPropertyList` for a plate chest at 100 Mercantile is 209 bytes; the `E9C46A\tVendor value: 50 gold` entry (cliloc `1072172`, 56-byte argument string) is followed only by the 4-byte terminator, i.e. it really is the **last** tooltip entry, while the cached shared `Item.PropertyList` still contains no value line.
  - **To pick up the fix:** restart the server so the scripts recompile (no `World.exe` rebuild needed).
  - Compile: mcs (Mono 6.14) over `Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts` against `WorldLinux.exe` → **14 warnings, zero errors** (unchanged baseline).
179. **Vendor value is hidden until the item is identified** — one file, `Source/Scripts/System/Misc/VendorValue.cs`; nothing else touched.
  - **Cause:** relic loot (cloth bolts, statues, clocks, the "unknown item" wrapper, and anything else carrying `CoinPrice` + `NotIdentified`) leaked a value through the tooltip. `ItemInformation.AddUpBenefits` deliberately returns a random `2 × RandomMinMax( 5, 25 )` for an unidentified item, so the vendor formula happily priced a bogus 5–25 gold (before barter) even though the shard's ID mechanic hides the real worth.
  - **Fix:** `VendorValue.GetValue` now returns 0 while `item.NotIdentified` is true, so unidentified items show no value line at all. The line appears as soon as the item is identified — the flag is cleared by the vendor ID service or `RelicFunctions.IDItem` (which then shows the relic's own "Worth N Gold" line).
  - **Runtime check (mcs harness):** iron ingot ×5 with `CoinPrice = 100` → **0** unidentified, **500** identified at 0 Mercantile and **1000** at 100 Mercantile; a real `DDRelicCloth` (CoinPrice 350) → **0** unidentified, **350** identified; the `NotIdentified` "unknown item" container → **0**.
  - Compile: mcs (Mono 6.14) over `Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts` against `WorldLinux.exe` → **14 warnings, zero errors** (unchanged baseline). Pre-change file backed up as `Backups/VendorValue.cs.2026-11-21-083142.bak`.
180. **Diablo-style colour tiers on equipment tooltips** — new `Source/Scripts/System/Misc/ItemRank.cs` plus a hook in the core `Source/System/Item.cs`; nothing else touched.
  - **Placement:** `Item.AddNameProperties` now calls `InvokeItemTier( this, list )` immediately after the `ArtifactLevel` switch, only when `ArtifactLevel == None`. That is the exact slot the **Artefact** marker occupies, so a rank reads where an artefact reads, and artefacts are never re-ranked.
  - **Hook:** the core resolves `Server.ItemRank.AppendTier( Item, ObjectPropertyList )` once via `ScriptCompiler.FindTypeByFullName`, caches it as a delegate and guards every call. The core keeps no script reference; a missing script or a throwing rank simply draws no line. Because the hook runs inside the normal `AddNameProperties` pass, the tier lives in the **cached shared** `Item.PropertyList` and costs nothing per player.
  - **Tiers and colours (Diablo names):** Common `C7C7C7`, Magic `6C8CFF`, Rare `FFE066`, Legendary `FF9C40`, Ancient `FF5A5A`, Primal `D46AFF`, drawn with cliloc `1072172`.
  - **Power estimate:** `ItemInformation.AddUpBenefits( item, 0, false, false ) - 1` — the same weights that price loot for vendors, with the base item price removed, so it is pure rolled-property budget (AoS attributes, weapon/armour attributes, skill bonuses, resists, slayer, spell channelling, enchant/gem). Then `+ ( CraftResources.GetGold( Resource ) - 1.0 ) × 30` for material and `× 1.15 / × 0.85` for exceptional / low quality. Tier floors: **Magic 25, Rare 80, Legendary 160, Ancient 280, Primal 450**; anything below is Common.
  - **Not ranked:** non-equipment, any `ArtifactLevel`, any `ArtifactRarity > 0`, `NotIdentified` loot and `CoinPrice` relics.
  - **Runtime checks (mcs harness against the new core + scripts):** plain longsword tooltip → entry[0] name, entry[1] `1072172:C7C7C7\tCommon`; artefact longsword → entry[1] `1070754` (artefact marker) and no tier entry; a manual 10 hits / 15 HCI / 40 DI roll scores exactly **400** (50 + 150 + 200); plain valorite plate scores **60** versus iron **0**.
  - **Calibration sample (250 rolls per cell):** 1 property at 20-40 intensity medians 12 (p90 80); 5 properties at 70-100 medians 344 (p90 594, max 902); 5 properties at 20-40 medians 156. Deep-dungeon rolls therefore reach Ancient/Primal while early loot stays Common/Magic/Rare.
  - Compile: core mcs → **7 warnings, zero errors**; scripts mcs over `Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts` → **14 warnings, zero errors**. `World/WorldLinux.exe` rebuilt. Pre-change core backed up as `Backups/Item.cs.2026-11-21-093036.bak`.
  - **To pick up:** restart the server (core already rebuilt, scripts recompile on start).
181. **Equipment tiers retuned — affix breadth now drives rarity, not gold value** — one file, `Source/Scripts/System/Misc/ItemRank.cs`; the core hook is unchanged.
  - **Symptom:** items shown as Legendary were often weak.
  - **Cause:** `GetTier` bucketed the *gold-value* budget directly, and that budget is dominated by single flags — Slayer `+200`, SpellChannelling `+200`, MageArmor `+200`, SelfRepair `×100` per point, skill bonuses `×12` per point — so a one- or two-property item could reach Legendary/Ancient without carrying a real spread of power.
  - **Fix:** rarity now comes from the **affix count** (distinct non-zero properties across `AosAttributes`, weapon/armour attributes, skill bonuses, resist bonuses and slayers; quivers also count DamageIncrease/LowerAmmoCost/WeightReduction). Base tier: ≤1 **Common**, 2 **Magic**, 3 **Rare**, 4 **Legendary**, 5 **Ancient**. The same weighted budget then shifts that one step: up at 150 / 250 / 375 / 475 / 575, down below 15 / 60 / 105 / 150 / 200. This is the Diablo II model — affix breadth sets the rarity, roll quality only nudges it.
  - **Distribution check (400 rolls per cell, Longsword/Kryss/Plate/Chain/Leather/Studded):** 1 affix → 88-94 % Common, the rest Magic; 2 affixes → Magic/Rare; 3 affixes → mostly Rare with ~10-13 % Legendary; 4 affixes → mostly Legendary with ~5-12 % Ancient; 5 affixes → mostly Ancient with ~10 % Primal.
  - **Targeted checks:** 1 affix (Slayer only) → **Magic**; 2 affixes (15 HCI + 40 DI) → **Rare**; 3 affixes (15 HCI + 40 DI + 25 SSI) → **Legendary**; 4 weak affixes (NightSight/Luck/ReflectPhysical/LowerStatReq) → **Rare**; plain weapon → **Common**; artefact → **None**.
  - Compile: scripts mcs over `Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts` against the already-rebuilt `WorldLinux.exe` → **14 warnings, zero errors**. No core rebuild needed. Pre-change script backed up as `Backups/ItemRank.cs.2026-11-21-095025.bak`.
  - The two tuning arrays (`m_Upgrade`, `m_Downgrade`) are the single place to shift the ladder if the spread feels too strict or too generous.
182. **Equipment tiers switched back to the shard's weighted strength budget** — one file, `Source/Scripts/System/Misc/ItemRank.cs`; the core hook is untouched.
  - **Why:** affix counting was not the wanted measure. Power should follow actual strength, and the shard already prices every AoS property through `ItemInformation.AddUpBenefits` (the vendor weights). The affix-count model and all of its counting helpers were removed.
  - **Power:** `AddUpBenefits( item, 0, false, false ) - 1` (base item price excluded), `+ ( CraftResources.GetGold( Resource ) - 1 ) × 30` for material, `× 1.15 / × 0.85` for exceptional / low quality. One utility correction: **self repair is credited at most 1 point**, because its gold weight is ×100 per point and a 5-point utility roll would otherwise score 500 and outrank real combat rolls.
  - **Tier floors (budget):** Magic **75**, Rare **200**, Legendary **400**, Ancient **600**, Primal **800**. A single flag — slayer, spell channelling or mage armour, each 200 — now tops out at **Rare**; the old over-rating came from 300/500 floors, where one flag plus a small roll reached Legendary/Ancient.
  - **Distribution (400 rolls per cell, swords/armour):** 1 property → 63-86 % Common, 9-33 % Magic, 3-4 % Rare, **no Legendary+**; 2 → Common/Magic/Rare, no Legendary; 3 → 22-54 % Common, 33-49 % Magic, 13-36 % Rare, ≤4 % Legendary; 4 → Legendary up to 16 % in the top band; 5 at 70-100 → 45 % Rare, 25 % Legendary, 4 % Ancient.
  - **Targeted:** slayer-only → **Rare**; spell-channelling-only → **Rare**; mage-armour-only → **Rare**; 15 HCI + 40 DI → **Rare**; 15 HCI + 40 DI + 25 SSI (500) → **Legendary**; four weak properties (46) → **Common**; self repair 5 → **Magic** (100); plain weapon → **Common**; artefact → **None**; plain valorite plate (60) → **Common**.
  - Compile: scripts mcs over `Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts` against `WorldLinux.exe` → **14 warnings, zero errors**. No core rebuild needed. Pre-change script backed up as `Backups/ItemRank.cs.2026-11-21-100009.bak`.
183. **Equipment tier floors rescaled — Legendary now 500** — one file, `Source/Scripts/System/Misc/ItemRank.cs`; nothing else touched.
  - **Change:** `m_PowerFloor` is now `{ 0, 0, 100, 250, 500, 750, 1000 }` (previously 75 / 200 / 400 / 600 / 800). That is every floor scaled by ×1.25 with Legendary pinned at 500; Magic is rounded up from 93.75 to 100.
  - **Read:** Magic **100**, Rare **250**, Legendary **500**, Ancient **750**, Primal **1000**.
  - **Effect (400 rolls per cell, swords/armour):** 1 property → 65-94 % Common, rest Magic, **no Rare+**; 2 → Common/Magic with 2.5-10 % Rare; 3 → Rare up to 32 %, Legendary only 0-0.75 %; 4 → Legendary 0.5-6.5 %; 5 at 70-100 → 51 % Rare, 13 % Legendary, 0.5 % Ancient.
  - **Targeted:** slayer-only (200) → **Magic**; 15 HCI + 40 DI (350) → **Rare**; 15 HCI + 40 DI + 25 SSI (500) → **Legendary**; self repair 5 (100) → **Magic**; plain weapon → **Common**; artefact → **None**.
  - **Note:** at this scale Ancient (750) is extremely rare and Primal (1000) is effectively a perfect-roll-only tier; lower those two floors if they should be more attainable.

184. **Equipment tier floors retuned to a Diablo-like drop spread** — one file, `Source/Scripts/System/Misc/ItemRank.cs`; nothing else touched.

    - Rich — heat 0: 52.7 / 30.4 / 15.2 / 1.6 / 0.2 / 0.0; heat 4: 37.5 / 40.4 / 19.8 / 2.1 / 0.1 / 0.0.
    - FilthyRich — heat 0: 40.1 / 36.2 / 19.8 / 3.3 / 0.5 / 0.0; heat 4: 26.1 / 39.7 / 27.7 / 5.6 / 0.9 / 0.0.
    - UltraRich — heat 0: 24.7 / 33.3 / 31.9 / 7.9 / 2.1 / 0.1; heat 4: 21.2 / 28.7 / 34.1 / 11.9 / 3.8 / 0.3.
  - **Per kill:** the Ultra pack drops ~6 equipment pieces, so a heat-4 Ultra boss is ~54 % to contain at least one Legendary and ~21 % an Ancient; the mixed population is ~4-5 % per kill for Legendary.

185. **Dev tier spawn commands for equipment testing** — new `Source/Scripts/System/Commands/LootTierCommands.cs` plus two small accessors in `Source/Scripts/System/Misc/ItemRank.cs`; nothing else touched.

  - **Builder:** picks a random weapon / armour-or-shield / clothing / hat / jewellery / instrument / quiver, then rolls **one random attribute at a time** (`BaseRunicTool.ApplyAttributes` with tier-appropriate intensity — Magic 20-50, Rare 30-70, Legendary 50-90, Ancient 60-100, Primal 70-100) until `ItemRank.GetPower` lands in the tier's band, retrying a fresh item if a roll overshoots. Common spawns a plain item. Up to 300 attempts.
  - **No duplicated thresholds:** added `ItemRank.GetFloor( tier )` / `GetCeiling( tier )`, so if the floors are retuned again the commands follow automatically.
  - **Verification (harness, 40 builds per tier):** all 240 items matched their requested tier — Common power 0; Magic 80-170 (avg 106); Rare 181-340 (214); Legendary 350-464 (392); Ancient 500-650 (549); Primal 700-870 (748). `Initialize()` registered all eight commands without error.

187. **Rarity-coloured item names** — `ItemRank.TryAppendColoredName`, a new hook in `Source/System/Item.cs`, plus guards in the five equipment name overrides.
  - **How:** the name is emitted through cliloc `1072171` (`<BASEFONT COLOR=#~1_VAL~> ~2_VAL~ </BASEFONT>`) with the tier hue — the same header markup the shard already renders for `ColorText1`. `Item.AddNameProperty` asks `Server.ItemRank.TryAppendColoredName` first (reflection hook, resolved once and cached), and `BaseWeapon`, `BaseArmor`, `BaseClothing`, `BaseTrinket` and `BaseInstrument` call it before their own formatting. `BaseWeapon` still writes the engraved-text line after the coloured name.
  - **Names resolved server-side:** custom `Name`, else `CliLocTable.Lookup( LabelNumber )`; the material prefix (e.g. "valorite") and the armour/trinket "exceptional" prefix reproduce the existing naming rules. If the text cannot be resolved the helper returns false and the normal name path runs, so raw cliloc tokens are never shown.
  - **Scope:** ranked equipment only. Artefacts (`ArtifactLevel` / `ArtifactRarity`), unidentified loot, `CoinPrice` relics, non-equipment and stackables keep their normal names. The colour lives in the cached property list, so there is no per-player cost.
  - **Verified:** plain longsword header `1072171:C7C7C7\tlongsword`; Legendary item `1072171:FF9C40\tcleaver`; valorite longsword `1072171:C7C7C7\tvalorite longsword`; exceptional plate `1072171:C7C7C7\texceptional platemail`; engraved longsword keeps `1062613:Test Engraving` directly after the coloured name; artefact keeps `1042971:longsword` + `1070754` and no tier line.
  - Compile: core mcs → **7 warnings, zero errors** (`World/WorldLinux.exe` rebuilt); scripts → **14 warnings, zero errors**. Backups: `Backups/{Item,ItemRank,BaseWeapon,BaseArmor,BaseClothing,BaseTrinket,BaseInstrument}.cs.2026-11-21-113420.bak`.
188. **Quivers now follow the tier name colour** — `ItemRank.GetNameHue` plus a new `InvokeNameHue` hook in `Source/System/Item.cs`; nothing else touched.
  - **Cause:** loot quivers are `MagicQuiver`, which sets `ColorText1` (e.g. "ancestral quiver"). `ColorText1` is added before the name, so it becomes the OPL header, and `Item.AddNameProperties` skips `AddNameProperty` when `ColorText1` is set on a standard-resource item — so the coloured-name hook never ran for them.
  - **Fix:** `AddNameProperties` now asks `Server.ItemRank.GetNameHue` and uses that hue for the `ColorText1` and `ColorText2` header lines, falling back to `ColorHue1/2` when the item is not ranked. The ordinary name hook is unchanged (ArcherQuiver, ElvenQuiver and all other equipment), and non-equipment keeps its own header hue.
  - **Verified:** MagicQuiver header `1072171:C7C7C7\tancestral quiver`; ArcherQuiver `1072171:C7C7C7\tquiver`; ElvenQuiver `1072171:C7C7C7\telven quiver`; a Legendary MagicQuiver `1072171:FF9C40\tphenomenal quiver`; MagicRuneBag (non-equipment) keeps `1072171:0080FF\thellish rune bag`.
  - Compile: core mcs → **7 warnings, zero errors** (`World/WorldLinux.exe` rebuilt); scripts → **14 warnings, zero errors**. Backups: `Backups/Item.cs.2026-11-21-114903.bak`, `Backups/ItemRank.cs.2026-11-21-114903.bak`.
