193. **Unidentified loot worth (base type + material), relics always show, unidentified gear keeps its tier** — `VendorValue.cs`, `ItemRank.cs`, `GenericSell.cs`.
  - **Relics always show:** the `NotIdentified` early return is gone. `IRelic` items go through `GetRelicValue`: identified → `CoinPrice`, unidentified → the original estimate `Utility.RandomMinMax( 5, 25 )`, both then scaled by the viewer's Mercantile. Every relic now produces a worth line.
  - **Unidentified loot:** a `NotIdentified` wrapper is priced from the item it contains. `GenericSellInfo.GetBasePriceFor` returns the raw table price for the wrapped item's type; `VendorValue` applies `CraftResources.GetGold( inner.Resource )`, the same second halving `GetSellPriceFor` performs, and the barter multiplier. Rolled properties are ignored, so the placeholder `CoinPrice = 100` and the magic modifiers never leak. Types missing from the table fall back to the old 5–25 estimate.
  - **Unidentified equipment tier:** `ItemRank.GetPower` unwraps `NotIdentified` before ranking, so the tier line uses the contained equipment. The header stays the generic wrapper name (`Container.AddNameProperty` does not run the coloured-name hook); `AppendTier` still draws `cliloc 1072172` in the tier hue.
  - **Verified:** unidentified `DDRelicCloth` → **5..25** at 0 Mercantile and **10..50** at 100 over 40 rolls, a worth line in **40/40**; identified → **1234 / 2468**. Valorite Longsword: plain and heavily decorated wrappers both **30** gold, equal to the open plain sword (modifiers ignored); wrapped tier **Primal** with `D46AFF\tPrimal`, no worth when Show Worth is off, no tier when Show Loot Tiers is off. Five greater heal potions → **55** gold, no tier.

Artefacts remain **unranked** (no tier line) and keep their existing marker; ordinary equipment still uses its tier hue.

212. **Dungeon Challenge Orb — region respawn + one-tier heat boost** — new `Items/Misc/DungeonChallengeOrb.cs`, new `System/Commands/DungeonChallengeCommands.cs`, core `System/Map.cs`.
  - **Respawn:** resolves the outermost dungeon `BaseRegion` from the player, calls `Respawn()` on every region `SpawnEntry`, then snapshots and resets in-region `PremiumSpawner`/`Spawner` items (`Respawn` + `Start`). Existing spawns are removed by the spawner APIs; only the chosen region (and its child regions) are touched.
  - **Boost:** `DungeonChallengeBoost` stores a region→expiry `+1`; core `Difficult.GetDifficulty` asks for it through the new `InvokeDifficultyBoost` reflection hook and adds it after the normal heat lookup, capped at 5 (Deadly). The hook retries when the script assembly is not loaded yet (world-build safety). Duration is `BoostMinutes` (default 15, 1–240), consumed by default; child regions inherit via `Region.IsPartOf`.
  - **Use:** double-click in a dungeon; broadcasts to players in the region, plays an effect, reports restored spawns/duration, deletes the orb. `[DungeonOrb` (GM) grants one for testing.
  - **Verified:** core compile **7 warnings, 0 errors** (`WorldLinux.exe` rebuilt); scripts **14 warnings, 0 errors**; focused harness **14/14** (parent/child boost inheritance, expiry, core hook resolves and clears, orb name/graphic/duration bounds/consume flag). Full in-world respawn execution needs a running map/region and was not exercised in the static harness.
  - Backups: `Backups/Map.cs.2026-11-21-162332.bak`. New files: `Source/Scripts/Items/Misc/DungeonChallengeOrb.cs`, `Source/Scripts/System/Commands/DungeonChallengeCommands.cs`.
213. **Cave of Virtue -- Angel / Archangel spawners and virtue ankhs** -- data files only, no code: `Info/Spawns/Spawns.map` and `Info/Decorations/Sosaria.cfg`. Backups: `Backups/Spawns.map.2026-11-21-194117.bak`, `Backups/Sosaria.cfg.2026-11-21-194117.bak`.
   - **Facet:** `the Cave of Virtue` is a `CaveRegion` declared under `<Facet name="Sosaria">` in `Data/System/XML/Regions.xml` (rect `5160,1685`, 116 x 80), and every supplied coordinate falls inside it, so the map column is `2` (`Map.Sosaria`). Using `0` would have duplicated each spawner on Lodor as well.
   - **Spawners (8):** seven `*|Angel||||||...|2|45|1h|1|1|1|1|0|0|0|0|0` lines plus one `*|Archangel|...` line at `5258,1735,0`. Column order in this build is `X|Y|Z|facet|MinTime|MaxTime|WalkingRange|HomeRange|SpawnID|Count|CountA..E`.
   - **Timings:** a bare number is minutes, and `SpawnGenerator.PlaceNPC` also honours the `s`/`m`/`h` suffixes this distro added, so `45` = 45 minutes and `1h` = one hour are stored exactly as requested instead of being written as `45` / `60` minutes. Replaying the parser gives `00:45:00` / `01:00:00`, and `DoTimer` rolls `Utility.RandomMinMax( 2700, 3600 )` seconds.
   - **Team 0:** the map format has no team column -- `SpawnGenerator` passes its fixed `private const int Team = 0`, and `PremiumSpawner.InitSpawn` only assigns `c.Team` when the value is above 0, so these spawners stay on the default team 0.
   - **Walk / home range:** both columns are `1` (HomeRange must never exceed WalkingRange, and identical values mean the argument shuffling between `PlaceNPC` and the `PremiumSpawner` constructor cannot change the result).
   - **Ankhs:** one `AnkhWest 3 (Bloodied=False)` block per coordinate (`5248,1733,0`, `5250,1738,0`). `DecorationList.Construct` builds `new AnkhWest( bloodied )` (the class also spawns its second tile at `Y+1`) and stamps `Weight = -2`; `[buildworld` deletes every `Weight == -2` item before regenerating from the cfg, so repeated builds never duplicate the ankhs.
   - **Verified:** both files were re-parsed with the server algorithms reproduced outside the game (spawn column mapping plus the timing math, and `DecorationList.Read`) -- 8 spawners and 2 ankhs, no stray rows. No C# changed for this entry; both changes appear on the next `[buildworld`.
214. **Cave of Virtue stays lit -- no night sight needed** -- one script file, `Source/Scripts/System/Regions/CaveRegion.cs`.
   - **Why it was dark:** `CaveRegion.AlterLightLevel` unconditionally set `global = LightCycle.CaveLevel` (26). `PlayerMobile.CheckLightLevels` calls `ComputeLightLevels`, which runs `ComputeBaseLightLevels` (`global = LightCycle.ComputeLevelFor( this )`) and then lets the region overwrite `global`; that value is what the `GlobalLightLevel` packet sends, so every cave rendered dark and only the NightSight item property (`personal = 19`) or a carried light source made it readable.
   - **Fix:** the override now branches on the region name, the same pattern `DungeonRegion`, `ProtectedRegion`, `SafeRegion`, `StartRegion` and `VillageRegion` already use for their lit exceptions: `if ( this.Name == "the Cave of Virtue" ){ global = LightCycle.DayLevel; } else { global = LightCycle.CaveLevel; }`. `DayLevel` is 0, exactly what `TownRegion` sends, so the cave is fully lit at every hour for everyone inside it, with or without night sight.
   - **Scope:** only the region named `the Cave of Virtue` (the single `CaveRegion` declared under `<Facet name="Sosaria">`, rect `5160,1685` 116 x 80) is affected. The other ~60 `CaveRegion`s, the dungeon levels (`DungeonLevel` 26), `NightLevel` (20) and the outdoor day/night cycle are untouched.
   - **Applying:** `CaveRegion.cs` is a *script*, not core code, so `World.exe` does not need rebuilding -- the server recompiles `Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts` on startup (writing `Data/Data.bin`). Restart the shard to pick it up.
   - **Verified:** the whole script set was compiled with the .NET 4.0 `csc` exactly as `ScriptCompiler` does (`/d:x64 /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0`, every `Assemblies.cfg` reference plus `World.exe`) -- **0 errors** and the same 11 pre-existing warnings as #175/#176, none in `CaveRegion.cs`; the emitted assembly contains the new `the Cave of Virtue` literal. Backup: `Backups/CaveRegion.cs.2026-11-21-195500.bak`.
   - **Tracker merge:** the working copy of this file arrived with leftover `<<<<<<< Updated upstream` / `=======` / `>>>>>>> Stashed changes` markers (a stash pop that was never resolved). It is now resolved as a union: the upstream items #177-#212 and their `Worth tooltip` / `Dungeon Challenge Orb` / `Equipment tier` / `Tier test commands` sections are kept, and the Cave of Virtue entries #213/#214 plus this section were re-applied on top.

These coordinates are therefore the ground immediately outside the entrance, not inside the `the Cave of Virtue` region rect (`5160,1685` 116 x 80).
   - **Ankhs:** two non-bloodied `AnkhNorth` shrines (art `0x4`) flanking the portal at `4178,269,0` and `4183,269,0`, written in the export format (`AnkhNorth 4 (Bloodied=False)` then `X Y Z`, blank line between entries).

216. **Cave of Virtue -- tall lit candelabra and glowing entrance ankhs** -- data file only: `Info/Decorations/Sosaria.cfg`. Backup: `Backups/Sosaria.cfg.2026-11-21-220847.bak`.
   - **Candelabra (21):** one `CandelabraStand 12342 (Light=Circle225; Hue=2498)` header followed by every requested tile at Z 0 (`5171,1752` ... `5194,1754`). `DecorationList.Read` attaches every coordinate line after a header to that header until the next blank line, so one header generates all 21 candelabras.
   - **Which art is "tall":** `CandelabraStand` is the tall floor candelabra -- `UnlitItemID` `0x3035`, `LitItemID` `0x3036` (12342), and `Data/Files/tiledata.mul` gives art `0x3035/0x3036` height **15** with `0x3036` carrying the `LightSource` flag. The plain `Candelabra` class is the short version (`0x302D/0x302E`, height **5**), so it was not used.
   - **Lit + protected:** `DecorationList.Construct` special-cases `BaseLight` (`Source/Scripts/System/Misc/Build.cs` ~4912). Unless the params contain `Unlit` it calls `Ignite()` (sets `Burning = true`, `ItemID`/`GraphicID` = `LitItemID`, and `Duration` is `TimeSpan.Zero` in this class so no burnout timer starts), and unless they contain `Unprotected` it sets `Protected = true`, so a player double-click cannot douse the flames. The header art `12342` then re-stamps the same lit art, and `Hue=2498` is applied by the generic `Hue` branch afterwards.
   - **Glow (2):** `GlowingLight 5703 (Light=Circle300)` at `4178,269,0` and `4183,269,0` -- exactly the tiles of the two entrance `AnkhNorth` shrines, which stay in place (items can share a tile with the ankh item), giving illuminated ankhs.
   - **Why not `StrangeGlow`:** `StrangeGlow` is the transient twin of `GlowingLight` (same art `0x1647`, same `LightType.Circle300`), but its constructor starts a 30-second `ItemRemovalTimer` and its `Deserialize` calls `Delete()`. A `StrangeGlow` placed by `[buildworld` would vanish after 30 seconds and never survive a restart, so the permanent, timer-free `GlowingLight` is used instead; the visible result is identical.
   - **Verified:** the cfg was re-parsed with `DecorationList.Read` replayed in Python: 7 headers / 27 entries -- 2 `AnkhWest`, 2 `AnkhNorth`, 21 `CandelabraStand`, 2 `GlowingLight` -- all Z 0, CRLF-only, and the 21 candelabra tiles are unique and in the requested order. Art names, heights and `LightSource` flags were read out of `tiledata.mul` with the exact `TileData` layout used by this core (file length 3188736, 7.0.9.0). No C# changed; the decorations appear on the next `[buildworld`.
217. **Cave of Virtue -- 8 dropped StoneColumns replaced with Static decorations** -- one data file plus the item index: `Info/Decorations/Sosaria.cfg`, `Saves/Items/Items.idx`. Backups: `Backups/Items.bin.2026-11-21-224838.bak`, `Backups/Items.idx.2026-11-21-224838.bak`, `Backups/Items.tdb.2026-11-21-224838.bak`.
   - **Found in the newest save:** `Saves/Items/Items.bin` + `.idx` + `.tdb` were parsed with this core's own persistence format (`Item.Serialize` v14 field order, `SaveFlag` bitfield, 7-bit encoded ints, 1-byte-prefixed strings, per-record `pos/length` from the index). The type table matched exactly (2,230 types) and all 269,986 index entries consumed the whole 5,399,724-byte index. Exactly **eight** `Server.Items.StoneColumn` items exist in the save, all on Sosaria (`MapIndex 1`), art `0x11B`, hue 0, Z 0, `Movable = True`, "Made From Granite", all inside the `the Cave of Virtue` rect.
   - **Tiles/serials:** `5233,1708,0` (`0x400A29DA`), `5234,1718,0` (`0x400A29D9`), `5255,1718,0` (`0x400A2A65`), `5248,1722,0` (`0x400A2A64`), `5216,1733,0` (`0x400A2769`), `5190,1735,0` (`0x400A26B8`), `5222,1739,0` (`0x400A280F`), `5191,1744,0` (`0x400A26B7`). None has a `Parent` flag, so nothing (container or mobile) referenced them.
   - **Removal (index-only):** `Items.bin` records are addressed by `(pos, length)` pairs in `Items.idx`, so deleting the eight 20-byte index entries removes the items without rewriting or shifting the 47 MB data file. `Items.bin` was left byte-identical (SHA-256 compared with the backup); the orphaned byte ranges are simply not referenced and compact away on the next world save. Result: 269,986 → 269,978 entries (5,399,724 → 5,399,564 bytes).
   - **Static decor:** one `Static 283` header (art `0x11B` = 283, hue 0) with the eight coordinate lines at Z 0. `DecorationList.Construct` special-cases `Static` → `new Static(283)` and then stamps `Movable = false` and `Weight = -2`, so `[buildworld` recreates them as immovable decoration items with the same art and position.
   - **Verified:** before the edit, the index re-serialised byte-for-byte from its parsed entries; after, all remaining entries are byte-identical and in the original order, 0 out-of-range records, no duplicate serials, and the eight serials are gone. A full base-record parse of every remaining index entry gives the same result as before (245,031 → 245,023 successes with the identical 24,955 container/bounce non-base records), proving no other record was disturbed. The decoration file re-parses to 8 blocks / 35 entries: 2 `AnkhWest`, 2 `AnkhNorth`, 21 `CandelabraStand`, 8 `Static`, 2 `GlowingLight`, all Z 0, and the `Static 283` tile list equals the removed StoneColumn list exactly.
   - **Applying:** the shard was **not** running while the index was rewritten (verified no `World`/`WorldLinux` process), so the edited index is the newest state on disk. Start the shard from this state, then run `[buildworld` to spawn the eight statics. If the shard is later started from an older copy of `Saves/Items/*`, the columns come back -- restore the files above instead.
218. **Umbra — the aristocracy is above the law (criminal immunity, pets/summons, SAFE PLACES, tavern rumor)** — 13 script files. All backups dated `2026-11-22-135403` in `Backups/`.
   - **Entering the Undercity forgives crime:** `UmbraRegion.OnEnter` now sets `Criminal = false` for every `PlayerMobile` that enters the region. The `Mobile.Criminal` setter stops `ExpireCriminalTimer` when cleared, so the flag is gone at once and `IntelligentAction.GetMyEnemies` treats the player as a non-enemy (blue via `!Criminal && Kills < 1`, murderer via the existing Umbra rule).
   - **No crime sticks for the aristocracy:** new `GetPlayerInfo.AboveTheLawInUmbra( Mobile )` returns true for a `PlayerMobile` in `UmbraRegion` with `Fame >= 10000` **and** `Karma <= -10000`. `PlayerMobile.CriminalAction` is overridden to return early for them, so attacking NPCs/innocents, stealing, corpse looting, chests, assisting criminals and the AI-acquisition paths never set `Criminal` (and the "You've committed a criminal act!!" message is suppressed).
   - **Raw `Criminal = true` assignments now go through `GetPlayerInfo.MakeCriminal( Mobile )`** (same Umbra test): `BaseVendor.OnBeforeDeath` (killing a vendor), `BasePerson.OnBeforeDeath`, `BaseCreature.OnBeforeDeath` (`AI_Citizen`), `HenchmanFunctions` (henchman death), `GraveRobbing` (witnessed grave digging — with an Umbra-specific "Someone has spotted you, but no one here seems to care." message), `ResearchOpenGround` / `ResearchDevastation`, the classic-stealing perma-flag in `PlayerMobile.OnDeath`, and `ProfanityProtection`'s `ProfanityAction.Criminal`.
   - **Pets and summons:** `BaseCreature.CriminalAction` returns early when the creature is in `UmbraRegion` and `GetMaster()` is an above-the-law player, so neither the pet/summon nor its owner is flagged. `IntelligentAction.GetMyEnemies` additionally exempts (a) an above-the-law player in Umbra even if a stray flag exists and (b) any creature in Umbra whose `ControlMaster`/`SummonMaster` is one. That second check is what stops Umbra guards from teleporting to/attacking player summons: player summons are created with `controlled = false` (`BaseCreature.Summon`) and use `FightMode.Closest`, so they were neither `Controlled` nor `Aggressor` and previously counted as guard enemies.
   - **Murder counts unchanged (per decision):** killing an Umbra NPC still adds `Kills`; the guards already ignore murderers who are not criminal.
   - **SAFE PLACES:** `GetPlayerInfo.GetWantedStatus` now always appends `Umbra Undercity` — the `umbra` flag that removed it for murderers/criminals is gone — so the `[wanted` gump reflects that Umbra accepts murderers, criminals, death knights and monster races.
   - **Rumor:** `TavernPatrons.GetChatter` gained `case 61: sSpeech = "It's said that in Umbra the aristocracy is above the law.";` and `CommonTalkingCount` went 60 → 61, putting it in the tavern speech pool (tavern patrons speak `sGossip`, which falls back to `sSpeech`).
   - **Compile:** the full script set was compiled with the .NET 4.0 `csc` exactly as `ScriptCompiler` does (`/d:NEWTIMERS /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0 /d:x64`, `World.exe` plus every `Assemblies.cfg` reference, `Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`) — **0 errors**, same pre-existing warnings as the pre-change baseline, none in the edited files. Backups: `Backups/{Behavior,BaseCreature,BaseVendor,BasePerson,PlayerMobile,Players,UmbraRegion,HenchmanFunctions,GraveRobbing,ResearchOpenGround,ResearchDevastation,ProfanityProtection,TavernPatrons}.cs.2026-11-22-135403.bak`.
219. **"(elite)" tag replaces "(boss)" and now covers champion spawn champions, which also drop Fragments of Infinity** — 3 script files: `Source/Scripts/Utilities/MobileUtilities.cs`, `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Base/BaseCreature.cs`, `Source/Scripts/Items/Misc/FragmentOfInfinity.cs`.
  - **Tag:** `BaseCreature.ApplyNameSuffix` now appends **"(elite)"** instead of "(boss)" after the `(cursed)` paragon marker, so a paragon elite reads **"… (cursed) (elite)"**. `Mobile.OnSingleClick` and the OPL tooltip already call `ApplyNameSuffix`, so no per-mob or per-click code was needed.
  - **Registry:** `MobileUtilities.IsBoss` was renamed **`IsElite`** and now returns true for `bc is BaseChampion` as well as the 23 named bosses (exact `GetType()` match) and the 25 Magical Prison escapees (`EmoteHue == 505`). The six champion spawn champions come from `ChampionSpawnInfo.Table` — `Semidar` (Abyss), `Mephitis` (Arachnid), `Rikktor` (Cold Blood), `Lord Oaks` (Forest Lord), `Barracoon` (Vermin Horde) and `Neira` (Unholy Terror). `BaseChampion` has no other subclasses in the tree, so ordinary creatures are unaffected.
  - **Drops:** `FragmentOfInfinityDrops.IsFragmentCarrier` now calls `MobileUtilities.IsElite`, so the champions roll the same flat **10%** corpse drop as the named and prison bosses. Champion corpses are kept (`ChampionSpawnRegion.OnBeforeDeath` only deletes ephemeral non-champion spawns), and `BlackGateDemon`/`Mangar` keep routing their roll into their reward chests through `DeleteCorpseOnDeath`.
  - **No save migration:** the tag and the drop test are computed at runtime, so champions, bosses and prison escapees already in `World/Saves` show "(elite)" immediately after a restart. `Name`/`Title` are untouched, so logs, loot-chest owners and other server-generated strings stay clean.
  - **Verified:** the whole script set (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`, 5,175 files) was compiled with the .NET 4.0 `csc` exactly as `ScriptCompiler` does (`/d:x64 /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0`, `World.exe` plus every `Assemblies.cfg` reference) — **0 errors, 11 warnings**, the same pre-existing baseline as #218 and none in the three edited files.
  - Backups: `Backups/MobileUtilities.cs.2026-11-24-165151.bak`, `Backups/BaseCreature.cs.2026-11-24-165151.bak`, `Backups/FragmentOfInfinity.cs.2026-11-24-165151.bak`.
220. **Henchmen — fencing, paperdoll equipment, persistent gear, weapon/spell restrictions and durability warnings** — 6 Comrades scripts plus `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Base/Behavior.cs`.
   - **Fighter:** `HenchmanFighter` now trains `SkillName.Fencing` at the same level as Swords and Bludgeoning.
   - **Paperdoll access:** the owner (`ControlMaster`) can add/remove gear on a fighter, archer or wizard henchman's paperdoll within **2 tiles**; GMs are still allowed. This is implemented with `AllowEquipFrom` (`EquipReq` path) and `CheckNonlocalLift` (drag-off path) overrides plus `HenchmanFunctions.CanUseHenchman`; non-owners and out-of-range players are still denied.
   - **Persistence:** the deed (`HenchmanItem`) now stores a `List<Item> HenchEquipment` and a `bool HenchGearMade`, serialization version 0 → 1. While the henchman is dismissed/dead the items are kept parentless in `Map.Internal` and referenced by the deed; on summon they are restored to the appropriate layers (through `EquipItem` where possible, with `AddItem` fallback for default/low-stat gear). Gear is stashed before death (`OnDead`), before the low-fame dismissal clears the deed (`OnMoving`) and in every `OnDelete`, so it survives death, release, low-fame dismissal and server restarts. `HenchmanItem.OnDelete` only cleans up items that are still orphaned in the stash; items the player has taken are never deleted.
   - **Default gear:** every default piece created by `DressUp` is now **movable, `LootType.Regular`, `StrRequirement = 10`**, named **“henchman's armor”** (armour/clothing/helm/shield/robe/hat/gloves/cloak/boots) or **“henchman's weapon”** (all weapons). Defaults are generated **once per deed** (`HenchGearMade`), so stripping/selling them and resummoning does not create a fresh free set. Legacy immovable+blessed defaults saved on old henchmen are migrated to the same properties the first time they are stashed.
   - **Fighter restriction:** a main/off-hand item must be a `BaseShield` or a `BaseWeapon` whose `Skill` is Swords, Bludgeoning or Fencing; otherwise the henchman says **“Give me a proper melee weapon.”** and rejects it.
   - **Archer restriction:** a main/off-hand item must be a `BaseShield` or a bow/crossbow, detected as `BaseRanged` with `AmmoType == typeof(Arrow)` or `typeof(Bolt)` (the same test the codebase uses for bowyer bonuses). This rejects `GiftStave`/`GiftSceptre`/`LevelStave`/`LevelSceptre`, which are `BaseRanged` but fire `MageEye`, and says **“Give me a bow or crossbow.”**
   - **Mage restriction:** a hand item must allow equipped casting (`item.AllowEquipedCast(henchman)`), i.e. have spell channeling or be a magic lantern/torch; otherwise **“I can't cast spells while holding that.”** Worn armour that would contribute to the passive meditation penalty (`RegenRates.GetArmorOffset`: armour on neck/gloves/helm/arms/legs/chest/shoes/cloak/outer-torso with no Mage Armor/spell channeling and `MeditationAllowance != All`) is accepted with **“I'll wear this, but it will inhibit my meditation.”**
   - **Nontransferable / avatar-only:** `ValidateHenchmanEquip` rejects `item.Nontransferable` and `Server.Engines.Avatar.IAvatarOnlyItem` with **“I cannot use that.”**
   - **Durability:** on every summon and after accepted bandages or accepted gold/treasure, all equipped `IDurability` items are checked. Any item below **5%** of maximum says **“Some of my equipment is in danger of breaking.”**; otherwise any item below **20%** says **“Some of my equipment is looking worn.”** (One line only, after the normal message.)
   - **Scope:** paperdoll editing/persistence is limited to fighter/archer/wizard. Creature henchmen keep their raw `Bow`/`WizardStaff` and are not editable/persisted; they also receive the minimum-31 class skills and durability warnings.
   - **Release/dismiss path fix:** `Behavior.DoOrderRelease` clears `HenchSerial` before calling `Delete()`, which made the `OnDelete` stash unable to find the deed and caused the next summon to be naked. It now calls `Server.Misc.HenchmanFunctions.StashGear( m_Mobile )` before clearing the serial, so release/dismissal (not just low-fame auto-despawn and death) preserves the gear.
   - **Minimum class skills:** `HenchmanItem.OnDoubleClick` clamps the skill copied from the owner to a minimum of **31** before constructing the henchman, so fighter/archer/wizard/creature henchmen always spawn with all of their class skills at ≥31 even when the owner's highest primary skill is lower. This also lets the default `WizardStaff` satisfy `BaseWizardStaff.CanEquip`'s Magery/Necromancy/Elementalism ≥ 30 check.
   - **Strength feedback:** `ValidateHenchmanEquip` now mirrors the core Strength checks for `BaseWeapon`, `BaseArmor` and `BaseClothing` and says the visible **“I'm not strong enough to use that.”** before `Mobile.EquipItem` runs. Previously the core's “not strong enough” message went to the henchman, who has no client, so the player saw nothing; this covers items such as Blade of the Cimmerian (AoS Strength Requirement 85) on a mage henchman.
   - **Fixed stats:** henchman Strength/Dexterity/Intelligence are no longer derived from the owner. Fighter and archer henchmen use **100 Str / 100 Dex / 50 Int**; mage henchmen use **100 Str / 50 Dex / 100 Int**. Creature henchmen follow their role (melee/archer use the fighter/archer block, mage uses the mage block). `nStats` still drives the existing armour/damage/resistance scaling; only the three primary stats were changed.
   - **Verified:** the full script set (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`) was compiled with the .NET 4.0 `csc` exactly as `ScriptCompiler` does (`/d:x64 /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0`, `World.exe` plus every `Assemblies.cfg` reference) — **0 errors, 11 warnings**, the same pre-existing baseline as #219 and none in the seven edited files.
   - Backups: `Backups/HenchmanFunctions.cs.2026-11-24-184536.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanItem.cs.2026-11-24-184536.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanFighter.cs.2026-11-24-184536.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanArcher.cs.2026-11-24-184536.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanWizard.cs.2026-11-24-184536.bak`, `Backups/Behavior.cs.2026-11-24-200822.bak`, follow-up backups `Backups/HenchmanItem.cs.2026-11-24-204920.bak` and `Backups/HenchmanFunctions.cs.2026-11-24-204920.bak`, and fixed-stat backups `Backups/HenchmanFighter.cs.2026-11-24-211328.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanArcher.cs.2026-11-24-211328.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanWizard.cs.2026-11-24-211328.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanMonster.cs.2026-11-24-211328.bak`.

221. **Henchmen — weapon-derived damage, gear-as-floor resistances and a permanent 70 cap** — 3 script files: `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Civilized/Comrades/HenchmanFighter.cs`, `HenchmanArcher.cs`, `HenchmanWizard.cs`.
   - **Weapon damage:** fighter, archer and mage henchmen no longer call `SetDamage`; `DamageMin`/`DamageMax` are overridden to report the equipped weapon's `MinDamage`/`MaxDamage` (the backing `-1` default also lets `BaseWeapon.GetBaseDamageRange` fall through to the weapon range), so base damage comes from the weapon exactly like a player's attack. The same override keeps information gumps (Druidism "Base Damage") from showing `-1 - -1`. `ScaleDamageAOS` still applies the normal Strength/Anatomy/Tactics bonus on top. An unarmed henchman falls back to fists (1–4), like a player. Creature henchmen (`HenchmanMonster`) keep their existing nStats `SetDamage` range.
   - **Resistance floor, not addition:** the nStats-derived values (Physical `nStats/2`, other four `nStats/4` for fighters; `nStats/3` / `nStats/6` for archers; `nStats/4` / `nStats/7` for mages) are no longer passed to `SetResistance`. They are stored in `m_ProtectResistance`/`m_ArmorResistance` and returned from `GetMinResistance`, which `Mobile.ComputeResistances` applies as a floor — the same mechanism as a player's Magic Resistance minimum. Gear is only used when its total is higher: nStats 40 + 20 gear = **40**, nStats 40 + 41 gear = **41**.
   - **Permanent 70 cap:** `GetMaxResistance` returns **70** for these three classes. The core clamps on every `ComputeResistances`, including the `UpdateResistances` triggered by `AddItem`/`RemoveItem` when an item carrying resistances is equipped or removed, so gear added after summoning can no longer push a henchman above 70. The summon-time `HenchmanFunctions.NormalizeArmor` call is kept but is now inert for these classes (computed values never exceed 70).
   - **Creature henchmen unchanged and still non-editable:** `HenchmanMonster` keeps its nStats damage/resistances and has no `AllowEquipFrom`/`CheckNonlocalLift` overrides, so the core refuses player equipping (only the mobile itself or a higher-level GM is allowed) and refuses another player lifting its equipped `Bow`/`WizardStaff`. Dropping equipment on it is rejected by `HenchmanFunctions.OnGive`. No change was needed for this part.
   - **Verified:** the full script set (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`) was compiled with the .NET 4.0 `csc` exactly as `ScriptCompiler` does (`/d:x64 /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0`, `World.exe` plus every `Assemblies.cfg` reference) — **0 errors, 11 warnings**, identical to the pre-change baseline and none in the three edited files. A reflection harness run against the compiled script assembly exercised the new overrides: 27/27 assertions passed (the floor/cap cases listed above plus a presence check that all three classes override `DamageMin`, `DamageMax`, `GetMinResistance` and `GetMaxResistance`).
   - Backups: `Backups/HenchmanFighter.cs.2026-11-24-220659.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanArcher.cs.2026-11-24-220659.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanWizard.cs.2026-11-24-220659.bak`.

222. **Henchmen — weapon elemental damage for human henchmen and owner-only enchantment-point spending** — 6 script files: `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Civilized/Comrades/HenchmanFunctions.cs`, `Source/Scripts/Items/Weapons/BaseWeapon.cs`, `Source/Scripts/Items/Magical/Gifts/Gifts.cs`, `Source/Scripts/Items/Magical/Gifts/GiftGump.cs`, `Source/Scripts/Items/Magical/God/LevelInfoEntry.cs`, `Source/Scripts/Items/Magical/God/Gumps/ItemExperienceGump.cs`.
   - **Weapon elemental damage:** `BaseWeapon.GetDamageTypes` now keeps the creature branch only for creatures that are not `HenchmanFighter`/`HenchmanArcher`/`HenchmanWizard` (`HenchmanFunctions.UsesWeaponDamageTypes`). Those three take the player branch, so their attack split comes from the equipped weapon's AOS elemental attributes and craft-resource element damage (a 100% fire weapon hits as 100% fire) instead of `SetDamageType( Physical 40, Fire/Cold/Poison/Energy 10 )`. Creature henchmen (`HenchmanMonster`) and every other creature are unchanged. The `SetDamageType` calls on the human henchmen are kept and now only feed creature-information displays (the Druidism "Base Damage" section).
   - **Enchantment points are owner-only:** new `HenchmanFunctions.CanSpendHenchmanPoints( item, from )` returns true for items not held by a henchman; when the item's `RootParent` is one of the four henchman types it returns true only for that henchman's `ControlMaster` (and for a GM with higher access, matching the paperdoll rule in `CanUseHenchman`). The check runs in `GiftInfoEntry.OnClick`, `LevelInfoEntry.OnClick`, `GiftGump.OnResponse` and `ItemExperienceGump.OnResponse`, so both the gift (`IGiftable`) and legendary (`ILevelable`) point pools are protected, including a gump that was opened before the item moved onto a henchman. A refused player sees **"You cannot spend the enchantment points on another person's henchman."**
   - **Verified:** compiled with the .NET 4.0 `csc` exactly as `ScriptCompiler` does (`/d:x64 /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0`, `World.exe` plus every `Assemblies.cfg` reference, `Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`) — **0 errors, 11 warnings**, the same baseline as #221 and none in the six edited files. A reflection harness against the compiled scripts passed **22/22 assertions**: fighter/archer/wizard take the weapon branch (empty elements = 100 physical), the creature henchman keeps 40/10/10/10/10, the point gate allows the owner and a GM but refuses a stranger for both human and creature henchmen, and ground/player-held items stay unrestricted.
   - Backups: `Backups/HenchmanFunctions.cs.2026-11-24-224923.bak`, `Backups/BaseWeapon.cs.2026-11-24-224923.bak`, `Backups/Gifts.cs.2026-11-24-224923.bak`, `Backups/GiftGump.cs.2026-11-24-224923.bak`, `Backups/LevelInfoEntry.cs.2026-11-24-224923.bak`, `Backups/ItemExperienceGump.cs.2026-11-24-224923.bak`.

223. **Henchmen — every equipment stat now reaches the human henchmen** — 5 script files: `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Civilized/Comrades/HenchmanFunctions.cs`, `HenchmanFighter.cs`, `HenchmanArcher.cs`, `HenchmanWizard.cs`, `HenchmanItem.cs`.
   - **Only the seeded maxima were missing:** every other AOS property already reaches any `Mobile` because `AosAttributes.GetValue` has no player gate, so Hit/Defend Chance, Weapon Damage/Speed, Spell Damage, Faster Cast/Recovery, Lower Mana/Reg Cost, Reflect Physical, Enhance Potions and the Hit/Stam/Mana regeneration rates already worked for NPCs. Str/Dex/Int come from each item class's `OnEquip`/`OnAdded` stat mods, skill bonuses from `AosSkillBonuses.AddTo`, gear resistances from `Mobile.ComputeResistances` (the #221 floor/cap still decides whether the nStats value or the gear value wins), and the weapon's leeches/hit spells/resist bonuses are read from the wielded item itself.
   - **BonusHits / BonusStam / BonusMana:** `BaseCreature.HitsMax`/`StamMax`/`ManaMax` only add `GetStatOffset( Str/Dex/Int )`, while `PlayerMobile` also adds those three AOS attributes. `HenchmanFighter`, `HenchmanArcher` and `HenchmanWizard` now override all three maxima and add the equipment values through `HenchmanFunctions.GetEquipmentPoolMax( m, attribute, baseValue )`, which clamps the result to the same 1..65000 range `BaseCreature` uses.
   - **Gear changes:** `OnHenchmanEquipped` and `OnHenchmanItemRemoved` call `HenchmanFunctions.RefreshEquipmentPools( henchman, false )`. It clamps Hits/Stam/Mana to the new maxima (removing a +Hits item can no longer leave a character above its maximum) and calls `CheckStatTimers()` so the regeneration timers use the new values. `AosAttributes.SetValue` already covers GM edits made through the properties gump.
   - **Summon starts full:** `HenchmanItem.OnDoubleClick` calls `RefreshEquipmentPools( friend, true )` after `EquipStoredGear`/`NormalizeArmor`, so a freshly summoned henchman starts at the full equipment-boosted hit point, stamina and mana maxima instead of the pre-gear constructor values.
   - **Staff / forced-gear restore:** `EquipStoredGear` now runs `item.OnEquip( henchman )` before its direct `AddItem` for `BaseWizardStaff`/`BaseGiftStave`/`BaseLevelStave` (the mage-eye OnEquip message goes to the clientless henchman, so it stays silent) and for the `EquipItem` fallback. That applies the weapon/armour stat mods and the `MageWeapon` skill mod that direct attachment would otherwise skip.
   - **Scope:** the overrides are on the three human henchmen only; `HenchmanMonster` and other creatures keep their seeded nStats/SetHits values, matching the #221/#222 split. Luck has no NPC effect because creature loot does not roll it.
   - **Verified:** compiled the full script set (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`, 5,175 files) with the .NET 4.0 `csc` exactly as `ScriptCompiler` does (`/d:x64 /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0`, `World.exe` plus every `Assemblies.cfg` reference) — **0 errors, 11 warnings**, the same pre-existing baseline and none in the edited files. A reflection harness against the compiled scripts passed **38/38 assertions**: fighter base pools 200/200/100; +10 Str / +25 Hits / +10 Stam / +5 Mana gear gives 235/210/105 with Str 110; removal clamps all three current pools and returns the maxima to base; a +15 AttackChance / +5 Dex / leeching longsword is used (weapon-driven `DamageMin`, `StamMax` +5); `RefreshEquipmentPools( fill:true )` restores 50/20/10 to full; wizard +10 Int / +20 Mana gives `ManaMax` 230; a staff's `OnEquip` applies +7 Str and +13 BonusHits; and all three classes declare the three overrides.
   - Backups: `Backups/HenchmanFunctions.cs.2026-11-25-135410.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanFighter.cs.2026-11-25-135410.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanArcher.cs.2026-11-25-135410.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanWizard.cs.2026-11-25-135410.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanItem.cs.2026-11-25-135410.bak`.


### Henchmen — NPC appearance customization (#224)
- **Trigger:** Added a literal **Customize** button under the henchman's name in the existing `HenchmanInfoGump` (opened by the existing **Information** context entry). It opens `HenchmanCustomizeGump` and its **Name/Title**, Hair, Beard, Gender and Skin Tone sub-gumps, which use the dark vendor panel, literal English labels, immediate live updates, and return to the main menu after each change or via BACK. Creature henchmen use `HenchmanCreatureInfoGump` and are excluded.
- **Persistence:** `HenchmanItem` gained `HenchBeard`/`HenchBeardHue` (CommandProperties), serialization version 1 → 2, and now applies `FacialHairItemID`/`FacialHairHue` on summon. New male deeds roll a beard whose hue matches the hair; old deeds are not backfilled. The tooltip name/title line is trimmed when the title is empty.
- **Name/Title:** Both fields are limited to 16 characters and HTML-sanitized (`<`, `>`, `&`, control characters removed); a blank title clears it and a blank name is rejected. The entry fields use white text (`0x7FFF`).
- **Gender:** Switches `BodyValue` `400`/`401` and `Female`; female→male is blocked while any worn `BaseArmor`/`BaseClothing` has `AllowMaleWearer == false`, with `"Remove gender-specific gear before customizing gender."` Switching to female clears the beard. Hair/beard use the vendor style lists plus Bald/None, and the natural 48 hues `1102`–`1149`; skin uses all 57 human hues `1002`–`1058`.
- **Lifecycle:** `OnMovement` (GoAway), `Behavior.DoOrderRelease` (before `SetControlMaster( null )`) and `OnDelete` close the customization gumps; every `OnResponse` re-validates that the henchman still exists and is controlled by the sender.
- **Removed:** Deleted the barkeep/tavern-keeper deed-swap branch from `BaseVendor.OnDragDrop`. `Talk.cs` still describes the old exchange and will be updated separately.
- **No client changes:** The trial cliloc `3011033` and the local TazUO client `Cliloc.enu` edit were reverted; the trigger uses literal gump text, so no client data changes are required.
- **Follow-up (#224):** Moved the **Customize** button under the henchman's name in `HenchmanInfoGump`; renamed the option/header to **Name/Title**; set the name/title text entries to white (`0x7FFF`); widened the hue-grid columns and row spacing (hair/beard 4 columns at 70px with 22px rows; skin 6 columns at 65px) and moved the color labels 24px from each button so the five asterisks no longer overlap. Backups: `Backups/HenchmanInfoGump.cs.2026-11-25-185313.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanCustomizeGump.cs.2026-11-25-185313.bak`.
- **Verified:** Compiled the full script set (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`) with the .NET 4.0 `csc` against `World.exe` using the same defines as `ScriptCompiler` (`x64`, `Framework_2_0`, `Framework_4_0`, `NEWTIMERS`, `NEWPARENT`) — **0 errors, 11 warnings** (the existing baseline; none in the edited files).
- Backups: `Backups/HenchmanItem.cs.2026-11-25-175658.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanFighter.cs.2026-11-25-175658.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanArcher.cs.2026-11-25-175658.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanWizard.cs.2026-11-25-175658.bak`, `Backups/BaseVendor.cs.2026-11-25-175658.bak`, `Backups/Behavior.cs.2026-11-25-175658.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanInfoGump.cs.2026-11-25-183514.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanFighter.cs.2026-11-25-183514.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanArcher.cs.2026-11-25-183514.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanWizard.cs.2026-11-25-183514.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanInfoGump.cs.2026-11-25-185313.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanCustomizeGump.cs.2026-11-25-185313.bak`.
- **Follow-up (#226):** the `0x7FFF` entry hue *was* the problem: a text entry takes a `hues.mul` index, and `0x7FFF` is out of range, so the client drew the name/title text black — both fields now use `WhiteTextHue` (`2049`, the hues.mul "Fade" whites). The 70px/65px columns were also still narrower than one swatch (a 22px-wide `0xFA5` button plus the five asterisks), so the grids now share an `AddColorSwatch` helper on 92px columns with 24px rows, and the hair/beard/skin panels grew to `580×440`, `680×440` and `600×380`. The two `&amp;` beard style names are literal `&` again. Backed up as `Backups/HenchmanCustomizeGump.cs.2026-11-25-200834.bak`.



### Henchmen — deed-driven gender for human henchmen (#225)
- **Root cause:** `BaseCreature`'s constructor ends with the Jako taming block `Female = Utility.RandomBool();` (`Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Base/BaseCreature.cs:5004`), which runs before every derived constructor body. `HenchmanFighter`/`HenchmanArcher`/`HenchmanWizard` only forced the flag for female deeds (`if ( Body == 401 ){ this.Female = true; }`), so a male deed (`HenchBody == 400`) kept the random roll — roughly half of all male henchmen spawned with `Female == true` while keeping the male body, the male `NameList.RandomName( "male" )` name and the beard.
- **Fix:** all three constructors now set the flag from the deed body: `this.Female = ( myBody == 401 );`. Female deeds are unchanged (`401` → female) and male deeds can no longer keep a random female flag. `HenchmanMonster` is untouched, so creature henchmen keep the Jako random gender.
- **Symptoms removed:** female vocal sounds on male henchmen (`HenchmanFunctions.OnGaveAttack`/`OnSpellAttack`/`OnGotAttack`), the hidden/blocked Beard menu (`HenchmanCustomizeGump`), the Gender gump reporting "Female" for a male paperdoll, female-only `BaseArmor`/`BaseClothing` (`AllowMaleWearer == false`) being equippable on a male henchman and then blocking the female→male switch through `HasFemaleOnlyGear()`, and `SaveAppearance()` persisting `HenchBody = 401` for a male henchman (which used to flip a male deed to a female body with a male name and beard on its next summon).
- **Existing state:** the flag is not stored on the deed (`HenchmanItem` saves `HenchBody` only), so live male deeds are correct from their next summon onward. Deeds already flipped to `HenchBody == 401` by the old `SaveAppearance()` path are not migrated; fix those manually if needed.
- **Verified:** full script compile (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`) with the .NET 4.0 `csc` against `World.exe` (`x64`, `Framework_2_0`, `Framework_4_0`, `NEWTIMERS`, `NEWPARENT`) — **0 errors, 11 warnings** (the documented baseline; none in the edited files).
- Backups: `Backups/HenchmanFighter.cs.2026-11-25-193816.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanArcher.cs.2026-11-25-193816.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanWizard.cs.2026-11-25-193816.bak`.


226. **Henchman customization — white name/title fields, roomier hue grids, literal ampersands** — 1 script file: `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Civilized/Comrades/HenchmanCustomizeGump.cs`.
   - **Name/Title text is white now (root cause):** the two `AddTextEntry` calls passed `0x7FFF`, which is the `<BASEFONT COLOR>`/`AddHtml` "white" — a text entry instead takes a **`hues.mul` index**, so `0x7FFF` is out of range and the client fell back to black. Both fields now use the new `HenchmanCustomizeGumpBase.WhiteTextHue = 2049`; the client's own `hues.mul` (3000 hues, read to confirm) has `Fade 29`/`Fade 30`/`Fade 31` at 2048-2050 with every ramp stop between 239 and 255, so the typed name and title read white whether the client indexes the entry hue as `h` or as `h + 1`.
   - **Colour grids were narrower than one swatch (root cause):** `gumpart.mul`/`gumpidx.mul` record the `0xFA5`/`0xFA7` arrow as a **22×30** image, and the label starts 24px after the button, so the old 70px hair/beard columns left only ~46px for the five asterisks while the next column's button began at 70px — the tail of every asterisk row sat under its neighbour, and the rightmost column's label ran past the panel. The grids now go through one helper, `HenchmanCustomizeGumpBase.AddColorSwatch`, using `SwatchColumnWidth = 92`, `SwatchRowHeight = 24` and `SwatchLabelOffset = 24`: 68px of clear label room per column, and 22-90px of panel margin after the last label.
   - **Panels:** hair `500×410` → `580×440` (4 colour columns from x=180, style list labels narrowed to 55…165); beard `500×410` → `680×440` (4 colour columns from x=300, so the style list's 240px label box clears the grid); skin `420×360` → `600×380` (6 columns from x=24). BACK moved to y=375 (hair/beard) and y=312 (skin) so the taller 24px rows stay clear of it, and the rows were computed against the bottom band (`RenderPanel` draws it at `height - 30`).
   - **Ampersands:** the style names were written as `Short Beard &amp; Mustache` / `Long Beard &amp; Mustache`, and TazUO does **not** decode HTML entities in gump HTML (the same reason the ZorkPad prompt uses a literal `>`), so the panel displayed the literal text "…&amp;…". Both names now carry a plain `&` and read "Short Beard & Mustache" / "Long Beard & Mustache".
   - **Verified:** full script compile (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts` — 5,179 files) with the .NET 4.0 `csc` exactly as `ScriptCompiler` does (`/d:x64 /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0 /d:NEWTIMERS /d:NEWPARENT`, `World.exe` plus every `Assemblies.cfg` reference) — **0 errors, 11 warnings** (the documented baseline, none in the edited file), and the script assembly was produced.
   - Backups: `Backups/HenchmanCustomizeGump.cs.2026-11-25-200834.bak`.


227. **Henchman customization — paperdoll kept the old gender after a female→male switch** — 1 script file: `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Civilized/Comrades/HenchmanCustomizeGump.cs`.
   - **Symptom:** switching a female henchman to male updated the world sprite and unlocked the Beard menu, but the paperdoll avatar (and its female armour variants) stayed female. Male→female always looked right.
   - **Root cause (client side, not the gump):** `PaperDollInteractable.UpdateUI` (TazUO `src/ClassicUO.Client/Game/UI/Controls/PaperDollInteractable.cs`, ClassicUO `src/ClassicUO.Client/Game/UI/Controls/PaperDollInteractable.cs`) matches bodies `0x0191`/`0x0193`, the elf/gargoyle/shroud bodies and `0x03DB` explicitly and then falls back to `mobile.IsFemale ? 0x000D : 0x000C` — and the male human body `0x0190` is only covered by that fallback. `Mobile.IsFemale` is a cached field the client fills from the graphic in `Mobile.CheckGraphicChange()`, which `ObjectHelpers.UpdateGameObject` only calls in its `created` branch. The update branch for a mobile the client already knows assigns `mobile.Graphic`, `FixHue` and `Flags` but never re-runs `CheckGraphicChange`, so after the 0x78 body update `Graphic` is `0x0190` (sprite turns male) while `IsFemale` still says female (paperdoll needs `IsFemale == false` for `0x0190`). A skin-tone swatch makes the repro deterministic because a hue change already removes and re-adds the mobile (`Mobile.ProcessDelta` sets `sendRemove` for `MobileDelta.Hue`), so the client rebuilds the henchman with `IsFemale == true` from its female body; the following gender switch is a body-only update and the paperdoll never learns about it. The server state, packets and deed were all correct.
   - **Fix:** added `HenchmanCustomizeGumpBase.ResendBodyToClients()` — `m_Henchman.SendRemovePacket(); m_Henchman.SendIncomingPacket();` — and both `HenchmanCustomizeGenderGump.OnResponse` branches call it after `ProcessDelta()`/`SaveAppearance()`. The remove makes the client drop its cached mobile and the `MobileIncoming` creates it again from the current body, which re-runs `CheckGraphicChange` and fixes `IsFemale`; this is the same remove/re-add the core performs for a hue change.
   - **Trade-off:** the client disposes an open paperdoll for that henchman when it processes the remove (`Mobile.Destroy()` closes `PaperDollGump`/`ModernPaperdoll` for non-player mobiles), exactly as a skin-tone swatch already does, so the window has to be re-opened once and then shows the new gender. **#228** later re-opens it automatically with `ShowPaperdoll`.
   - **Proper client fix (for upstream / to drop the workaround later):** call `mobile.CheckGraphicChange();` next to `mobile.Graphic = (ushort)(graphic & 0x3FFF);` in the mobile update branch of `ObjectHelpers.UpdateGameObject` (TazUO `Network/PacketHandlers/Helpers/ObjectHelpers.cs`, ClassicUO `UpdateGameObject` in `Network/PacketHandlers.cs`), matching what the `created` branch and the item branch (`item.CheckGraphicChange(item.AnimIndex)`) already do. With that in place a body-only update refreshes the paperdoll and `ResendBodyToClients()` becomes redundant.
   - **Verified:** full script compile (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`, 5,179 files) with the .NET 4.0 `csc` against `World.exe` (`x64`, `Framework_2_0`, `Framework_4_0`, `NEWTIMERS`, `NEWPARENT`, every `Assemblies.cfg` reference) — **0 errors, 11 warnings** (the documented baseline, none in the edited file), before and after the change; the emitted script assembly was produced both times (`Scripts.dll`, 15,957,504 bytes, identical size).
   - Backups: `Backups/HenchmanCustomizeGump.cs.2026-11-25-204620.bak`.


228. **Henchman customization — 32-character name/title fields, and the paperdoll follows the customization** — 2 script files: `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Civilized/Comrades/HenchmanCustomizeGump.cs`, `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Civilized/Comrades/HenchmanInfoGump.cs`.
   - **Fields were too short (root cause):** `Sanitize` truncated to 16 characters and both `AddTextEntry` calls capped at 16, while the vendor titles henchmen are created with (`TavernPatrons.GetTitle`/`GetEvilTitle`) run to **28** characters — `"the " + aKiller + " of " + kKiller` (case 106) gives "the Executioner of Minotaurs" (28), and the fixed lists reach "of the Malevolent Society" (23) and "of the Vampire's Tomb" (21) — so a pre-existing title was cut mid-word the moment the Name/Title gump was opened and saved.
   - **New limit:** `HenchmanCustomizeGumpBase.NameTitleMaxLength = 32` now drives both `Sanitize` and the two text entries. 32 keeps every shipped title intact with a little room for typed text and is also the most that still renders in full: a 32-character name plus a 32-character title is 65 characters, which fits on one line in the 600px info-gump header and wraps to at most two lines in the main gump's header.
   - **Layout:** the NAME/TITLE panel grew from `370×240` to `480×240` with the entries at x=90 and 360px wide (was 250px) so all 32 characters stay visible while typing, and BACK moved from x=200 to x=300 to keep the pair centred. The main customization gump's name/title header block is `340×40` (was `330×36`) so a long name + title wraps onto two lines clear of the first option row at y=85, and `HenchmanInfoGump`'s header block is 600px wide (was 400) for the same reason.
   - **Paperdoll:** new `HenchmanCustomizeGumpBase.ShowPaperdoll( beholder, henchman )` sends the core paperdoll packet — `DisplayPaperdoll` plus the henchman's item OPLs, exactly the pair `Server.Misc.Paperdoll` sends when a player double-clicks a mobile. The client opens the paperdoll when it has none (at the position it saved when the last one closed) and otherwise refreshes the title and canLift state. It is sent when customization starts (the **Customize** button in `HenchmanInfoGump`, before the customization gump), after a successful name/title save (so the paperdoll's own title caption follows a rename), and after every gender and skin-tone change — both of those make the client destroy and re-create the henchman (`ResendBodyToClients` for gender, the core's hue `sendRemove` for skin) and that closes a non-player's paperdoll, so the re-send is what keeps the window on screen and shows the new body/hue.
   - **Verified:** full script compile (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`, 5,179 files) with the .NET 4.0 `csc` against `World.exe` (`x64`, `Framework_2_0`, `Framework_4_0`, `NEWTIMERS`, `NEWPARENT`, every `Assemblies.cfg` reference) — **0 errors, 11 warnings** (the documented baseline, none in the edited files), and the script assembly was produced (`Scripts.dll`, 15,958,016 bytes).
   - Backups: `Backups/HenchmanCustomizeGump.cs.2026-11-25-210349.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanInfoGump.cs.2026-11-25-210349.bak`.


229. **Henchmen — lent gear keeps its condition, and the durability warnings are removed** — 4 script files: `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Civilized/Comrades/HenchmanFunctions.cs`, `HenchmanItem.cs`, `Source/Scripts/Items/Weapons/BaseWeapon.cs`, `Source/Scripts/System/Skills/ArmsLore.cs`.
   - **Why:** gear handed to a henchman is the player's own equipment, and the henchman is a clientless NPC that cannot repair anything. `BaseWeapon.OnHit` wore a wielded weapon whenever `Utility.Random( 20 + Density ) == 0` (1 in 24 for an iron weapon) on every successful hit and, worse, **guaranteed** one point of wear on every melee hit against `ToxicElemental` ("an acid elemental"), `Slime`, `GreenSlime`, `BlackPudding`, `LavaPuddle`, `AcidPuddle` and `Xenomorph` — that acid branch runs before `ArmsLore.AvoidDurabilityHit`, and henchmen have no Arms Lore skill anyway. At `MaxHitPoints < 1` the weapon called `Delete()`, which `Mobile.OnItemRemoved` turned into `HenchmanFunctions.OnHenchmanItemRemoved`, so the item also vanished from the deed's `HenchEquipment` stash: the player's weapon was destroyed for good.
   - **Weapons:** `BaseWeapon.OnHit` now requires `!Server.Misc.HenchmanFunctions.IgnoresDurability( attacker )` on the `m_MaxHits > 0 && Density != Density.None` guard, so the random wear roll and the acid-blood scar are both skipped while a henchman is the wielder. `BaseRanged` and `BaseWizardStaff` call `base.OnHit`, so bows and staves are covered too; the armour/shield block later in the same method (`AbsorbDamageAOS`) never touched weapons.
   - **New helper:** `HenchmanFunctions.IgnoresDurability( Mobile )` returns true for `HenchmanFighter`, `HenchmanArcher`, `HenchmanWizard` and `HenchmanMonster` (the same four types `CanSpendHenchmanPoints` lists) and false for everything else, including `HenchmanFamiliar`, `PlayerMobile` and plain `BaseCreature`s. No per-item flag and no serialization change: the test is made against the holder at the moment of a hit, so an item taken back to a player wears normally again.
   - **Armour, shields and clothing:** `ArmsLore.AvoidDurabilityHit` now returns `true` before its Arms Lore roll when the `Parent` is a henchman. That is the one hook `BaseArmor.OnHit`, `BaseShield.OnHit` and `BaseClothing.OnHit` all consult, so it also covers the `CraftResources.GetRandomItemWithDurability` corrosion used by `AbsorbDamageAOS` and `CrystalGoliath.AlterMeleeDamageTo`. Player Arms Lore and its "You notice your equipment is especially durable." message are unchanged.
   - **Warnings removed:** `HenchmanFunctions.CheckDurability` (and its `HenchmanItem.OnDoubleClick`, bandage and accepted gold/treasure call sites) is gone, so “Some of my equipment is looking worn.” / “…in danger of breaking.” no longer appear. Nothing else referenced it.
   - **Out of scope, deliberately:** the acid branch still bypasses Arms Lore for players, and the tool-wear path (`Lumberjacking`'s axe) plus failed-`Repair` weakening are untouched — a henchman never chops wood or repairs.
   - **Verified:** full script compile (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`) with the .NET 4.0 `csc` exactly as `ScriptCompiler` does (`/d:x64 /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0 /d:NEWTIMERS /d:NEWPARENT`, `World.exe` plus every `Assemblies.cfg` reference) — **0 errors, 11 warnings** (the documented baseline, none in the edited files), and `Scripts.dll` was produced (15,957,504 bytes). A reflection harness over the built assembly passed **10/10** type assertions (`IgnoresDurability` → true for the four henchman types, false for `HenchmanFamiliar`, `PlayerMobile`, `BaseCreature` and `null`; `CheckDurability` no longer exists), and an IL scan confirmed `BaseWeapon.OnHit` and `ArmsLore.AvoidDurabilityHit` call `IgnoresDurability`, that `BaseArmor`/`BaseShield`/`BaseClothing.OnHit` still route through `AvoidDurabilityHit`, and that `HenchmanItem` has no `CheckDurability` call left.
   - Backups: `Backups/BaseWeapon.cs.2026-11-25-220713.bak`, `Backups/ArmsLore.cs.2026-11-25-220713.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanFunctions.cs.2026-11-25-220713.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanItem.cs.2026-11-25-220713.bak`.


230. **Henchmen — mage henchmen refuse bows, crossbows and throwing gloves** — 1 script file: `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Civilized/Comrades/HenchmanFunctions.cs`.
   - **Symptom:** a mage henchman only asked whether the held item allows equipped casting (`Item.AllowEquipedCast`, which for a `BaseWeapon` is `Attributes.SpellChanneling`), so a **spell channelling** bow, crossbow or pair of `ThrowingGloves` (created with `SpellChanneling = 1`) was accepted even though the mage can never fire it.
   - **Fix:** inside the `HenchmanWizard` branch of `ValidateHenchmanEquip`, a hand item that is a bow/crossbow (`IsBowOrCrossbow`, the helper the archer already uses — `BaseRanged` with `Arrow`/`Bolt` ammo) or implements `IThrowingGloves` (`ThrowingGloves`, `GiftThrowingGloves`, `LevelThrowingGloves`) is refused with the spoken line **“I have no idea how to handle such a weapon.”** The check sits **before** the `AllowEquipedCast` test, so it applies whether or not the weapon is spell channelling; every other hand item still goes through the old cast test unchanged (“I can't cast spells while holding that.”).
   - **Scope:** mage henchmen only. The archer keeps “Give me a bow or crossbow.” for non-bows and the fighter keeps “Give me a proper melee weapon.”. Harpoons (`HarpoonRope`), mage-eye staves (`MageEye`), blowguns/darts and melee weapons match neither test, so they behave exactly as before. `HenchmanFunctions.RestoringGear` still bypasses validation when saved gear is re-equipped, so a bow already stashed on an existing mage henchman is not purged — remove it once by hand if needed.
   - **Verified:** full script compile (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`) with the .NET 4.0 `csc` exactly as `ScriptCompiler` does (`/d:x64 /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0 /d:NEWTIMERS /d:NEWPARENT`, `World.exe` plus every `Assemblies.cfg` reference) — **0 errors, 11 warnings** (the documented baseline, none in the edited file). A reflection harness over the built assembly checked 34 item types: `IsBowOrCrossbow` is true for all 22 bow/crossbow classes (plain, gift and legendary variants) and `IThrowingGloves` for the three glove classes; `ValidateHenchmanEquip` returns **false** for every one of those against a `HenchmanWizard`, while 9 controls (harpoons, gift/legendary staves, katana, longsword, mace, wizard staff) match neither test. The IL of `ValidateHenchmanEquip` shows `call IsHandItem` → `call IsBowOrCrossbow` → `isinst IThrowingGloves` → `ldstr "I have no idea how to handle such a weapon."` → `callvirt AllowEquipedCast`, confirming the new refusal runs before the spell-channelling rule.
   - Backups: `Backups/HenchmanFunctions.cs.2026-11-25-222528.bak`.


231. **Henchmen — harpoons are refused by every henchman class** — 1 script file: `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Civilized/Comrades/HenchmanFunctions.cs`.
   - **What a harpoon is:** `Harpoon` (and `GiftHarpoon`/`LevelHarpoon`) is a `BaseRanged` marked with the `IHarpoon` interface; it fires `HarpoonRope` and trains Marksmanship, so it is neither a melee weapon nor a bow/crossbow. A **spell channelling** harpoon was therefore accepted by a mage henchman (the cast test only asks whether casting is allowed), while fighters and archers only rejected it indirectly — through the fighter's Swords/Bludgeoning/Fencing skill test and the archer's Arrow/Bolt ammo test.
   - **New helper:** `HenchmanFunctions.IsHarpoon( Item )` returns `item is IHarpoon`, covering `Harpoon`, `GiftHarpoon` and `LevelHarpoon` with one test.
   - **Mage:** harpoons join the second list — `IsBowOrCrossbow( item ) || IsHarpoon( item ) || item is IThrowingGloves` — so a harpoon is refused with **“I have no idea how to handle such a weapon.”** before the `AllowEquipedCast` test, spell channelling or not (a channelling harpoon gets the same line; it is not treated as a “not spell channelling” refusal).
   - **Fighter / archer:** their generic tests now exclude harpoons explicitly — `( IsHarpoon( item ) || !IsFighterWeapon( item ) )` → “Give me a proper melee weapon.” and `( IsHarpoon( item ) || !IsBowOrCrossbow( item ) )` → “Give me a bow or crossbow.”. Every other item behaves exactly as before (the new term is false unless the item is a harpoon), and harpoons are now refused independently of the skill/ammo tables.
   - **Verified:** full script compile (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`) with the .NET 4.0 `csc` exactly as `ScriptCompiler` does (`/d:x64 /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0 /d:NEWTIMERS /d:NEWPARENT`, `World.exe` plus every `Assemblies.cfg` reference) — **0 errors, 11 warnings** (the documented baseline, none in the edited file), `Scripts.dll` produced (15,958,016 bytes). A reflection harness over the built assembly: `IsHarpoon` is true for `Harpoon`, `GiftHarpoon` and `LevelHarpoon` and false for bows, throwing gloves, staves and melee weapons; `ValidateHenchmanEquip` returns **false** for all three harpoon classes against `HenchmanFighter`, `HenchmanArcher` **and** `HenchmanWizard`, while the same harness still reaches the accept path for an archer + bow, a fighter + katana/mace and a mage + channelling staff, so no other item was caught. The IL of `ValidateHenchmanEquip` shows `call IsHarpoon` in all three branches, with the mage sequence `IsHandItem` → `IsBowOrCrossbow` → `IsHarpoon` → `isinst IThrowingGloves` → `ldstr "I have no idea how to handle such a weapon."` → `callvirt AllowEquipedCast`.
   - Backups: `Backups/HenchmanFunctions.cs.2026-11-25-234641.bak`.


232. **Henchmen — blowguns are refused by every henchman class; Mage Eye staves need no ammo for a henchman (finding)** — 1 script file: `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Civilized/Comrades/HenchmanFunctions.cs`.
   - **What a blowgun is:** `Fukiya` is a plain `Item, INinjaWeapon` on `Layer.OneHanded` that is loaded and fired through the Ninjitsu weapon UI (`NinjaWeapons.AttemptShoot`) instead of a normal weapon swing; `FukiyaDarts` is its ammunition (`Item, INinjaAmmo`, `Layer.Invalid`).
   - **New helper:** `HenchmanFunctions.IsBlowgun( Item )` returns `item is Fukiya || item is FukiyaDarts`. The darts are listed for completeness only — as `Layer.Invalid` ammunition they never reach the equip rules.
   - **Fighter / archer / mage:** `IsBlowgun( item )` joins `IsHarpoon( item )` in all three rules, so a blowgun is refused with “Give me a proper melee weapon.”, “Give me a bow or crossbow.” and “I have no idea how to handle such a weapon.” respectively (the mage rule still runs before its spell-channelling test). Every other item behaves as before.
   - **Finding — Mage Eye staves (no change made):** `BaseRanged.OnFired` and `BaseWizardStaff.OnFired` keep the ammo check inside `if ( attacker.Player )`, so a henchman (`Player == false`) fires `GiftStave`/`GiftSceptre`/`LevelStave`/`LevelSceptre` (`AmmoType = MageEye`) and `WizardStaff`/`WizardStick` without consuming or even owning a mage eye — the same way archer henchmen shoot without arrows. The staves are ranged weapons (`DefMaxRange` 10 for a stave, 8 for a sceptre, `DefSkill = Marksmanship`) and `Mobile.CombatTimer` swings any wielded weapon at `MaxRange` while `MageAI` keeps casting, so a mage henchman does actually fire them; `HenchmanWizard` trains Marksmanship (min 31) and `BaseWeapon.WizardCheck` only needs Magery/Necromancy/Elementalism Base ≥ 30, both of which it meets. The remaining gate is `AllowEquipedCast`: `WizardStaff`/`WizardStick` set `Attributes.SpellChanneling = 1` in their constructors, while the gift/legendary staves only pass when their rolled properties include spell channelling (otherwise the mage says “I can't cast spells while holding that.”). Recorded here so the intentional free ammo is not mistaken for a bug later.
   - **Verified:** full script compile (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`) with the .NET 4.0 `csc` exactly as `ScriptCompiler` does (`/d:x64 /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0 /d:NEWTIMERS /d:NEWPARENT`, `World.exe` plus every `Assemblies.cfg` reference) — **0 errors, 11 warnings** (the documented baseline, none in the edited file), `Scripts.dll` produced (15,958,016 bytes). A reflection harness over the built assembly: `IsBlowgun` is true for `Fukiya` and `FukiyaDarts` and false for harpoons, bows, throwing gloves, melee weapons and `MageEye`; `ValidateHenchmanEquip` returns **false** for a `Fukiya` against `HenchmanFighter`, `HenchmanArcher` **and** `HenchmanWizard`, while the controls (archer + bow, fighter + katana, mage + staff) still reach their accept paths. The IL of `ValidateHenchmanEquip` shows `call IsBlowgun` in all three branches, after `call IsHarpoon` and before each message.
   - Backups: `Backups/HenchmanFunctions.cs.2026-11-25-235306.bak`.


233. **Henchmen — mage henchmen warn when they are only carrying a weapon** — 2 script files: `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Civilized/Comrades/HenchmanFunctions.cs`, `HenchmanWizard.cs`.
   - **Rule:** when a mage henchman accepts a hand weapon whose `BaseWeapon.Skill` is not `SkillName.Marksmanship` (a spell channelling sword, mace, dagger, …), the weapon is carried but never used, so the henchman says **“I'll carry this, but don't expect me to fight hand-to-hand.”** The Mage-Eye staves (`WizardStaff`/`WizardStick`/`GiftStave`/`GiftSceptre`/`LevelStave`/`LevelSceptre`) all report Marksmanship and never trigger it.
   - **Timing:** this follows the existing meditation-warning pattern so the line can only appear after a real equip. `ValidateHenchmanEquip` clears the new `m_HandToHandWarning` together with `m_MeditationWarning` at the top of every call, sets it at the end of the accepted hand-item branch, and clears it again on the Strength refusal; `HenchmanFunctions.AnnounceHandToHandWarning` speaks the line and resets the flag; `HenchmanWizard.EquipItem` calls it directly after `AnnounceMeditationWarning`, i.e. only once `base.EquipItem` has returned true. A bounced/refused item never produces the message, and a saved-gear restore (`m_RestoringGear`) stays silent because validation short-circuits.
   - **Scope:** mage henchmen only — the fighter fights hand-to-hand with the weapons it accepts, and the archer only accepts bows/crossbows (Marksmanship). Non-weapon hand items that pass `AllowEquipedCast` (magic lanterns, torches, trinket lanterns) are unaffected because the check requires a `BaseWeapon`.
   - **Verified:** full script compile (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts`) with the .NET 4.0 `csc` exactly as `ScriptCompiler` does (`/d:x64 /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0 /d:NEWTIMERS /d:NEWPARENT`, `World.exe` plus every `Assemblies.cfg` reference) — **0 errors, 11 warnings** (the documented baseline, none in the edited files), `Scripts.dll` produced (15,958,016 bytes). A reflection harness over the built assembly (with `Core.Expansion` raised to `ML` in-process so `AOS` attributes resolve, and `AosAttributes`/`AosWeaponAttributes`/`m_StatMods` injected into otherwise uninitialised items/mobiles) gave: spell-channelling `Katana` → accepted, flag **true**; spell-channelling `Mace` → accepted, flag **true**; `WizardStaff` → accepted, flag **false**; `GiftStave` → accepted, flag **false**; non-channelling `Katana` → refused, flag false; `Fukiya` and `Bow` → refused, flag false; `TrinketLantern` (non-weapon hand item) → accepted, flag **false**; `AnnounceHandToHandWarning` cleared a pre-set flag. The IL of `ValidateHenchmanEquip` shows the flag reset, the `AllowEquipedCast` → `BaseWeapon.get_Skill` → `stsfld m_HandToHandWarning` sequence and the strength-stage reset, and `HenchmanWizard.EquipItem` calls `AnnounceHandToHandWarning`.
   - Backups: `Backups/HenchmanFunctions.cs.2026-11-26-002031.bak`, `Backups/HenchmanWizard.cs.2026-11-26-002031.bak`.



---

## Current Behaviour

### Artefact Book (`ArtefactBook`)
- Item: `Tome of Wondrous Artifacts`, graphic `0x22C5`, hue `MementoCoin.MementoHue` (2141).
- `Charges` default `1`; set negative for unlimited.
- Catalogue = `ArtifactQuestList` entries filtered to `ArtefactLevel.StandardArtefact` (about 317 items), cached in `ArtefactBook.Entries`.
- Gump: 16 artefacts per page, two columns, no in-book title, prev/next arrows with a right-aligned "Page X of Y" next to the next arrow. Clicking an artefact opens a detail page with the icon on the left page and, on the right page, a "UNIQUE POWER" description (for artefacts with special abilities) plus the full stat sheet (`ItemProps.ItemProperties`); Claim (a checkmark-box button with its label right-aligned beside it) grants the item and consumes a charge, Back returns to the list.

### Memento Coin (`MementoCoin`)
- Stackable, hue `2141`, `DefaultWeight 0.02`.
- Displays as "Memento Coin" when the stack size is 1, and "Memento Coins" (with the amount) when 2 or more.

### Memento Collector (`MementoCollector`)
- Random male/female with matching `NameList` name; title `"the artefact collector"`. Name forced to the merchant blue (`Notoriety.GetHue(Notoriety.Innocent)`).
- Team `0` (the same neutral team town vendors use), re-asserted in `OnAfterSpawn`.
- Invulnerable / untargetable: derives from `BaseNPC` (always-invulnerable), so `Blessed` is kept; `Deserialize` re-asserts it. Cannot be targeted or harmed.
- Wanders (`CantWalk` not set).
- Wears dark-gray leather (`0x83B`): gorget, arms/sleeves, gloves, legs and chest; a random cloth hat (floppy, wide-brim, cap, skull cap, bandana, straw, tall straw, wizard, feathered, tricorne, pirate, hood, witch or bonnet), cloak and sash in hue `2141`; dark boots; a lit lantern (hue `2141`) in hand.
- On spawn each armour slot has a 50% chance to be replaced by a random standard artefact of the same `Layer` (at least one swap is guaranteed; gender-specific pieces are skipped). It also wields a random one-handed standard artefact weapon (`Layer.OneHanded`), which coexists with the lantern (`Layer.TwoHanded`).
- Idle emotes (yawn, sigh, cough, etc.) play when a player is within 12 tiles, on a 20–45s cooldown.
- Coin payouts by artefact tier: Legendary `15`, Standard `11`, Artifact `7`, Decorative `3`; non-artefacts are refused.
- Spawns: one collector per custom-merchant stall via `Info/Spawns/Merchant.map` (34 entries), loaded on `[buildworld` when `MySettings.S_CustomMerchant` is true.

### Talk / trade rules
- Dropping an artefact: deletes it and gives the tier payout in `MementoCoin`s.
- Dropping 75+ `MementoCoin`s: consumes 75 (refunds the remainder) and gives an `ArtefactBook`.

### Boat / Magic Carpet speed (`BaseBoat.ApplySpeedBoost`)
- Sailing vessels: driven only by `SkillName.Seafaring` (Magery has no effect) — 50/75/100/125 skill gives 1/2/3/4 boosts (25 ms per boost off the 250 ms fast interval).
- Magic carpets (`MagicCarpetA`–`MagicCarpetI`, detected by `BaseBoat.isCarpet`): driven only by `SkillName.Magery` (Seafaring has no effect); the same 50/75/100/125 tier table applies to the carpet owner's Magery.
- Overall maximum is `BaseBoat.MAX_SPEED_BOOSTS = 6` (also `Constants.BOAT_SPEED_MAX_LEVEL`), so the Avatar "Fast Seaman" upgrade and other sources can reach 6 on sailing vessels.
- Magic carpets are additionally clamped to `BaseBoat.MAX_CARPET_SPEED_BOOSTS = 4`, so a carpet can never exceed 4 bonus speed steps no matter what the Magery or Avatar values are.
- Slow movement and slow drift are unchanged (no skill boost applied).

### Compile shortcuts (`Compile-Linux.sh` / `Compile-Windows.bat`)
- Both live directly in the `World` folder, next to `Data`, `Info`, `Saves`, `Source`, etc.
- Base folder = the parent of `World` (the git repository root). Each shortcut runs `git pull` there first, then runs the compiler from `Source/Tools`.
  - Linux: `Compile-Linux.sh` → `bash Source/Tools/compile-world-linux.sh` (Mono `mcs`, output `World/WorldLinux.exe`). Run with `./Compile-Linux.sh`, `bash Compile-Linux.sh`, or double-click.
  - Windows: `Compile-Windows.bat` → `call Source\Tools\compile-world-win.bat` (.NET 4.0 `csc`, output `World\World.exe`). Double-click to run.
- Flow: `git pull` (base folder) → if it fails, abort with a message → otherwise compile from `Source/Tools` → report done.
- Both compilers use paths relative to `Source/Tools`, hence the shortcuts `cd`/`pushd` into that folder before invoking them.
- **Double-click on Linux:** Dolphin **executes** `Compile-Linux.sh` on double-click because `~/.config/kiorc` has `[Executable scripts] behaviourOnLaunch=execute`. That launch has no terminal, so the script re-opens itself in `konsole -e bash …` (build output visible; the window closes automatically when the build finishes) and sets up `PATH` (Homebrew/Linuxbrew) first. Console runs already have a TTY and skip the Konsole relaunch.
- The double-click behaviour is a per-user KIO setting (`~/.config/kiorc`, not part of the repo); set `behaviourOnLaunch=alwaysAsk` there to get the "execute or open?" prompt back instead.

### Dungeon Master's Guide (`DungeonMastersGuide`)
- Item graphic `0x3046`, name "Dungeon Masters Guide", weight 1.0; obtainable via `Loot.cs` and required for the `GygaxStatue` quest.
- Double-click opens a two-column gump listing all dungeons from `Worlds.GetDungeonListing` (row count = `Worlds.DungeonCount`, currently 85); each row shows world / dungeon name / sextant coordinate, plus a close button (button ID 0).
- Clicking a dungeon opens `Sextants.MapGump` at its entrance **only if the player carries a Sextant or MagicSextant**; otherwise the player is messaged "You need a sextant to locate this dungeon." (the raw `MapGump` renders empty without one).
- Fixed rows: "the Dungeon of the Lich King" and "the Dungeon of the Mad Archmage" now resolve to their real entrances instead of (0,0).

### Chat (Knives Chat3)
- Open with the client **chat button** (wired via `EventSink.ChatRequest` → routes through the Public channel exactly like `[c`) or the commands `[c` / `[chat` (Public channel), `[Va`/`[ViewAll`, `[Fri`/`[Friends`, `[Ma`/`[Mail`, `[pm`/`[msg`.
- Channels (`Public`, `Staff`, `Guild`, `Alliance`, `Faction`, `Irc`, `Multi`) load from `Saves/ChatBeta8/Channels.bin`; if that file is missing or fails to load, `PredefinedChannels()` is now a fallback so chat still works.
- `[c` dispatches to the Public channel's `OnChat` (empty text opens the list); chat gumps build via `GumpPlus` (deferred to a `Timer.DelayCall`).
- Known cosmetic leftover: `General.LoadHelpFile()` only clears the help table, so in-chat "Help" topics report "Help Error" (help contents aren't loaded from a file).

### Hair stylist / barber (`CustomHairstylist`)
- The context option (`BarberEntry`) opens `HairstylistBuyGump`, listing services: change hairstyle (500), facial hair (500), and several hair-color palettes (50–1000).
- The list now always shows the applicable services regardless of gold (facial-hair options are hidden for female characters). Choosing one charges the price from the backpack and opens the hairstyle/hue picker; if you can't afford it, the vendor says "You cannot afford my services for that style."

### House lawn / yard tools (`LawnTools`)
- `LawnTools` opens `LawnGump` and places `LawnItem`s around the player's house; placement is validated by `LawnTarget.ValidatePlacement`.
- You must be standing inside a house you own/co-own (`BaseHouse.FindHouseAt(from)` + `IsCoOwner`). Region is **ignored entirely**, but the target tile must fall within the house footprint (via `house.Components`) plus the `Remodeling` margin (**15** tiles per side).

### Umbra — murderers / criminals / the aristocracy
- In `UmbraRegion` ("the Undercity of Umbra"), guards do **not** attack players who are murderers (`Kills > 0`) **while they are not currently criminal** — `IntelligentAction.GetMyEnemies` returns false for them there, so `TownGuards.IsEnemy` returns false.
- **Entering the Undercity clears the criminal flag immediately** (`UmbraRegion.OnEnter`), so a criminal who walks in is not attacked by the guards.
- **The aristocracy is above the law:** a player with **Fame ≥ 10000** and **Karma ≤ -10000** *inside Umbra* cannot become criminal at all (`PlayerMobile.CriminalAction` plus `GetPlayerInfo.MakeCriminal` at every raw assignment site), and the exemption covers their pets and summons (`BaseCreature.CriminalAction` and the extra `GetMyEnemies` checks), so they can attack/kill Umbra shopkeepers with impunity while their pets and summons are ignored by the guards.
- Killing an Umbra NPC still adds a murder count (`Kills`); Umbra guards ignore murderers who are not criminal.
- A player who does **not** meet those requirements can still become criminal in Umbra by committing a fresh crime (attacking an NPC/innocent sets `Criminal`), guards then respond, and their summons are treated as enemies exactly as before.
- Tavern patrons occasionally gossip: *"It's said that in Umbra the aristocracy is above the law."*
- NPCs still talk/teach/interact (`CheckChattingAccess`) and vendors still buy/sell (`CheckVendorAccess`) with murderers/criminals in Umbra (Umbra counts as a "public" region in those checks).
- The `[wanted` gump's **SAFE PLACES** list always includes **Umbra Undercity**, for murderers, criminals, death knights and monster races alike.
- Only initiating hostility is suppressed: a guard still defends itself if the player attacks it. Outside Umbra nothing changes.

### Moongates — dynamic slot 30 (`GateMoon` / "Vortex - Umbra Cave")
- The 30th moongate destination is dynamic. Precedence: **Druid's Glade** (non-evil, `Karma >= 0`, `Kills < 1`, discovered Sosaria) → race Vortexes (evil sea/dead/demon) → **"Vortex - Umbra Cave"** for a **negative-karma** (`Karma < 0`) player with no other option in that slot → otherwise placeholder.
- "Vortex - Umbra Cave" teleports to `5254, 1063, 5` on Sosaria (same entrance as the evil-dead race vortex).
- The red "precluded" CANCEL icon now only appears on the Druid's Glade entry (non-evil players with negative karma or murder counts), so the Umbra Cave fallback is selectable.
- The **Umbra door** (`RaceTeleporter`, `Name == "umbra"`) shares this access via `GateMoon.CanUseUmbraCave( m )`: anyone who can reach the "Vortex - Umbra Cave" moongate can pass the door; everyone else still gets "The doors seem to be rusted shut!".

### Lawn / Shanty decor removal — no line-of-sight gate
- Refunding/removing a lawn or shanty decoration no longer requires line of sight: `LawnRemoveTarget` and `ShantyRemoveTarget` set `CheckLOS = false` (matching the placement targets). You must still own/co-own the house and target an item that belongs to that house (`item.House == m_House`).

### Memento Collector — gear
- The collector is dressed entirely in **standard artefacts** (armour set, boots and helm each a random same-layer `StandardArtefact`), with dark leather only as a fallback if a layer has no artefact.
- The **hued cloak and sash** are kept as the identity pieces.
- A **lit lantern is always carried** (added after the one-handed artefact weapon; the lantern uses the two-handed layer, so both are held).

### Exodus boss — barrier & spell retaliation
- The magic barrier is up while Exodus is at/above **90%** HP (`CanUseField`); `OnThink` re-raises it only when he's back at full HP (`!IsHurt`).
- Hitting the barrier with melee reduces that hit to 75%, and pets that melee the barrier are one-shot by `SendEBoltOnPet`.
