- **Spell reflect / retaliation:** 25% of incoming spells are reflected back at the caster (`CheckReflect`), and 40% of the time a spell hits he fires an EBolt retaliation (`SendEBolt`).
- **Periodic explosion:** every **8 s** (while he is **in combat**) he targets a **random player within 20 tiles** and drops a single **3×3** block (9 tiles) centered on them, each tile telegraphed **2 s** earlier — each blast (radius 2) hits its area for 35–40 fire — **players only** (Exodus is never damaged).
- **Never flees:** he runs `OmniAI` (`ChangeAIType` routes `AI_Mage`/`AI_Melee`/etc. to `OmniAI`), and OmniAI's flee check excludes `Exodus`; he's also immune to fear/flee effects (`BeginFlee`/`CheckFlee` overridden), so neither low HP nor Dispel Evil/Cause Fear makes him run.
- **Gold on death (champion-style):** when he dies he scatters gold across a 12-tile radius — one `Gold(300–800 × goldCutRate)` pile per tile, each after a random 0–10 s delay, with a small burst effect.
- **Horde minions:** his corpse also drops **two `HoardMinionFamiliarItem`** (champion-style).
- **Spawning:** on spawn Exodus relocates to a random dungeon (`Worlds.MoveToRandomDungeon` in `OnAfterSpawn`), with the burn-away spawn visual kept.
- **Stats (TEMPORARY — testing):** core stat block tripled — STR 900-1260, DEX 531-765, INT 453-750, Hits 2400-3000 (~6× original), Damage 20-25 (base; strength-scaled ≈105-159 pre-resist, ≈32-48 vs 70 resist).
- **Skills (TEMPORARY — testing):** Anatomy/Psychology/MagicResist/**Magery**/Tactics/FistFighting = **125**.

### Glistening ore veins (Rich Veins)
- A "glistening ore vein" (`RichVeinMineable`) is **only visible to miners with a trained (base) Mining of 65 or more** — `SendInfoTo` suppresses the world packet and OPL for anyone below 65, so ineligible players genuinely never receive the item. This matches the `Mining.Base >= 65` requirement to mine a vein. Staff (`AccessLevel > Player`) always see veins.
- The proximity "glistening" sparkle (`0x3039`) is likewise sent **only to eligible miners** (it used to broadcast to everyone in range, which would have revealed an otherwise-invisible vein). The 100‑Mining sound cue is unchanged.
- Placement is unchanged: candidates come from `Data/RichVeins/<Map>.xml`, spawned by `RichVeinEngine` (50–70 min refresh, `MinDistance` 25).

### ZorkPad (vintage computer)
- `ZorkPad` — DataPad art (`0x27FB`/`0x27FC`), **always the green hue `0x85D`**; weight 1, `Technology`, emits `Circle150` light. Spawn it with `[add ZorkPad`.
- Double-click (within 4 tiles) opens a large (740×780) terminal: solid dark‑gray board frame with a black screen, green phosphor text, the screen clears when you move or type look (other actions append below), a typeable prompt (submit with the keyboard ENTER), and a clickable **QWERTY keyboard** at the bottom, centred on the gump, on the gray board (keys append to the input; BKSP/CLR/SPACE/ENTER).
- **Press Enter** in the prompt to submit a command (core `0xAC` handler → `Gump.OnTextEntry`); clicking the ENTER button also works. `help` lists verbs, `restart` resets.
- Runs a playable Zork I "White House" slice with a real parser and per-player progress (in memory; resets on server restart). `restart` re-rolls the game; `help` lists verbs.
- **Scoring & win:** authentic Zork I scoring — **350 points** = treasure values (143) + trophy‑case values (129) + room values (65) + the lit Lower Shaft (13), with **−10 per death**. `score` gives the original breakdown ("Your score is N (total of 350 points)…") and the original rank ladder (Beginner → … → Wizard → **Master Adventurer** at 350); reaching 350 triggers the **YOU HAVE WON** endgame. **Combat:** `attack` the troll, the thief (wherever he is) and the cyclops (fatal — use the magic word instead); you have 10 HP and respawn if killed. **Action routines ported from the original:** the wandering, robbing thief (and the loot he drops), the bat that throws you across the mine unless you carry garlic, coal‑gas explosions, the dumbwaiter (`raise`/`lower basket`), the mirror rooms (`rub mirror` swaps rooms and contents), and the egg/canary trap. Rooms are lit per the original `ONBIT` flags and darkness is dangerous (the grue is a 30 % chance per move). **Consumables behave:** the brass lantern lasts 185 lit turns before it burns out (three warnings, then it's dead), the matchbook holds six matches (and they blow out in the drafty shaft), the temple candles last 35 turns (and a gust in the Tiny Cave can snuff them), the elvish sword glows blue near villains, and `eat`/`drink` consume the garlic, lunch and bottled water. `throw <thing> [at <creature>]` works, including smashing the lantern, the 10 % chance a thrown knife frightens the thief off his bag, and Zork's joke death from throwing something at yourself. **The windable canary** calls a songbird in the forest and the brass bauble drops (that is how the treasure is obtained); containers have lids and capacities; the thief can be knocked out (dropping his stiletto) and revives to scramble away; and `pray`, `yell`, `zork`, `hello` and `swim` all answer as the original does. **Beyond the map:** `leap`/`jump` (safe from the tree, fatal where there is no way down), the forest's song bird, a provoked thief who stabs back and then withdraws when beaten, and the Living Room listing the trophy case's contents.


### Transmogrification (`Transmogrify*`)
- Files: `Systems/Transmogrify/TransmogrifyCatalog.cs`, `TransmogrifyGump.cs`, `TransmogrifyTestItem.cs`.
- **Test item:** `Transmogrification Scroll` — art `0x1F4C`, hue `0x48E`, weight 1, blessed. `[add TransmogrifyTestItem`.
- **Use:** double-click the scroll → target a piece of **armor or clothing** within 4 tiles → the picker opens listing only appearances that occupy the *same slot*, each named after its **base item** ("Leather Gorget", "Stuffed Gorget", "Plate Mempo") with no class or artifact names shown. Clicking an option **previews** it (highlighted in the list, art shown at the top, and the item swapped on the paperdoll model); **Apply** commits it, changing `ItemID` (appearance) only — stats, hue and durability are untouched.
- **Paperdoll window (the only preview):** a throw-away `Mobile` (the player's body, wearing only the target item unless **Gear on** is set) is shown in the client's paperdoll window, which the picker **opens by default**. It is never added to the world, so it is never saved and no other client sees it. It re-dresses live on each selection and is removed whenever the gump closes, is cancelled, applied or reverted. The window's position cannot be pinned by the server (packet `0x88` carries no coordinates), so park it once and the client will remember where it goes.
- **Gear toggle:** `Gear off` (default) shows a bare body with just the piece being changed; `Gear on` mirrors the rest of the player's outfit. Toggles live through `RemoveItem`/`EquipUpdate`, without closing the paperdoll.
- **Revert:** restores the item's recorded pre-transmog `ItemID` (the hue stays in the file but is not re-applied, so a later dye survives a revert) and clears its record. Every Apply first stores the item's current visuals in `World/Saves/Transmogrify/Originals.xml` (serial → ItemID + hue), written on every world save and pruned of deleted items. An item with nothing recorded answers "That item has no recorded original appearance."
- **How you get one:** `TransmogrifyGem` drops on **Paragons** — 12.5 % of kills at 0 luck, rising with the killer's Luck and capped at 45 % — placed in the corpse. The GM `TransmogrifyTestItem` scroll can still be `[add`-ed freely. The **gypsy lady's "Hire" option** also reshapes a targeted piece of armour or clothing, for a fee that scales with the item: **200** for plain gear, **1000** for anything unusual, **2000** for gift/artifact equipment, less her usual Begging discount; the fee is taken on Apply, not on opening the picker.
- **Single use:** the scroll is consumed only when **Apply** is pressed. Cancel/closing the gump, a bad or out-of-range target, an empty slot, or Apply with nothing selected all leave the scroll in the pack.
- **Refusals:** non-armor/clothing targets answer "That is not armor or clothing."; a slot with no known appearances answers with the slot name plus the catalogue totals (diagnostic).
- **Catalogue scope:** every `[Constructable]` `BaseArmor` / `BaseClothing` / `BaseWeapon` / `BaseTrinket` in the script assemblies — 1,342 classes, deduplicated per slot by art. Built lazily on first use and cached; `TransmogrifyCatalog.Rebuild()` refreshes it.
- **Known caveat:** option art offsets in the grid are fixed estimates, so very large graphics (shields, robes) can sit slightly off-centre until they are sized per art (e.g. via `AddImageTiledButton`, as `PolymorphGump` does).

### Fragment of Infinity (`FragmentOfInfinity`)
- **Item:** "Fragment of Infinity" — graphic `7964`, hue `1266`, weight 1. Spawn it with `[add FragmentOfInfinity`.
- **Tooltip:** beneath the name, *"Adds 10 points of enchantment to any artefact"* in hue `71e26c` (`ColorText3` / `ColorHue3`).
- **Use:** double-click it while it is in your backpack, then target an artefact you are wearing or carrying. The target gains **10 enchantment points** (`IGiftable.Points` for gift artefacts, `ILevelable.Points` for legendary leveling artefacts), its properties refresh, a magic sound plays, and the fragment is consumed.
- **Accepted targets:** anything implementing `IGiftable` — every `BaseGift*` artefact weapon, armour piece, shield, jewel and clothing item — **or** `ILevelable` — every `BaseLevel*` legendary leveling weapon, armour piece, shield, jewel and clothing item. Leveling items show the added total in the `ItemExperienceGump` line **"Spending Points(sp) Avail"**; their object property list does not display the point pool.
- **Refusals:** anything that cannot accept points answers **"This only works on artefacts."** and the fragment is **not** consumed. An artefact that is not worn or carried answers "You can only enchant artefacts you are wearing or carrying." and is likewise not consumed.
- **Drop chance:** a flat **10%** per kill, rolled once per corpse. The named carriers are `BlackGateDemon`, `BloodDemigod`, `DragonKing`, `OrkDemigod`, `LichKing`, `Dracula`, `Jormungandr`, `Xurtzar`, `CaddelliteDragon`, `Vulcrum`, `Arachnar`, `Surtaz`, `Mangar`, `Shadowlord`, `Tarjan`, `TitanHydros`, `TitanLithos`, `TitanPyros`, `TitanStratos`, `KhumashGor`, `Satan`, `SlasherOfVoid` and `Exodus`.
- **Champion spawn champions:** every champion boss (`Semidar`, `Mephitis`, `Rikktor`, `Lord Oaks`, `Barracoon`, `Neira` — all derive from `BaseChampion`) is also a carrier and drops at the same flat 10%.
- **Magical Prison bosses:** all **25** are covered via `EmoteHue == 505` (stamped by `SummonPrison` when it releases one), so ordinary `AncientWyrm`, `DaemonTemplate`, `Berserker`, etc. creatures do **not** drop fragments.
- **Corpse-less bosses:** `BlackGateDemon` and `Mangar` delete their corpses; their fragment roll goes into BlackGateDemon's `demonic chest` and Mangar's Vault loot chest respectively.

### Elite name tag ("(elite)")
- Every **named boss** (`BlackGateDemon`, `BloodDemigod`, `DragonKing`, `OrkDemigod`, `LichKing`, `Dracula`, `Jormungandr`, `Xurtzar`, `CaddelliteDragon`, `Vulcrum`, `Arachnar`, `Surtaz`, `Mangar`, `Shadowlord`, `Tarjan`, `TitanHydros`, `TitanLithos`, `TitanPyros`, `TitanStratos`, `KhumashGor`, `Satan`, `SlasherOfVoid`, `Exodus`), every **Magical Prison escapee** (`EmoteHue == 505`) and every **champion spawn champion** (`Semidar`, `Mephitis`, `Rikktor`, `Lord Oaks`, `Barracoon`, `Neira` — all `BaseChampion`) displays **"(elite)"** as the final part of its name in both the single-click label and the tooltip, e.g. **"Satan the devil lord (elite)"**, **"Tiamat the Lord of Dragons (elite)"** or **"Mephitis (elite)"**.
- `Dracula`, `Mangar` and `Shadowlord` hide their title on single click (`ClickTitle = false`), so their click label is just **"Dracula (elite)"** / **"Mangar (elite)"** / **"Nosfentor (elite)"** (the name Shadowlord picks at spawn), while the tooltip still shows the full title.
- `Exodus` has no title and simply reads **"Exodus (elite)"**.
- A paragon elite reads **"… (cursed) (elite)"** — the elite tag is always last.
- Only the **displayed** name is tagged; `Name`/`Title` stay unchanged, so logs, loot-chest owners, `DragonLamp`/`DragonPedStatue` names and other server-generated strings are unaffected.
- The registry lives in `MobileUtilities.IsElite` (`Source/Scripts/Utilities/MobileUtilities.cs`) and is shared with the Fragment of Infinity drop rule, so both always cover the same creatures.

### Chest of Satanic Relics (`Satan`)
- **Drop:** `Satan` rolls `GetPlayerInfo.LuckyKiller( luck ) && Utility.RandomMinMax( 1, 5 ) == 1` in `OnDeath` (the same 1-in-5 chance as LichKing's relic chest) and places a **Chest of Satanic Relics** in his corpse. The killer must be a player and becomes the chest's owner.
- **Chest:** `ManualOfItems` with hue `0x21` (red), one charge, 300 enchantment points, `m_Extra = "of Satan"` and `m_FromWho = "Taken from the Devil Lord"`.
- **Generated items:** red (`m_Hue = 0x21`), named **"<item> of Satan"**, and given 300 enchantment points (`IGiftable.Points`).
- **Weapon:** Fey Slayer plus **+20** of the weapon's own skill (`ManualOfItems.SKILL_WEAPON_OR_NECROMANCY` = `101`).
- **Everything else:** **+20 Necromancy** — armour, shields, clothing, jewellery and the candle/lantern/torch trinkets.

### The Lich King (`LichKing`) — melee attacker with necromancy
- **No more mage staff:** `IntelligentAction.BeforeMyBirth` (`Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Base/Behavior.cs`) gave every caster a `WizardStaff` (or "wizard gloves") and copied their Magery into Marksmanship, which made the Lich King fire from up to 10 tiles. A new `PackStaff` flag skips that whole block for `LichKing` only (`if ( from is LichKing ){ PackStaff = 0; }` in the undead branch), so he no longer receives the staff, the wizard gloves, the `SpellChanneling` prop or the Marksmanship skill.
- **Melee behaviour:** `OmniAI.m_PrefersRanged` (`Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Omni AI/OmniAI Core.cs`) now returns `false` for `LichKing`; without that his INT (1066-1145) is more than 100 points above his STR (316-405), so `MoveTo` would keep him parked 4 tiles away and he would never close to swing. He now walks into melee range and fights with his existing melee skills (Tactics 90.1-100.0, FistFighting 95.1-100.0, damage 29-35 — all unchanged).
- **Necromancy added:** `Source/Scripts/Mobiles/Undead/LichKing.cs` gains `SetSkill( SkillName.Necromancy, 120.1, 130.0 )` and `SetSkill( SkillName.Spiritualism, 120.1, 130.0 )`. Magery and Psychology stay at 120.1-130.0. `OmniAI` treats any creature with Necromancy above 10 as a necromancer, so his cast rotation now picks randomly between Magery and Necromancy (Animate Dead / necro familiar, curses, Blood Oath, damage spells, Lich/Vampiric Embrace form), and Spiritualism drives `TryToHeal` for him.
- **Verified:** full script compile (`Info/Scripts` + `Source/Scripts` against `World.exe`, `System.Net.Http.dll` included, `/d:x64 /d:Framework_2_0 /d:Framework_4_0`) → **0 errors, 11 warnings** (unchanged baseline). Pre-change files backed up as `Backups/LichKing.cs.2026-11-15-132447.bak`, `Backups/Behavior.cs.2026-11-15-132447.bak` and `Backups/OmniAI Core.cs.2026-11-15-132447.bak`.

### Worth tooltip (`VendorValue`)
- Every sellable item tooltip ends with `Worth: N gold` (a stack reads `Worth: N gold (X each)`), drawn with cliloc `1072172` in hue `E9C46A`.
- Price = `GenericSellInfo.GetSellPriceFor( item, barter )` on one global sell table built with `ItemInformation.GetBuysList( ..., force: true )`; `barter` is the viewer's Mercantile, capped at 100, so the line is up to 2× higher at 100 Mercantile than at 0.
- Matches a guild merchant's deterministic price; ordinary merchants roll 60–100 % of it at spawn. Cheap items floor at 1 gold because the tables halve the `ItemSalesInfo` price twice before barter.
- No line for types missing from the sell table, non-standard loot (blessed / cursed / insured / blessed-for), immovable items, non-empty containers, runebooks with entries, or items inside a locked container.
- Unidentified relics always show their worth: `IRelic` items use `CoinPrice` once identified and the original pre-identification estimate (`RandomMinMax( 5, 25 )`, before barter) while `NotIdentified` is set.
- Unidentified loot (`NotIdentified` wrapper) shows the **base item** worth: the sell-table price for the wrapped item's type × its material, halved once more like `GetSellPriceFor`, with rolled properties ignored so modifiers do not leak. Types outside the sell table fall back to the old 5–25 estimate.
- Unidentified equipment still shows its tier: the wrapper is ranked from the contained item, so both the coloured name (`1072171`) and the tier line are drawn in the tier hue while the header text stays the generic "unknown item".
- Built per player on every property query (the `0xD6` handler is re-registered in **both** the normal and 6017 tables, plus `0xBF/0x10`, by `VendorValue.Initialize()`), and only appended while the player's **Show Worth** preference is on; items without a value still use the cached shared `Item.PropertyList`.
- Players can hide the line with **Show Worth** (Help → Settings → MobileUO Settings) or `[VendorValues`; relic loot is exempt and always shows its worth. It is independent of the loot tier toggle. Toggling either display calls `VendorValue.RefreshTooltips`, which re-sends tooltips for the backpack, equipment and nearby items so the client updates immediately.
- Relics (`DDRelic*`, `HighSeasRelic`, `EmptyCanopicJar`) always show the line, even with **Show Worth** off: identified relics use their authored `CoinPrice` on the normal Mercantile curve, unidentified ones the 5–25 estimate, and relics bought from a vendor resell for 1 gold like any purchased item. Their old `ColorText3` "Worth N Gold" header is no longer printed (`Item.AddNameProperties` → `VendorValue.HideLegacyWorthText`, `1072173` skipped); the serialized text is untouched, so old saves behave the same.

### Dungeon Challenge Orb (`DungeonChallengeOrb`)
- A consumable orb (`0x122B`, default `BoostMinutes` 15, `ConsumeOnUse` true) used from the backpack while inside a dungeon.
- On use it resolves the outermost dungeon `BaseRegion` from the player's current region (walking parents), respawns every `SpawnEntry` on that region, then finds and resets `PremiumSpawner` and classic `Spawner` items whose region is the target or a child of it. Use is limited to `DungeonRegion`, `CaveRegion`, `BardDungeonRegion`, `DeadRegion`, `OutDoorBadRegion`, `DungeonHomeRegion` and `MazeRegion`. Spawners are snapshotted before respawn so world-item enumeration is not mutated mid-loop.
- Applies `DungeonChallengeBoost` +1 heat to the target region for `BoostMinutes`; `Difficult.GetDifficulty` adds the bonus after its normal lookup and caps the result at **5 (Deadly)**. Child regions inherit the boost through `Region.IsPartOf`; expired entries prune on query and a server restart clears all boosts.
- Broadcasts a short warning to players in the region, plays an effect, reports the restored spawn count and boost duration to the user, then deletes itself when `ConsumeOnUse` is true.
- GM helper: `[DungeonOrb` places one in your backpack.

### Equipment tier (`ItemRank`)
- Non-artefact equipment shows a coloured tier line in the same slot the artefact marker uses: Common `C7C7C7`, Magic `6C8CFF`, Rare `FFFF99`, Legendary `FF9C40`, Ancient `FF5A5A`, Primal `D46AFF`. Artefacts are the top tier **Artefact** `FFC000`; their existing marker replaces the tier line, so only the gold name/label shows the tier.
- The item **name** uses the same tier hue through cliloc `1072171`; material ("valorite …") and "exceptional" prefixes are preserved, engraved weapon text still follows the name, and items whose header is built from `ColorText1`/`ColorText2` (loot quivers) get the tier hue on that header line too. With **Show Loot Tiers off**, names are plain; quiver headers switch to the default white (`FFFFFF`) to match ordinary equipment, while other items keep their authored `ColorHue1/2`.
- Unidentified loot wrappers (`NotIdentified` containers) rank their contained equipment, so unidentified gear shows its tier line and a name in the tier hue; `NotIdentified.AddNameProperty` calls `ItemRank.TryAppendColoredName` explicitly because `Container.AddNameProperty` bypasses the normal hook.
- Artefacts (any `ArtifactLevel`, or `ArtifactRarity > 0`) are the top tier **Artefact** (`FFC000`): their existing marker replaces the tier line, so the tier shows through the gold name and ground label (`0x0B54`) instead of a duplicate "Artefact" line. Both follow **Show Loot Tiers** and revert to default when it is off. `NotIdentified` wrappers around an artefact have no marker and do print one Artefact tier line.
- Power = the shard's own weighted property budget: `ItemInformation.AddUpBenefits( item, 0, false, false ) - 1` (base item price excluded) `+ ( CraftResources.GetGold( Resource ) - 1 ) × 30` for material, `× 0.85` (low) / `× 1.15` (exceptional) for quality. Self repair is credited at most one point.
- Tier floors: Magic **80**, Rare **220**, Legendary **400**, Ancient **500**, Primal **700**. Artefact is not power-based (`GetFloor`/`GetCeiling` return `int.MaxValue`) and is assigned directly. Magic deliberately spans 80-219. Simulated (2,000 kills per pack/heat, luck 500): mixed monsters ~51/38/11/0.8/0.2/0.0 % at heat 0 and ~41/43/15/1.4/0.4/0.0 % at heat 4 (C/M/R/L/A/P); a heat-4 Ultra-rich boss runs ~6 % Legendary, ~3.5 % Ancient and ~0.2 % Primal per item.
- Computed while the property list is built. The cached shared list is always neutral; `VendorValue.SendPropertiesTo` builds a viewer-specific list for ranked items (and for items with a vendor value).
- Players can hide the tier line, the normal equipment name tint and the `ColorText1/2` quiver tint with **Show Loot Tiers** (Help → Settings → MobileUO Settings) or `[LootTiers`. Toggling calls `VendorValue.RefreshTooltips` so cached tooltips update immediately.

### Tier test commands (`LootTierCommands`)
- `[TierCommon`, `[TierMagic`, `[TierRare`, `[TierLegendary`, `[TierAncient`, `[TierPrimal` (GameMaster) each spawn one random equipment item built to that tier; `[TierAll` spawns one of each; `[TierItem <tier>` accepts the tier name.
- The builder picks a random weapon / armour-or-shield / clothing / hat / jewellery / instrument / quiver and rolls single random attributes until the item's power falls in the requested band; the band comes from `ItemRank.GetFloor` / `GetCeiling`, so future retunes carry over automatically.

### Cave of Virtue -- Angel / Archangel spawners, ankhs, candelabra and stone columns
- **Where:** data plus one script. `Info/Spawns/Spawns.map` (spawners), `Info/Decorations/Sosaria.cfg` (ankhs, candelabra, stone columns, entrance glow), `Saves/Items/Items.idx` (removal of the eight dropped columns) and `Source/Scripts/System/Regions/CaveRegion.cs` (lighting); nothing in `Info/Scripts/Settings.cs` is touched.
- **Location:** `the Cave of Virtue` is a `CaveRegion` on **Sosaria** (`Data/System/XML/Regions.xml`, rect `5160,1685` 116 x 80), so the spawn file map column is `2` and the items go in the Sosaria custom-decoration file.
- **Spawners:** seven `Angel` spawners at `5180,1739,0`, `5199,1740,0`, `5218,1744,0`, `5219,1727,0`, `5229,1711,0`, `5247,1710,0`, `5251,1721,0` and one `Archangel` spawner at `5258,1735,0`. Every one is: count `1`, Team `0` (the fixed `SpawnGenerator.Team` constant -- the map format has no team column), MinDelay **45 minutes**, MaxDelay **1 hour**, WalkingRange `1`, HomeRange `1`, SpawnID `1`.
- **File line shape:** `*|Angel||||||5180|1739|0|2|45|1h|1|1|1|1|0|0|0|0|0` = `creature | A | B | C | D | E | X | Y | Z | facet | MinTime | MaxTime | WalkingRange | HomeRange | SpawnID | Count | CountA..E`. Bare times are minutes; `1h` uses the hour suffix supported by `SpawnGenerator.PlaceNPC`.
- **Ankhs:** two non-bloodied `AnkhWest` shrines at `5248,1733,0` and `5250,1738,0`, written in the export format (`AnkhWest 3 (Bloodied=False)` then `X Y Z`, blank line between entries). They resurrect exactly like every other virtue shrine (walk onto one while dead, or double-click it).
- **Candelabra:** 21 lit `CandelabraStand` decorations, all at Z 0 and hue **2498**, written as one `CandelabraStand 12342 (Light=Circle225; Hue=2498)` header with one coordinate line per tile (the reader attaches every line up to the next blank line to that header). `CandelabraStand` is the tall floor candelabra (unlit `0x3035`, lit `0x3036` = `12342`, tiledata height 15). The loader ignites every `BaseLight` it places (`Burning = true`, art set to `LitItemID`, `Duration` zero so it never burns out) and sets `Protected = true`, so players cannot douse them.
- **Stone columns:** eight `Static 283` (art `0x11B`, hue 0) entries at `5233,1708`, `5234,1718`, `5255,1718`, `5248,1722`, `5216,1733`, `5190,1735`, `5222,1739` and `5191,1744` (all Z 0). They replace the eight movable `StoneColumn` objects that were lying on the cave floor; those eight item records were removed from `Saves/Items/Items.idx` (only the index was rewritten, `Items.bin` is untouched). `[buildworld` spawns them as immovable `Server.Static` items (`Movable = false`, `Weight = -2`), so they cannot be picked up, dragged or looted.
- **Lighting:** the Cave of Virtue is **always lit** -- `CaveRegion.AlterLightLevel` sets `global = LightCycle.DayLevel` (0, town brightness) for the region named `the Cave of Virtue`, so no night sight or light source is needed inside the cave. Every other cave keeps `LightCycle.CaveLevel` (26).
- **Entrance (outside):** the cave mouth on Sosaria is the `Teleporter` block at `4180-4181,267-268` (`PointDest=(5180,1760,0)`); two `AnkhNorth` shrines stand either side of it at `4178,269,0` and `4183,269,0`, each sharing its tile with a `GlowingLight` (`0x1647`, `Circle300`) so the ankhs glow, and one `Archangel` spawner (`4180,274,0`, same settings as the cave spawners) guards the approach. `GlowingLight` is used instead of `StrangeGlow` because `StrangeGlow` deletes itself after 30 seconds (and on world load) and cannot persist as a decoration.
- **Applying:** run `[buildworld` as an Administrator for the spawners, ankhs, candelabra, stone columns and glow lights. It deletes all `PremiumSpawner`s and every `Weight == -2` decoration item, rebuilds `Info/Decorations/*.cfg`, and then parses `Info/Spawns/Spawns.map` in the "Spawn Custom..." step, so every addition is recreated identically on each rebuild. The lighting change is a script, so it simply needs the shard restarted (scripts are recompiled on startup). The eight dropped columns are already gone from `Saves/Items/Items.idx`; do not restore an older `Saves/Items/*` copy unless you want them back.
- **Backups:** `Backups/Spawns.map.2026-11-21-194117.bak`, `Backups/Sosaria.cfg.2026-11-21-194117.bak` and `Backups/CaveRegion.cs.2026-11-21-195500.bak`, plus `Backups/Spawns.map.2026-11-21-202442.bak` and `Backups/Sosaria.cfg.2026-11-21-202442.bak`, plus `Backups/Sosaria.cfg.2026-11-21-220847.bak` (candelabra + glow, #216), plus `Backups/Items.bin.2026-11-21-224838.bak`, `Backups/Items.idx.2026-11-21-224838.bak` and `Backups/Items.tdb.2026-11-21-224838.bak` (pre-index-edit save, #217).

### Henchmen — equipment, restrictions and durability (#220)
- **Fighter skill:** `HenchmanFighter` trains Swords, Bludgeoning and now Fencing at the owner's highest primary skill (minimum 31).
- **Fixed stats:** Strength/Dexterity/Intelligence no longer come from the owner. Fighter and archer henchmen are **100 Str / 100 Dex / 50 Int**; mage henchmen are **100 Str / 50 Dex / 100 Int**. Creature henchmen use the same blocks by role (melee/archer vs mage). The `nStats`-based armour (`VirtualArmor`) is unchanged; damage now comes from the weapon and gear resistances act as a floor since #221 (see below).
- **Paperdoll editing:** the owner (`ControlMaster`) can drag gear onto/off a fighter, archer or wizard henchman's paperdoll within 2 tiles; GMs retain their normal access. Other players, out-of-range players and dead/ghost owners are refused.
- **Persistence:** `HenchmanItem` stores `HenchEquipment` and `HenchGearMade` (serialization v1). Gear is removed to a private internal-map stash before death, low-fame dismissal, normal release/dismissal (`Behavior.DoOrderRelease`) and every `Delete()`; it is restored onto the next summoned fighter/archer/wizard. A deed deletion only deletes items still orphaned in that stash.
- **Defaults:** default gear is movable, regular loot, `StrRequirement 10`, named `henchman's armor` / `henchman's weapon`. It is generated once per deed; old immovable+blessed defaults are migrated to the new rules when first stashed.
- **Fighter accepts:** shields, and weapons whose skill is Swords, Bludgeoning or Fencing. Harpoons (`IHarpoon`) and blowguns (`Fukiya`) are refused even though they are weapons. Otherwise: “Give me a proper melee weapon.”
- **Archer accepts:** shields, and real bows/crossbows (`BaseRanged` using Arrow/Bolt ammo); harpoons (`IHarpoon`, which fire `HarpoonRope`) and blowguns (`Fukiya`) are refused. Mage staves/sceptres that use marksmanship (`GiftStave`, `GiftSceptre`, `LevelStave`, `LevelSceptre`, `WizardStaff`) are refused with “Give me a bow or crossbow.”
- **Mage accepts:** hand items that allow equipped casting (`AllowEquipedCast`, covering spell channeling and magic lanterns/torches); otherwise “I can't cast spells while holding that.” Bows, crossbows (`IsBowOrCrossbow`), harpoons (`IHarpoon`), blowguns (`Fukiya`) and throwing gloves (`IThrowingGloves`: `ThrowingGloves`, `GiftThrowingGloves`, `LevelThrowingGloves`) are always refused with “I have no idea how to handle such a weapon.”, spell channelling or not. An accepted **weapon whose required skill is not Marksmanship** (e.g. a spell channelling sword or mace) is carried but never used, and the henchman says “I'll carry this, but don't expect me to fight hand-to-hand.” once the equip succeeds; staves (Marksmanship) never trigger it. Worn armour that would block passive meditation is still accepted, with “I'll wear this, but it will inhibit my meditation.”
- **Minimum class skills:** henchmen spawn with all of their class skills at least **31**, even if the owner's highest primary skill is lower. This also lets the default `WizardStaff` pass `BaseWizardStaff.CanEquip`'s Magery/Necromancy/Elementalism ≥ 30 check.
- **Strength feedback:** an item whose Strength requirement the henchman cannot meet is refused with a visible **“I'm not strong enough to use that.”** (covers weapons, armour and clothing; the core's own message would only go to the clientless henchman).
- **Restricted gear:** `Nontransferable` and avatar-only items are refused with “I cannot use that.”
- **Durability:** equipment worn or wielded by a fighter, archer, wizard or creature henchman never loses durability. `BaseWeapon.OnHit` skips its wear block for those four types (the random use wear **and** the guaranteed acid-blood scar from an acid elemental, slime, pudding, lava/acid puddle or xenomorph), and `ArmsLore.AvoidDurabilityHit` returns true for them, which covers armour, shields and clothing — including monster corrosion. The check is made against the holder at the moment of a hit, so gear starts wearing again as soon as a player takes it back. The old `<5%` / `<20%` “in danger of breaking” / “looking worn” warnings were removed with `HenchmanFunctions.CheckDurability`; see #229.
- **Creature henchmen:** gear remains non-editable/non-persistent (raw `Bow`/`WizardStaff`), though they also receive the minimum-31 class skills and the same no-wear rule.

### Henchmen — weapon damage and resistance rules (#221)
- **Weapon damage:** fighter, archer and mage henchmen derive their base damage from the equipped weapon, like a player. `HenchmanFighter`/`HenchmanArcher`/`HenchmanWizard` no longer call `SetDamage`, and their `DamageMin`/`DamageMax` overrides return the equipped weapon's own `MinDamage`/`MaxDamage` (so `BaseWeapon.GetBaseDamageRange`, the status bar and the Druidism "Base Damage" gump all agree, with no `-1 - -1`); the normal Strength/Anatomy/Tactics `ScaleDamageAOS` bonus still applies. An unarmed henchman uses fists (1–4), exactly like a player. Creature henchmen keep the nStats-derived `SetDamage` range.
- **Resistances are a floor:** the nStats-derived values (Physical = `nStats/2` and the other four = `nStats/4` for fighters; `nStats/3` / `nStats/6` for archers; `nStats/4` / `nStats/7` for mages) are no longer base resistances. They are stored per henchman and returned from `GetMinResistance`, so the core's clamp (the same mechanism as a player's Magic Resistance minimum) uses gear only when the gear total is higher: 40 nStats + 20 gear = **40**, 40 + 41 = **41**.
- **70 cap always:** `GetMaxResistance` returns **70**, and the clamp runs on every `ComputeResistances`, including the `UpdateResistances` triggered by equipping or removing an item with resistances — so gear added after summoning cannot exceed 70. `HenchmanFunctions.NormalizeArmor` is still called at summon but is now inert for these three classes.
- **Creature henchmen remain non-editable:** `HenchmanMonster` has no `AllowEquipFrom`/`CheckNonlocalLift` overrides, so the core refuses both equipping items (only self or a higher-level GM is allowed) and lifting their equipped `Bow`/`WizardStaff` by another player; dropping equipment on them is rejected by `HenchmanFunctions.OnGive`. Their nStats damage and resistances are unchanged.


### Henchmen — weapon damage types and enchantment-point ownership (#222)
- **Elemental attack split:** `HenchmanFighter`, `HenchmanArcher` and `HenchmanWizard` now take their attack's elemental split from the equipped weapon (AOS elemental attributes plus craft-resource element damage), exactly like a player. `BaseWeapon.GetDamageTypes` keeps the old creature branch only for creatures that are not those three types — creature henchmen and every other creature still use their `SetDamageType`/`*Damage` values. The human henchmen's `SetDamageType( 40 Physical / 10 each other )` calls remain but now only affect creature-information displays.
- **Enchantment-point ownership:** when an artefact is equipped to (or otherwise carried by) any henchman, only that henchman's `ControlMaster` may spend its `IGiftable`/`ILevelable` points; a GM with higher access is also allowed, matching the paperdoll rules. The gate is enforced in both context-menu entries (`GiftInfoEntry`, `LevelInfoEntry`) and in both gumps' `OnResponse` (so an already-open gump cannot be used), and the refusal reads **"You cannot spend the enchantment points on another person's henchman."** Items not on a henchman are unrestricted.


### Henchmen — equipment stats on the human henchmen (#223)
- **All AOS item properties now count for fighter/archer/wizard henchmen.** The ones that were already generic (attack/defend chance, damage increase, swing speed, spell damage, faster cast/recovery, lower mana/reagent cost, reflect physical, enhance potions, hit/stam/mana regeneration, skill bonuses, gear resistances, weapon leeches/hit spells/resist bonuses, `UseBestSkill`, `MageWeapon`, `MageArmor`/spell channelling, elemental damage) keep working because they are read from the mobile or the wielded item with no player check.
- **Hit Point/Stamina/Mana Increase:** the three henchman classes override `HitsMax`, `StamMax` and `ManaMax` and add the equipment `BonusHits`/`BonusStam`/`BonusMana` values to the seeded `BaseCreature` maxima (clamped 1..65000). A +25 Hits / +10 Stam / +5 Mana set adds exactly those amounts on top of the Str/Dex/Int offsets the core already applied.
- **Gear changes are live:** equipping or removing a piece clamps the current pools to the new maxima and re-arms the regeneration timers. A summoned henchman is set to full HP/Stam/Mana after its saved gear is restored.
- **Staff and forced-gear restore:** wizard staves and the `EquipItem` fallback now run the item's own `OnEquip` before being attached, so their stat mods (and `MageWeapon` magery modifier) are not lost on resummon. The mage-eye crystal message stays invisible because it is sent to the clientless henchman.
- **Creature henchmen and other NPCs:** `HenchmanMonster` and ordinary creatures keep their seeded nStats/`SetHits` values; only the three human henchman classes have the new overrides.




### Henchmen — appearance customization (#224)
- **Customize on the NPC:** Human henchmen (fighter/archer/wizard) show a literal **Customize** button under the henchman's name in the existing **Information** gump (single-click the NPC → **Information** → **Customize**); creature henchmen use `HenchmanCreatureInfoGump` and do not get the button. It opens a dark vendor-style panel listing **Name/Title**, Hair, Beard (male only), Gender and Skin Tone.
- **Live and persistent:** Changes apply immediately to the summoned henchman and are written back to its summoning deed (`HenchName`, `HenchTitle`, `HenchBody`, `HenchHue`, `HenchHair`, `HenchHairHue`, `HenchBeard`, `HenchBeardHue`), so the next summon keeps them.
- **Name/Title:** Up to **32** characters each (`HenchmanCustomizeGumpBase.NameTitleMaxLength`; the limit was 16, which cut the 28-character vendor titles such as *the Executioner of Minotaurs* short), HTML/control characters stripped; a blank title clears it, a blank name is rejected with "A henchman must have a name." Both entries sit at x=90 in a `480×240` panel and are 360px wide, so a full 32-character value stays visible while typing. The text-entry fields use `WhiteTextHue` (`2049`, the hues.mul "Fade 30"/"Fade 31" whites); `0x7FFF` is an AddHtml colour, not a valid text-entry hue, and rendered the typed text black.
- **Hair:** The vendor hair styles (Short, Long, Ponytail, Mohawk, Pageboy, Topknot, Curly, Receding, 2-Tails) plus Bald, and the 48 natural hair hues (`1102`–`1149`).
- **Beard:** Male only; the seven vendor beard styles plus None (the two mixed styles read "Short Beard & Mustache" and "Long Beard & Mustache" — a literal `&`, because the client does not decode `&amp;`); the same 48 natural hues.
- **Gender:** Switches body `400`/`401` and `Female`; switching to female clears the beard. Switching female→male is refused while any worn armour/clothing has `AllowMaleWearer == false`, with the message **"Remove gender-specific gear before customizing gender."** The paperdoll is re-opened after the switch (see **Paperdoll** below).
- **Paperdoll (#228):** `HenchmanCustomizeGumpBase.ShowPaperdoll` sends the core paperdoll packet (`DisplayPaperdoll` plus the henchman's item OPLs, the same pair `Server.Misc.Paperdoll` sends for a double-click). It is sent when customization starts (the **Customize** button in `HenchmanInfoGump`), after a name/title save (so the paperdoll's title caption follows a rename) and after every gender and skin-tone change — those changes make the client destroy and re-create the henchman (`ResendBodyToClients` for gender, the core's hue `sendRemove` for skin), which closes a non-player's paperdoll, so the re-send brings the window back with the new body/hue.
- **Gender refresh (#227):** after each switch the henchman is re-sent to the clients in range (`HenchmanCustomizeGumpBase.ResendBodyToClients` — remove packet + fresh `MobileIncoming`), because TazUO/ClassicUO only recompute their cached `Mobile.IsFemale` when they create a mobile and draw the `0x0190` paperdoll avatar from that flag: without the re-send the world sprite turned male while the paperdoll stayed female (and kept the female armour variants). The client closes an open paperdoll for the henchman while it processes the remove, so since #228 the paperdoll is re-opened straight afterwards (see **Paperdoll** above).
- **Gender source:** The summoned henchman's `Female` flag comes from the deed body (`401` → female, `400` → male) instead of the random Jako gender rolled in `BaseCreature`'s constructor, so male henchmen keep male sounds, the Beard menu and the male-only gear rules. See #225.
- **Skin Tone:** All 57 human skin hues (`1002`–`1058`); the paperdoll is re-opened after each swatch (the hue change closes it — see **Paperdoll** above).
- **Defaults:** New male deeds receive a random beard whose hue matches the hair hue; old deeds and female deeds get no beard (no retroactive backfill).
- **Closing:** The customization gumps re-validate on every response and are closed when the henchman is dismissed (including the release command), killed or otherwise deleted.
- **Removed system:** Dropping a henchman deed on a barkeep/tavern keeper no longer swaps it for a randomized replacement. The barkeep Talk text still describes the old exchange until it is updated separately.
- **Hue grids:** Hair/beard colors use 4 columns of 92px with 24px rows (starting at x=180 on the hair gump and x=300 on the beard gump); skin uses 6 columns of 92px with 24px rows starting at x=24. Every swatch is a 22px-wide `0xFA5` arrow button plus five asterisks 24px to its right (`AddColorSwatch`), which leaves 68px of clear label room per column and keeps the rightmost column inside the panel: hair panel `580×440`, beard `680×440`, skin `600×380`.
- **No client changes:** The trial cliloc `3011033` and the local TazUO client `Cliloc.enu` edit were reverted; the trigger uses literal gump text, so no client data changes are required.
